- **KNOWN LATENT WRINKLE (not fixed, pre-existing shape):** `evacuatingOutOfAmmo` prunes only on dead / despawned / owner-change, so a unit whose `RotateToEdge` is cancelled by something external (a player order, another trait) stays in the set and is invisible to the offense for the rest of the match. `HelicopterSquadBotModule.evacuating` has the identical shape (cleared only in `CleanUpHelicopters`, when the heli leaves the world), so this is the established precedent rather than a new hazard, and it was left alone deliberately in review. A real repair would key on the `evacuating` condition granted by the activity itself (`RotateToEdge.cs:145`) via `Actor.GetConditionCount` — the read the composition entry below already identified as the honest "is this unit evacuating?" signal, and which no bot-side caller uses yet.

## 2026-08-04 — Transport employment (branch `auto/transport-employ`): the "AI buys transport helis and they idle forever" bug was NOT a missing role case in the squad logic — the employment path exists and selects `Role == Transport` correctly. The blocker is a **budget asymmetry**: a gate that reads a shared counter the gated path never increments is a PERMANENT starve

> **[promoted (partial): → architecture.md §AI configuration, new heli-lift paragraph — *a precondition gate that reads a counter the gated path never increments starves that path forever, and reads as “the feature was never implemented”*; the fix is a reserved slice (`TransportMissionSlots`, `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1007`), not new behaviour. ALSO → architecture.md §Call-in bookkeeping for the corrected serialization model: `UnitLimits` counts world actors only (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:465-468`), `UnitDelays` is an absolute world-tick opening threshold (`:460-463`), and the real serializer is `BuildUnit`'s empty-queue precondition (`:440`, single-name overload `:664`) — all three re-verified, all three line refs drifted from this entry. NOT banked: the heli-twin `enable-ai-any` split recipe and the both-gate-sites rule (module-local implementation detail), and the env/test-count note.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

The wave was dispatched with a root cause of "the helicopter-squad role filter never employs or retires transport helicopters (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1280-1285`)". Re-verification in code found that **half of that is wrong**, and the wrong half is the more interesting one. Kept here rather than in `DOCS/reference/` pending curation:

- **A precondition gate that reads a counter the gated path never increments starves that path FOREVER — and reads as "the feature was never implemented".** `TryLaunchTransportMission` (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:890`) opens with `if (activeSquads.Count >= Info.MaxActiveSquads) return;` (`MaxActiveSquads = 3`), but a transport mission is tracked in `transportTasks` / `transportsAwaitingUnload` and **never adds to `activeSquads`**. So the attack loop can hold all three slots indefinitely while the counter never reflects a single transport mission — lift loses every race and never runs. The symptom (transports parked at the SR all match) is indistinguishable from "no squad role uses transports", which is exactly how the original diagnosis went wrong: a whole mature subsystem (`TransportLoadTask`, `AdvanceTransportTasks`, `EnsureTransportsUnload`, `TransportLoadMath`, `TransportDropSiteMath`, plus the already-fixed empty-delivery bug at `conventions.md:120`) was invisible because its entry gate never opened. **General shape: when a feature appears absent, check whether its entry precondition is coupled to a counter it does not participate in — the fix is a reserved slice, not new behaviour.** Sibling of the 2026-08-04 fires finding "a gate that is never re-entered is a gate that does not exist": here it is "a gate whose budget you never join is a gate that never opens".
- **The role filter at `:1283-1285` IS a real hole — but for RETIREMENT, not employment.** `EvaluateIdleHelicopters` admits only `AttackHeavy`/`AttackLight`, so `Transport` falls straight through and nothing ever retires one, even with `EvacuateWhenIdle` on. Employment and retirement are two different walks in this module, and only the retirement walk was role-blind to transports. When a report says "X is never used *or* cleaned up", price the two halves separately — they were in different methods here, with different causes.
- **"Who buys what" is split across FOUR `UnitBuilderBotModule` instances per faction, and misattributing the buyer is easy.** The ground twins (`ai-america.yaml:7/:54`, `ai-russia.yaml`) run `UnitQueues: Vehicle, Infantry` — they cannot buy aircraft at all. Aircraft come from the separate `@<faction>.fixedwing` and `@<faction>.heli` twins in `ai.yaml`, which carry `UnitQueues: Aircraft`. Transports are bought by the heli twins at a flat lottery weight (`tran: 15` / `halo: 15`, `UnitLimits: 2`, `UnitDelays: 2500`) with no demand test whatsoever. Corollary: `BuildUnit` hard-rejects any type absent from `UnitsToBuild` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:360-361`), so "is this type in *some* module's `UnitsToBuild`?" is the first question when tracing a purchase — and the answer may live in a different file from the one you are reading.
- **The heli builder twins were `enable-ai-any` (shared by @stable AND @experimental), so an @experimental-only flag could not simply be added to them.** `enable-ai-any` is granted to experimental+stable both, so gating required splitting each into a frozen twin guarded `enable-ai-any && !enable-ai-experimental` plus a new `@experimental.<faction>.heli` copy — the same twin idiom the ground production split already uses (`ai-america.yaml:7`, comment: "the `!enable-ai-experimental` guard keeps exactly one UnitBuilder enabled per player"). **Before adding an experimental flag to an existing module instance, check its condition: if it is `enable-ai-any`, you need a twin split, not a field.**
- **The composition lane's deferred heli piece resolves by gating at the POST-PICK sites, not by adding helis to `UnitTargetShares`.** `UnitBuilderBotModule` applies its eligibility gates in two places that must stay in lockstep: after the pick in `BuildUnit` (`:375-385`) and inside `IsCompositionCandidateEligible` (`:728-740`), the latter existing precisely so "a slot is eligible only if the buy would ACTUALLY go through". Putting the transport demand gate in both means it behaves identically whether the pick came from `ChooseByDeficit` or the legacy lottery — so helicopters keep their deliberate absence from `UnitTargetShares` (`ai-america.yaml:119`, `ai-russia.yaml:115`, `UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:87`: *"an unlisted buyable type is simply never chosen by it (that is how helicopter pools stay deferred)"*) and gain demand-gating without the two systems fighting. **Any new post-pick gate in this module must be added to BOTH sites or the composition path silently disagrees with the lottery path.**
- **CORRECTED by adversarial review — what actually serializes a repeat call-in in `UnitBuilderBotModule`, because the obvious two answers are both wrong.** The original rationale here claimed the per-type ceiling made a demand gate consistent with the existing checks, i.e. that `UnitLimits`/`UnitDelays` already prevent a double-buy. Both are false. (1) **`UnitLimits` counts WORLD ACTORS only** — `world.Actors.Count(a => a.Owner == player && a.Info.Name == name) >= limit` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:393-396`) — so a call-in still sitting in the production queue is INVISIBLE to it, and any cap derived from it inherits that blind spot. (2) **`UnitDelays` is an ABSOLUTE world-tick opening threshold, not a repeat cooldown**: `Info.UnitDelays[name] > world.WorldTick` (`:388-391`) opens once at that tick and never closes again. (3) The actual serializer is **`BuildUnit`'s empty-queue precondition** — `FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())` (`:368`) only picks a queue with nothing already queued — and since WW3MOD calls units in through the Supply Route's single `Aircraft` queue, that admits no second simultaneous call-in. Once `ProductionFromMapEdge` spawns the unit into the world, `UnitLimits` bounds the rest. The residual is **multi-SR** (a captured second SR = a second queue), and it too is bounded by `UnitLimits` at spawn. **Generalisation: in this module, "already in flight" is a QUEUE property, not a limits property — never reason about in-flight production from `UnitLimits`/`UnitDelays`.** (Consistent with the 2026-08-04 composition entry's finding that the selected queue is empty by construction.)
- **Env note:** build clean (0 warnings, 0 errors); NUnit **960 green, +20** over the 940 baseline (the new `TransportEmploymentMathTest` pins; raised to 965 by the post-review fix commit, which added 5 more). `make test` reports **442 errors — exactly the documented pre-existing baseline** — and none of the new traits/fields appear in the output. No autotest run: no transport-helicopter scenario exists (`tools/autotest/scenarios/` has `test-heli-squad-forms`, `test-heli-standoff`, `test-heli-vs-heli-missile`, `test-balance-heli-1v1`, none exercising lift), so the single permitted smoke run had no directly relevant target and was skipped rather than spent on an unrelated scenario.

## 2026-08-04 — Adversarial review of `41913bd8` (transport employment): a DECISION PROXY must apply every gate the ACTION'S EXECUTOR applies, or it reproduces the bug it was written to fix. The Employ verdict omitted `IsReadyForMission` and re-created River Zeta issue 4 in a new place

> **[promoted (partial): → architecture.md §AI configuration, heli-lift paragraph — *a DECISION PROXY must apply every gate the ACTION'S EXECUTOR applies, or it converts “cannot act” into “will act forever”*, with the concrete mechanism (`IsReadyForMission` rejects below `ReEngageHealthPercent`; TRAN/HALO ship 90 and WW3MOD has no AI repair host, so one chip of damage makes a transport permanently unpickable — and `ShouldBuyTransport` then refuses the replacement, so lift dies rather than degrades) and the fix shape (fold the executor's gate in as an ARGUMENT at the call site, `:1546`, keeping the pure math class world-free). NOT banked: the truth-table-exhaustiveness lesson, the accepted `dropZone` residual, and the honesty note that the pins bind the math and not the fold — review methodology tied to this commit pair.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Found by adversarial review, not by the build, not by NUnit, and not by the YAML lint — all three were green on the defective commit. Fixed in the follow-up commit on `auto/transport-employ`:

- **The pattern: a "should we act?" proxy that is a STRICT SUBSET of the executor's preconditions silently converts "cannot act" into "will act forever".** `EvaluateIdleTransport` computed its Employ/Hold/Evacuate inputs from lift demand + mission-slot occupancy only, but the executor `TryLaunchTransportMission` *additionally* requires `IsReadyForMission`, which rejects any transport below `ReEngageHealthPercent`. TRAN/HALO ship **90** (`aircraft-america.yaml` / `aircraft-russia.yaml` `AIHelicopterRole`; the C# fallback is 80) and **WW3MOD has no AI repair host**, so one chip of damage makes a transport permanently unpickable. The proxy nonetheless saw `hasDemand && slotFree` and returned **Employ** — and because Employ outranks Evacuate by design, the idle counter climbed past the 900-tick window forever and the airframe parked at the SR: **the exact symptom the wave exists to remove**. Worse, it compounded through the purchase gate: at `UnitLimits: 2` both transports end up counted owned+idle by `ShouldBuyTransport`, which then refuses replacements, so lift dies permanently rather than degrading.
- **The fix shape that keeps the pure class pure: fold the executor's gate into an ARGUMENT at the call site**, not into the math. `var launchable = IsReadyForMission(h);` then pass `hasDemand && launchable`. `TransportEmploymentMath` keeps its signature, stays world-free and NUnit-pinnable, and the invariant lives as a comment at the fold: *Employ must imply actually-launchable*. An unlaunchable transport now falls through to the evac window, retires for its refund, and the demand gate buys a healthy replacement — the degradation path the design intended.
- **Why the tests did not catch it:** the pinned truth table covered the rows the happy path exercises but left the `demand == false` × `slot == false` rows unpinned — and after the fix those rows are exactly the damaged-transport path. A truth table is only load-bearing if it is EXHAUSTIVE over its inputs; a partial table pins the cases you already believed in. The table is now complete (all demand × slot × window rows), and the cap-precedence pin was fixed too — it had passed `idleTransports: 0`, so it never actually tested cap-vs-transports-first ordering.
- **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL (deliberate, documented at the fold):** the launcher's `dropZone.HasValue` precondition is NOT folded into the proxy. It is transient in a live game (the drop-site picker recovers as the threat map fills), so treating it as unlaunchable would evac transports over a momentary gap. The risk it leaves is endgame-only (a permanently unresolvable drop site would hold rather than retire) and is judged smaller than the false-evac it would cause.
- **General rule worth carrying:** when you add a "should we do X?" evaluator beside an existing "do X" method, diff their preconditions explicitly. Any gate in the executor and absent from the evaluator is a latent permanent-stall — and it will present as the feature never working, not as a wrong decision.
- **Honesty note from the delta re-review of the fix (`fd3bc036`, verdict MERGE-READY): the pins bind the MATH, not the FOLD.** `TransportEmploymentMath` is unchanged by the fix, so all 25 pins would pass unchanged against the defective commit — nothing in the suite fails if a future edit removes `&& launchable` at the call site. The fold is *comment-covered* (the INVARIANT block at the fold), not *test-covered*; an integration pin would need a game harness. Do not read a green `TransportEmploymentMathTest` as coverage of the launchability fold. Same re-review also traced the remaining unfolded executor gates: `idleHelicopters` membership (≤100-tick transient, safe), `squadManagerRef == null` (unreachable in shipped profiles), and `dropZone.HasValue` — the last is safe **only because `RiskWeightedDropSite: true` makes the drop-site picker a weight, not a filter**; the frozen picker's `threat < 50` filter can stay closed indefinitely and would re-open the pin (third config coupling, now documented at the trait Desc + ai.yaml).

## 2026-08-04 — Fires Phases 2+3 (prep fires + suppression-coordinated advance) + Squad-Brain 1c leg (a) built default-off on `auto/brain-fires-batch`. The durable lessons are all from the adversarial review, which found FIVE mechanism defects that a green build, a green NUnit suite and a clean YAML lint all failed to catch: **a gate that is never re-entered is a gate that does not exist**, and **a pin proves nothing unless it is asserted at the value that actually ships**

> **[promoted (partial): → architecture.md §AI configuration fires paragraph, which **carried a verifiably WRONG claim this entry contradicted** — it said suppression-coordinated advance “remains unbuilt (no fires/offense module reads a target's suppression state)”. Both phases are BUILT: `PreparatoryFires` (default false, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:355`) and `SuppressionCoordinatedAdvance` (`:372`) with `ObservedSuppressionAt` at `:3599`; both ship OFF including in `@experimental` (`ai.yaml:487`/`:499`). Doc corrected. Banked with it: *a hold needs a DISTANCE bound, not just a time bound — prove the supporting effect can reach* (`PrepFiresReachCells`, `:3533`), and *count-normalise any threshold compared against a sum over observed actors*. The fog-legal suppression read (`Actor.GetConditionCount("suppressed")`, name kept as an Info field) → conventions.md §`GetConditionCount`. NOT banked: the score-quantization transform history, the pin-at-the-shipped-band lesson (test methodology), and the design-doc status-claim warning. The positional discipline “a `Committed` axis never re-enters `CommitAndOrder`” was already in influence-stack.md §P5 and is now extended there with the removing-a-gate-is-a-re-route corollary.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Built the two residual fires-doctrine gaps (`WORKSPACE/plans/260803_fires_cycle_design.md` §3 Phase 2/3 = gaps G2/G3) plus the missing leg of Squad-Brain Phase 1c. Findings, with the review's corrections folded in (the pre-review designs are recorded because *why* they were wrong is the transferable part):

- **A `Committed` axis never re-enters `CommitAndOrder`, so any gate living there fires exactly ONCE.** `PartitionHeldAxes` (`PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs`) pulls committed axes out of the eval entirely. The first design stamped the mission-commitment snapshot *above* the prep gate, so a prep-holding axis was frozen the moment it held: `PrepFireMaxTicks` became a dead knob (the real hold ran to `MissionCommitmentWindowTicks`, 400), and — much worse — the Phase-3 suppression read was evaluated once, at `elapsed == 0`, *before a single shell had landed*, leaving its only reachable effect "cancel the prep of an objective that was already suppressed": the exact inverse of its purpose. **Two levers, one root cause, and neither was visible in a unit test.** The fix is to hoist the snapshot into a local applied per exit path, so the prep-hold exit does not stamp commitment — the same positional discipline the retreat/damper/posture gates already use by returning above it. When adding a gate to this method, the question is not "is it in the right order" but "**what re-enters this method, and will my gate be reached again?**"
- **The prep gate must sit AFTER the fires peel-off, not before it.** Holding earlier (next to the retreat/damper/posture gates) would leave the artillery unordered — there would be no barrage to wait for. This is the *inverse* of the posture-hold constraint: posture-hold must return **early** so a held axis is never marked `Committed`; prep-hold must return **late** so the guns are already shooting — and therefore has to solve the commitment problem by not stamping rather than by placement.
- **A hold needs a distance bound, not just a time bound.** Prep-fires as first written held any approaching axis beyond `AssaultRadiusCells` — but under `EchelonPositioning` the guns sit BEHIND the screen, so their reach past it collapses to roughly (screen engagement range − `EchelonBuffer`) ≈ 21 cells, **not** the gun's own 35+. A fresh axis is typically assigned 40–100 cells out, i.e. the common case was a multi-hundred-tick stall with nothing landing on the objective. `ContinuousBombardment` does not cover the gap (it tasks *idle* pieces only). The bound is now derived from the same inputs `OrderFiresStandoff` uses (`EchelonMath.EchelonDepth` over the same screen-range max and fallback) so it tracks where the guns really go. General shape: **before holding a force to wait for a supporting effect, prove the supporting effect can actually reach.**
- **Score quantization for ALLOCATION SIZING needs a different transform from the rival compare — and TWO wrong answers were tried before the right one.** (1) `MissionCommitmentMath.QuantizeAxisScore`'s floor-to-band is right for a **ratio test with a margin**, but fed to `AllocateProportional` it zeroes every axis below one band and `Math.Max(1, score)` then hands the leader the whole leftover. (2) The replacement — band **index + 1** — was worse in a way that only shows up at the shipping value: at band 25 it was a **no-op** for its own headline near-tie case, it **amplified** near-ties where it did bite (a 0.06% score gap → an 11% allocation gap, because index arithmetic exaggerates small absolute differences), and the `+1` floor handed a 0.1% axis 16.7% of the leftover. (3) The shipped answer is **round to the nearest band multiple**: near-ties collapse to equal, separated scores stay within one band of their raw ratio, and a worthless axis snaps to 0 — starvation needs no special case because `AllocateProportional` already guarantees `minAxisSize`. **The `+1` was invented to solve a starvation problem that the allocator had already solved** — check what downstream already guarantees before adding a floor.
- **A damping pin asserted at a band the mod does not ship proves nothing.** Every original quantization pin used band 60 while the mod shipped 25; the transform was green in CI and inert in the game. The tests now carry a single `SweepBand` constant equal to the value a sweep should start from, every damping property is asserted at it, and the allocation-level assertions go through `AllocateProportional` rather than stopping at the transform. Same trap for any future banded/bucketed lever.
- **Count-normalise any threshold compared against a sum over observed actors.** `PrepSuppressionThreshold` was first compared to the SUM of suppression stacks across visible defenders, which makes the bar *easier the more defenders there are* — five lightly-rattled defenders would read "suppressed" while one pinned defender would not. Now averaged over the observed ARMED defenders (`AttackBase`, the same "holds ground" test the control field uses for presence), so the threshold sits on the same per-actor scale as the mechanic (`GarrisonManager` ducks a soldier at 30 stacks, recalls at 60) and means what it says.
- **Suppression is readable fog-legally with no new mechanic**: a stacking condition via `Actor.GetConditionCount("suppressed")` (the name is an Info field on `GarrisonManager`, mirrored on the offense module rather than hard-coded). Scan discipline copied from `RocketFireWorthy` — one bounded `FindActorsInCircle`, `RelationshipWith == Enemy && CanBeViewedByPlayer`, order-independent accumulation.
- **Squad-Brain Phase 1c/1d were ALREADY LANDED** at `f37442a3` (2026-08-02) though `260802_squad_brain_design.md` §5 still said "REMAINING" (now corrected). Only 1c leg **(a)** was genuinely missing — the landed commit says so explicitly ("No other score consumer changed"). **Re-verify a design doc's status claims with `git log -S <symbol>` before building from them.**
- **All three levers ship OFF in `@experimental`** (manager decision): `@experimental`'s top live regression is axes that hold and never close (see the 2026-08-03 entry below — ~85-92% of the army standing off on one SR-Pressure axis), so no new HOLD discipline goes live until a user-gated sweep prices it. Correct-but-dark is the deliberate state.
- **Env note:** `make test` DOES run on this machine (contra the 2026-08-03 Phase-1 note) but the repo carries a large pre-existing error baseline (442: box conditions, map cordons, a `VehicleCrew` field), so verification is scoped to "no reported error mentions the touched trait or its new fields" — meaningful because the validator *does* flag unknown trait fields. Build (0 errors) + NUnit (919 green, +17 over the 902 baseline) are the primary gates. No autotest/game run (not needed, and gated).

## 2026-08-04 — Composition-directed purchasing (branch `auto/composition`): `Actor.GetConditionCount(name)` is the per-actor granted-condition read the bot layer had no precedent for, and the UnitBuilder "pending credit" cannot be scoped to the queue it is buying on

> **[promoted (partial): → conventions.md §Engine behaviors that surprise — `Actor.GetConditionCount(name)` is a public, cache-backed read that works for conditions NOTHING consumes, because `GrantCondition` (`Actor.cs:674`) writes `conditionCache[condition]` (`:660`) before the consumer fan-out; verified exactly as cited, including the `evacuating`-from-an-activity case (`RotateToEdge.cs:145`). ALSO → architecture.md §Call-in bookkeeping: a pending-production credit CANNOT be scoped to the selected queue, which is provably empty by construction — it must span the module's other queues plus its own request lists (`RequestedProductionCount`, `:429`). NOT banked: the counter-matrix flatten guard (module-local) and the `IgnoreGroundUnits`/`idleUnitCount` framing correction (in-flight `@experimental` state).]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Shipped `ForceCompositionMath` + `UnitBuilderBotModule.CompositionDirected` (default-off; `@experimental` YAML only). Three non-obvious things found while wiring it, kept here rather than in `DOCS/reference/` pending curation:

- **`Actor.GetConditionCount(string)` (`engine/OpenRA.Game/Actor.cs:636`) is a PUBLIC, cache-backed read of a granted condition BY NAME, and it works for conditions granted from C# with no consumer.** `GrantCondition` (`:674`) unconditionally routes through `UpdateConditionState`, which writes `conditionCache[condition] = tokens.Count` (`:660`) — so the cache is populated even when nothing on the actor declares a `RequiresCondition` for that token. This matters because `evacuating` is granted from an ACTIVITY (`RotateToEdge.cs:145`, `self.GrantCondition("evacuating")`) and is only declared as a bare `ExternalCondition` on the unit templates (`vehicles.yaml:31`, `infantry.yaml:24`, `aircraft.yaml:15`). Every existing bot-side "is this unit evacuating?" test instead keys on an ammo proxy — `IsOutOfAmmo(a)` behind `SkipOutOfAmmoUnits` (`PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:1712/:1756`, `LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:387`) — which is a *cause* of evacuation, not the state itself, and so misses evacuations triggered by `Sellable`/`DeliversCash`/heli mission-exit (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1357`). `GetConditionCount("evacuating") > 0` reads the state directly and needs no per-actor activity walk.
- **A `UnitBuilderBotModule` pending-production credit CANNOT be scoped to "this queue" — that queue is empty by construction.** `BuildUnit` selects its queue with `FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:343`), so by the time any post-pick logic runs, `queue.AllQueued()` is provably empty and a credit read from it is always zero. Any "don't re-buy what is already in flight" logic must span the module's OTHER queues (`AIUtils.FindQueuesByCategory(player)` over `Info.UnitQueues`) plus the module's own `priorityBuildRequests`/`queuedBuildRequests` lists. Without this the 30-tick purchase cycle re-picks the same deficit type every cycle and overshoots it badly before the first reinforcement ever walks in from the map edge (map-edge spawning means delivery latency >> purchase cadence).
- **FRAMING CORRECTION — `CompositionDirected` ROUTES AROUND the idle-count defect, it does not repair it.** The root cause remains live: `SquadManagerBotModule.FindNewUnits` (`:329`) never reports ground units as idle-base units while `IgnoreGroundUnits` is set on both `@experimental` SquadManager twins, so `idleUnitCount` stays 0, `buildRandom` is permanently true, and the legacy weighted picker `ChooseUnitToBuild` is **dead code on this profile**. The composition path simply takes the decision away from that branch. Two consequences worth remembering: (1) the weighted `UnitsToBuild` shares are still inert as priorities on `@experimental` (they act only as an eligibility whitelist), and (2) the no-slot-eligible fallback in `ChooseByDeficit` still lands on the uniform `ChooseRandomUnitToBuild` lottery — deliberately, to preserve purchase volume, but it means the drift behaviour is reachable again in the degenerate case where nothing is eligible. A real repair would be to fix the idle-unit accounting (or drop `IgnoreGroundUnits`), which is a SquadManager-side change and out of this lane's scope.
- **A counter-matrix keyed by ROLE must be expanded to TYPE columns at flatten time, or the bias silently self-disables.** The natural YAML shape is `"enemyclass>ownrole"`, but the vector the bias multiplies is the per-TYPE target-share array. `ForceCompositionMath.ApplyCounterBias` guards on `matrixPct.GetLength(1) == baseTargets.Length` and treats a mismatch as "no bias" (an inert identity pass) — which is the safe failure but an INVISIBLE one. Caught in review, not by a test: the module now expands `roleMatrix[class, role]` into `counterMatrix[class, typeSlot]` once in `Created` via the per-type role index. Worth a pin if more role-keyed config lands.

## 2026-08-04 — `ITick` traits KEEP FIRING for an actor removed from the world but not disposed — `IsInWorld` is not a tick gate (branch `auto/supply-hunt`)

> **[promoted: → conventions.md §Engine behaviors that surprise, in full — every sub-claim re-verified against code, line drift only. `World.Tick`'s actor walk drives ACTIVITIES (`World.cs:499-500`); `ITick` traits go through `ApplyToActorsWithTraitTimed<ITick>` (`:502`) → `TraitDictionary.ApplyToAllTimed` (`TraitDictionary.cs:305-316`), a bare indexed walk with no `IsInWorld`/`Disposed` filter; `World.Remove` (`:398-405`) never touches the trait container; the actor leaves it only in `Actor.Dispose`'s frame-end task (`Actor.cs:469`); the reachable case is `Activities/PickupUnit.cs:174` (`World.Remove` with no `Dispose`). One refinement banked from verification: `SupplyProvider`'s guard is now in code at `:186` and is a *release-then-return* (`ReleaseTargetOnExit(); return;`), not a bare return — and it is specific to `ITick.Tick`; the same file deliberately leaves other paths unguarded so a condition revoke still runs while out of world.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Caught by adversarial review after I asserted the opposite in a code comment, so it is worth writing down precisely — the wrong model is the intuitive one.

- **Actors and traits are ticked from two different places.** `World.Tick` iterates `actors.Values` calling `a.Tick()` (`World.cs:496-497`) — that drives **activities** only. `ITick` **traits** are driven separately on the next line by `ApplyToActorsWithTraitTimed<ITick>` → `TraitDictionary.ApplyToAllTimed` (`TraitDictionary.cs:305-316`), a plain indexed walk of the trait container with **no `IsInWorld` and no `Disposed` filter**.
- **`World.Remove` does not touch the trait container.** It sets `IsInWorld = false`, drops the actor from the `actors` dict, and fires `INotifyRemovedFromWorld` (`World.cs:395-403`). An actor leaves the trait container only via `World.TraitDict.RemoveActor(this)` inside `Actor.Dispose`'s frame-end task (`Actor.cs:469`). **So removal stops activities but not `ITick` traits.**
- **Removed-but-not-disposed is a real, reachable state, not a theoretical one.** A Carryall pickup calls `cargo.World.Remove(cargo)` with no `Dispose` (`PickupUnit.cs:174`); loading into cargo is the same shape. The actor comes back later via `World.Add`. Throughout the carry, its `ITick` traits run and its `CenterPosition` stays frozen at the pickup point.
- **Consequence for any proximity/aura trait:** it keeps operating from a stale position while inside the carrier. Concretely here, `SupplyProvider` went on scanning from where the truck was picked up — pushing ammo to units near that spot from inside the transport, and re-granting the `replenish-soldiers` external condition one tick after `INotifyRemovedFromWorld` had revoked it. The notification revokes; only an explicit `if (!self.IsInWorld) return;` at the top of `Tick` actually stops the trait. Any world-position-dependent `ITick` trait wants that guard.
- **Corollary for cleanup design:** `INotifyRemovedFromWorld` is the right place to *release* state, but it is not a guarantee that the trait has stopped running. For permanent teardown the terminal pair is `INotifyKilled` + `INotifyActorDisposing`; `RemovedFromWorld` additionally covers the non-terminal removals, which are exactly the ones that resume.

## 2026-08-04 — Infantry auto-seek-supplies: the traps were `INotifyIdle` cadence, `QueueChild`'s append semantics, and an autotest that would have passed itself (branch `auto/supply-hunt`)

> **[promoted (partial): → conventions.md §Engine behaviors that surprise — `INotifyIdle.TickIdle` fires EVERY tick the actor stays idle (`Actor.cs:285-303`), `INotifyBecomingIdle` is the edge-triggered one, and the throttle phase must come from `self.ActorID % Interval` rather than a `SharedRandom` draw; and `Activity.QueueChild` APPENDS to the child chain (`Activity.cs:220-226`) while `Cancel` only sets `Canceling` for a child already past `Queued` (`:209`, a never-run child is marked `Done`), so a cancel-then-queue runs the stale leg unless you drain first. Both verified. NOT banked: the two test-design halves — prove the autotest RED by disabling the flag rather than by reasoning, and strip `UnitDefaultsManager` for a stance-gated human-owned run — the first is AUTOTEST-recipe material and the second is already conventions.md §`UnitDefaultsManager`.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Built the unit-level supply run (idle + low ammo → walk to a truck → top up → walk back), stance-gated, shipped OFF. Four things worth keeping:

- **`INotifyIdle.TickIdle` fires EVERY tick an actor stays idle, not once on going idle.** `Actor.Tick` calls `becomingIdles` once on the `!wasIdle && IsIdle` edge, then `tickIdles` on every subsequent tick while still idle (`Actor.cs:290-302`). So an idle-driven trait that scans the world is a per-tick world scan unless it throttles itself — the exact thing "no per-tick scans" is meant to avoid. `INotifyBecomingIdle` is the edge-triggered one (that is what `AmmoPool` uses). Throttled here with a counter whose **phase comes from `self.ActorID % ScanInterval`**, not `World.SharedRandom`: this trait loads for every profile, so a synced draw would shift the stream for control games too (conventions.md's SharedRandom rule).
- **`Activity.QueueChild` APPENDS to the child chain; it does not replace.** `QueueChild` does `if (childActivity != null) childActivity.Queue(activity); else childActivity = activity;` (`Activity.cs:220-226`). A multi-leg activity that cancels its child and immediately queues the next leg therefore runs the **stale, cancelled** leg first — and `Cancel` only sets `State = Canceling` (`:209`), so a child that is never ticked again sits in the slot indefinitely. The fix is to drain: after cancelling, tick the child until `ChildActivity == null` *before* queuing the replacement. Same hazard for any state-machine activity, not just this one.
- **A "does the new behaviour work" autotest can pass on pre-existing machinery — check by disabling the flag, not by reasoning.** `AmmoPool.AutoRearmIfAllEmpty` (`AmmoPool.cs:170`) already walks a unit to a resupplier via `SeekSupplyProvider`, but only when **every** pool is empty (`ammoPools.All(a => !a.HasAmmo)`). A test that drains an `e3` to zero would have been satisfied by that path with the new trait switched off. Draining to a **partial** 10/100 with the RPG left loaded keeps the legacy path inert, so only the new behaviour can move the unit. Verified RED by flipping the scenario's `Enabled` to false — fails at baseline 10; true — passes.
- **A stance-gated, human-owned autotest MUST strip `UnitDefaultsManager`.** Already in conventions.md as a general rule; this is the first case where it is load-bearing for the *assertion* rather than for combat behaviour — the run is gated on Fire/Engagement/Resupply, and a dev whose `unit-defaults.yaml` once set riflemen to Ambush or Resupply-Hold would see a silent, machine-specific failure.

## 2026-08-04 — `SupplyProvider`'s aura Range gated SELECTION only, never DELIVERY — the arithmetic was fine, the gate was missing (branch `auto/supply-hunt`)

> **[promoted (partial), REPHRASED PAST-TENSE: → economy.md §What rearms what — the aura `Range` gated selection but never delivery, and the general lesson that a proximity aura must re-check range at the moment of EFFECT because the wait between selection and delivery is where the geometry changes. The fix is in current code and the entry's line refs are all stale: `ResupplyTarget` is `:638`, the delivery gate `:664-675`, `InAuraRange` `:927-930` (squared, matching `WorldUtils.cs:84`, avoiding the floor-`ISqrt` at `Exts.cs:306`); the pre-fix `HorizontalLength > Info.Range.Length` forms no longer exist. **The entry's site list is WRONG:** the four `InAuraRange` sites are `:470` (IsValidTarget), `:516` (SetTarget), `:552` (SyncTargetCondition — omitted from the entry) and `:666` (delivery); the `FindActorsInCircle` call at `:372` does NOT use it, since a circle query takes no predicate and inherits `WorldUtils`'s own identical filter. The Hunt-branch half is banked with the Tier-2 entry above.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Audited every distance comparison in `SupplyProvider.cs` looking for a wrong-quantity range defect. Worth recording because the obvious suspicion was wrong and the real defect is a *missing* comparison, which greps find far less easily:

- **All three `Info.Range` comparisons were dimensionally correct** — `FindActorsInCircle(self.CenterPosition, Info.Range)` (`:314`), `IsValidTarget`'s `HorizontalLength > Info.Range.Length` (`:412`), and `SetTarget`'s identical form (`:461`). No `Length`/`LengthSquared` mixing, no cell-vs-WDist confusion. Hunting for bad arithmetic here finds nothing.
- **The real defect: `ResupplyTarget()` (`:497`) called `GiveAmmo` with no range check at all.** The aura is a proximity push, but `Range` was enforced only while *picking* a target. Two ways an out-of-aura actor reached delivery: (1) the Hunt branch (`:239-248`) calls `FindNeedsResupplyTarget` (`:298`), an unbounded map-wide `ActorsHavingTrait<AmmoPool>()` scan with **no range bound and no leash** — `SetTarget` then grants the `RearmCondition`, queues a `MoveTo`, and `Tick` fires `ResupplyTarget` after `RearmDelay` (6 ticks on TRUK) *while still driving*, so the truck rearms a soldier clean across the map; (2) any selected target that walks out of the 5c0 aura during the `RearmDelay` wait (a ≤7-tick window, since `ScanInterval` is 7).
- **The Hunt branch is latent, not dead.** TRUK does carry `AutoTarget` (`Inherits@AutoTarget: ^AutoTarget`, `vehicles.yaml`), and while the default engagement stance is `Defensive` (`AutoTarget.cs:160/163`), `Hunt` is player- and AI-settable — and `UnitDefaultsManager` persists a human's per-type stance choice across games. So this file's earlier "supply trucks are not Hunt-stance, so this does not arise in practice" holds by default but is not an invariant.
- **Fix:** extracted the predicate to `SupplyProvider.InAuraRange(WPos, WPos, WDist)` — a squared horizontal comparison matching `WorldUtils.FindActorsInCircle`'s own `HorizontalLengthSquared <= r.LengthSquared` filter (`WorldUtils.cs:84`) rather than the floor()'d `WVec.HorizontalLength` (`Exts.ISqrt` defaults to `ISqrtRoundMode.Floor`, `Exts.cs:306`), so selection and delivery agree exactly on the boundary. Applied at all three sites plus the new delivery gate. The gate **keeps** the target and re-arms `rearmTicks` rather than dropping it, so an approaching Hunt-stance provider still serves on arrival instead of thrashing its target pick. Sheltered garrison passengers stay exempt — they are `!IsInWorld` with a stale `CenterPosition`, and their building was in range when picked. Pinned by `SupplyAuraRangeTest`.

## 2026-08-03 — DIAGNOSIS: the "green mass pools mid-map" regression (post posture-fix `7baa3885`, River Zeta @experimental-won game) is DOMINATED by SR-Pressure ×260 single-axis funneling into a standoff axis that never closes — NOT the retreat damper; the damper/posture rear-muster is a real but secondary pool. Static-analysis + log mine (debug.log tick 27→31180, binary `OpenRA.Mods.Common.dll` built 09:59:29 = 15s after merge, fix present).

> **[promoted (partial): the durable CAUSAL mechanism → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging, new Phase-7 bullet — the size allocator is min-fund-then-remainder-by-score (`PoiOffenseMath.AllocateProportional`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:2773-2791`), so any large score multiplier collects the bulk of the army onto ONE axis; with `SrPressureScoreMultiplier: 260` (`RescaleSrPressure`, `:1133-1140`) that axis is an enemy-SR Pressure axis which holds at standoff and never resolves. Banked together with the fix this diagnosis motivated (`LateralSpreadMath.Rebalance` SR share cap + opportunity-weighted redistribution, `ForwardMuster` per-axis muster anchor `ResolveMusterAnchor` `:1619-1632`) and the "SrPressureScoreMultiplier stays parked behind an A/B" decision. NOT banked: the per-game log numbers (92-of-~100 units, tick ranges, `clumpRadius` growth, `rally=13,45`) are one-match measurement → WORKSPACE; the "free=0 always ⇒ forward staging inert" refutation is already influence-stack.md's own §P2 correction; the damper/posture rear-muster secondary pool is superseded — its cause was the posture target-sector bug (entry at the foot of this file) and its symptom is fixed by ForwardMuster. **STALE as written:** "There is NO offense-side lateral-spread mechanism" was true at diagnosis time and is FALSE at HEAD — Phase 7 landed it (`LateralSpreadEnabled`/`ForwardMusterEnabled` ON, `rules/ai/ai.yaml:461/463`). Verified against HEAD.]** (curation 2026-08-04).

Log source: `%APPDATA%/OpenRA/Logs/debug.log` (Aug 3 22:54, one `Experimental AI` vs `Stable AI 0802` on River Zeta WW3). Ranked verdict on why most of the army pools mid-map west of the river:

- **PRIMARY — single-axis funneling via `SrPressureScoreMultiplier: 260` into a STANDOFF axis that never closes (hyp C confirmed; it is a DESIGN GAP, not a bug).** `RescaleSrPressure` (`PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:1097-1104`) multiplies the enemy-SR **Pressure** axis score ×2.6; `AllocateProportional` (`:2668`) funds each axis at `min` size then hands the ENTIRE remainder proportional to score → the SR axis absorbs the bulk. Quantified: end-game (tick 30945-31145) **92 of ~100-108 units (~85-92%) on the single held `supplyroute@79,34` Pressure axis**; the other 8-16 are the free pool. It is a Pressure/`FiresStandoff` (`:243`) axis — IndirectFire members hold at weapon-range standoff, MainBattle members route to a Stage-E danger-nav `via`-waypoint — so across the whole 25-min game its `[exp-offense] order … distToTarget` **never drops below ~26** (target `79,34` is across the river; own force at X≈53-69, near bank), `clumpRadius` grows to 44-49. The dominant block therefore stands off mid-map on the west bank instead of closing/spreading. **There is NO offense-side lateral-spread mechanism:** P5 man-the-line (`ManTheLineEnabled`) is wired to `LayeredDefenceBotModule`'s DEFENSIVE reserve budget, not the offense pool; the offense module only uses `WeakestPointBias` as a per-axis ×150 multiplier (`:449-450`), never a "one axis per manned frontier sector" spread. So "cover the whole front" is unimplemented in the offense allocator.

- **SECONDARY — damper + posture-hold REAR muster (hyp A confirmed, smaller).** Zero `[exp-retreat] state` lines all game ⇒ **no axis ever entered a genuine `Retreating` state**, so all 34 `[exp-retreat] fallback` events were the damper-hold `(b)` (`NearRally && OwnStrength < MinAdvanceStrength(1200)`, `RetreatDamperMath.ShouldHold:90`; the dwell path `(a)` never armed because it requires a `Retreating→Engaged` transition) OR posture-hold — both `OrderRetreat(stagingAnchor ?? rallyCell)` (`:1707/:1721`). The muster cell resolves REARWARD (e.g. `rally=13,45`, deep west of the X=74-79 targets), so a small axis (~9-10 units) is pulled back each eval → a second, more-rearward pool. Real, but ~10 units vs the ~90-unit SR blob.

- **REFUTED — B (forward-staging single-anchor of the FREE pool):** `free=0` on EVERY reeval ⇒ `AllocateProportional` drains the free pool completely each eval, so `ForwardStaging` of the free pool is inert (matches influence-stack.md:124's own correction). Staging appears only as the fall-back DESTINATION (mechanism A); the one large `[exp-staging] idle=16` event was at tick 30945 (very end). **REFUTED — D (man-the-line absorbing offense units):** man-the-line governs the defense reserve, a different unit set.

- **Minimal next changes (NOT implemented):** (1) *needs-own-design* — an offense-side lateral-spread allocator distributing axes ACROSS frontier sectors, reusing the existing `FrontlineProfileMath`/`FrontlineAllocationMath.AllocateAcrossAvenues` (already built + consumed by defense) on the offense pool; this is the real "spread along the front" fix. (2) *one-line, A/B-gated* — lower `SrPressureScoreMultiplier` 260→~130-150 (or stop applying the full ×2.6 to the remainder); 260 alone funnels ~90% onto the SR standoff axis, but it interacts with SR-denial doctrine so it needs measurement, not a blind change. (3) *small tweak* — pull the damper/posture fall-back muster forward (tighten `StagingStandoffCells:6`, or make `stagingAnchor` track the axis's own frontier sector instead of one module-wide anchor) so held units wait AT the contact, not the rear. Env: no build/test run (Windows, possible DLL lock; user may be playing) — verdict is static-analysis + logs only.

## 2026-08-03 — Fires Phase 1 (gap G1) continuous bombardment shipped default-off (`ContinuousBombardMath` + `PoiOffensiveBotModule.BombardStaticPositions`, branch `auto/fires-p1`); the load-bearing subtleties are the ledger-invisibility of committed pieces, the fog-legal target WPos, and the lone-static clump identity

> **[promoted (partial): the shipped employment + its two generalizable disciplines → architecture.md §AI fires paragraph — continuous bombardment is now BUILT and ON (`ContinuousBombardment`, `rules/ai/ai.yaml:377`; `BombardStaticPositions` `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:2242`, ordered before `StageFreePool` `:1113`/`:1118`), which also retires that paragraph's stale "two doctrine employments remain unbuilt" claim (one left: suppression-coordinated advance). Banked as durable: (1) a ledger-committed piece is invisible to `BuildFreePool()` (`:1567`) ⇒ a standing mission must be carried forward from module state and explicitly RELEASED when not re-tasked, or it strands committed; (2) the aim point is the BELIEVED cell centre (`:2308`/`:2365`), never a `world.Actors` lookup — fog-legal and NRE-safe; plus the lone-static clump identity ⇒ rocket-holds/tube-fires, pinned by `ContinuousBombardMathTest.Select_RocketHolds_TubeFires_OnLoneCheapStatic`. NOT banked: the per-profile single-flag gating bullet is already influence-stack.md §Invariants pattern 1 (already covered), and the `make test`/.NET-6 env note is harness material. Verified against HEAD.]** (curation 2026-08-04).

Built the standing fire-mission behaviour the fires-cycle design (`WORKSPACE/plans/260803_fires_cycle_design.md` §3 Phase 1) flagged as absent: idle `IndirectFire` pieces with ammo shell believed-static enemy positions from standoff, independent of any offensive axis. Non-obvious findings pinned:

- **A ledger-committed bombard piece is INVISIBLE to `BuildFreePool()`** (`PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs`) — the free pool excludes anything `goalGuard.Ledger.IsCommitted`. So a piece already on a standing mission cannot be re-discovered by re-scanning the free pool next eval; it must be **carried forward explicitly** from a module-side tracking map (`bombardAssigned`). The pass therefore unions {carried-in committed pieces, re-guarded for alive/ours/ammo/weapon} ∪ {newly-idle artillery from the free pool}, runs the pure decision, then RELEASES any carried-in piece not re-tasked this eval (out of range / cap / hysteresis with no candidate) so it can evac or rejoin the offense. Same shape as the fires-EV `ReconcileFiresHoldFire` stranding guard.
- **The believed-static target's WPos is `world.Map.CenterOfCell(contact.Cell)`, NEVER a live-actor lookup.** The belief store keeps `IsStatic` contacts under fog (decay-exempt), so the structure may be currently fogged or already gone; resolving its position via `world.Actors`/`FindActor` would be both omniscient AND a candidate for the positionless-`PlayerActor`/gone-actor NRE class. Reading the believed cell centre keeps the aim point fog-legal and NRE-safe. The only `world.Actors` scan in the pass is `BuildFreePool`, which filters `IPositionableInfo`+`AttackBaseInfo` before any `.Location` read (existing guard).
- **A lone believed-static's rocket CLUMP value equals its own build value** (self at dist 0 → splash weight 100 → `ProjectedClumpValue == Value`), because the clump is built over ALL believed statics with `FiresEconMath.ProjectedClumpValue` (weight 0 beyond `FiresEvClumpRadius`). Consequence, and the tube/rocket split in action: a lone cheap static never repays an expensive rocket salvo (`FireWorthy(value, rocketSalvoCost, margin)` fails ⇒ rocket holds) but a cheap tube salvo does (`FireWorthy(value, tubeSalvoCost, margin)` passes ⇒ tube shells the single). Pinned in `ContinuousBombardMathTest.Select_RocketHolds_TubeFires_OnLoneCheapStatic`. Static structures carry no fog-legal HP, so the clump uses `damagePercent = 100` (a "worth firing at this position" heuristic, not a ballistic model).
- **Placement matters: the bombard pass runs BEFORE `StageFreePool` and commits its pieces to the ledger**, so forward-staging's own `BuildFreePool` scan then excludes bombarding artillery — a piece that can shell a known defence line does so instead of mustering forward empty-handed.
- **Gating is the per-profile single-flag pattern** (influence-stack.md §Invariants pattern 1): `PoiOffensiveBotModule@experimental` is already `RequiresCondition: enable-ai-experimental` and `@stable` is a separate instance, so a lone default-off `ContinuousBombardment` set only in the `@experimental` ai.yaml block is sufficient — no `enable-ai-any` BotType gate needed (unlike `GarrisonBotModule@defenses`). Verified byte-identical-off per call site: the belief-store resolve adds `|| Info.ContinuousBombardment` (unchanged when false), the stale-anchor prune is `Count>0`-skipped (map always empty when off), and `BombardStaticPositions` early-returns before any `BuildFreePool`/RNG/order when the flag is off.
- **Env note:** `make test` (`--check-yaml`) was NOT runnable here — no .NET 6 runtime (only 8/9) and the ww3mod manifest mounts the base `ra` mod content (a full engine distribution) absent from the checkout; built `OpenRA.Utility`+`OpenRA.Mods.Cnc` and it loaded ww3mod's own assemblies but died mounting `ra`. Build (0 errors) + NUnit (890 green, +17 new) stand as verification.

## 2026-08-03 — The fires/artillery doctrine cycle is already SHIPPED + ENABLED in `@experimental` (standoff + echelon + frontier + EV gate + cluster targeting); the "artillery charges like MBTs" premise is stale — the residual gaps are bombardment / prep-fires / suppression-feedback / measurement (fires-cycle design recon)

> **[promoted (partial): the two genuinely-missing durable slivers → architecture.md §AI fires paragraph — `FiresEvGate` (rocket-only ammo-EV gate: `RocketFireWorthy` over `FiresEconMath.SalvoCost`/`ProjectedClumpValue`, tube pieces exempt) and the SHARED-`AutoTarget` AoE cluster targeting (`ClusterTargetingCondition: enable-ai-experimental`, defaults.yaml:395,413). The rest is NOT banked: standoff/echelon/role-split are already in architecture.md:321/364, frontier-hold in influence-stack.md:113, and the two-missing-employments + unmeasured-A/B is plan/tracker material tied to `WORKSPACE/plans/260803_fires_cycle_design.md` (deep-linked from architecture.md). Verified `ai.yaml:341/348/356`, `FiresEconMath.cs:90/:121`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:1987`, `AutoTarget.cs:225-231`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

A recon+design tasking for the fires/artillery behaviour cycle assumed a greenfield design ("today `ai.yaml` lumps artillery/SHORAD with tanks, they charge like MBTs"). That premise is **stale** — the core doctrine is built, committed, and on. Pinned so it isn't re-scoped as new work:

- **Shipped + enabled in `@experimental` (all default-off on the class, YAML-on only under `@experimental`):** `UseUnitRoles` role split incl. tube-vs-rocket `IndirectFireKind` (`UnitRoleResolver.cs:50-60/:308-314/:364`); `FiresStandoff` weapon-range standoff (`FiresStandoffMath.cs`; consumed `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:1684-1715/:1819`; commit `6a33813d`); `EchelonPositioning` hold-behind-screen (`EchelonMath.cs`; `:1874-1875`; commit `c43dc391`); `MinFrontierDistanceCells:4` hold-behind-frontier (`PushEchelonBehindFrontier`, `:1922`); `FiresEvGate` rocket-only ammo-EV gate (`FiresEconMath.SalvoCost/ProjectedClumpValue/FireWorthy`; `RocketFireWorthy` `:1987-2061`; commit `b25ba2f0`); AoE cluster targeting in the SHARED autotargeter (`AutoTarget.cs:1141-1198`, `ClusterTargetingCondition: enable-ai-experimental` at `defaults.yaml:395,413`). ai.yaml flags at `:282/:335-367`.
- **Two doctrine employments genuinely MISSING (not merely off):** (1) **continuous bombardment of static positions** — arty only fires as a peeled member of an active offensive axis; no standing fire-mission shells believed-static enemy positions from standoff. The fog-legal target set already exists unused: belief store keeps `IsStatic` structure/defence contacts decay-exempt (`BeliefStore.cs:273-278`) + `ControlField` believed-enemy site anchors (`ControlField.cs:493-501`). (2) **preparatory fires + suppression-coordinated advance** — the screen and guns commit together on geometry/timer; **no fires/offense bot module reads a target's suppression state** (verified: the only bot-side `Suppress*` readers are `GarrisonManager` for its own soldiers, plus support-power/base-builder — none in the fires path), so "advance under our own barrage" only *emerges* from AutoTarget FireAtWill at standoff, it is never coordinated.
- **The whole cycle is UNMEASURED:** every fires flag is on, yet the item-25 re-baseline shows `@experimental` underperforming `@stable` with everything enabled (influence-stack.md §Known gaps). A fires-on vs fires-off isolation A/B has never been run — a prerequisite before stacking more fires behaviour. Design + gap phases: `WORKSPACE/plans/260803_fires_cycle_design.md`.

## 2026-08-03 — VERDICT (re-investigation of the LocalRandom-seed determinism question): the fix already landed — option (a)/(b) "seed LocalRandom from RandomSeed via a decorrelated LCG" is committed on `main`; re-implementing would be a no-op and re-doing it as (b')="move bot decisions into SharedRandom" would be the WRONG fix

> **[promoted: the sync-semantics verdict → architecture.md §Bot decisions ARE seed-reproducible — "do not route bot decisions through `SharedRandom`": `LocalRandom` is excluded from `World.SyncHash()` (folds only Actors + `ISync` + synced effects + `SharedRandom.Last`), bots emit orders not RNG draws, and moving draws into `SharedRandom` would shift every combat roll and break the frozen `@stable` A/B baseline recorded on the seeded-`LocalRandom` build `2d3c8fe0`. The seeding FACT itself was already promoted there. Verified `World.cs:217/:225-228/:283-286/:540-554`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

A tasking asked to decide the right fix for "same-seed bot games diverge because `World.cs:214` leaves `LocalRandom` unseeded" (citing the 2026-07-19 finding). That premise is **stale**: the fix shipped ~2 weeks ago and the cited line numbers (213/214) predate it. No new code was written; this entry is the verdict + the sync-semantics reasoning so the question is not re-opened a third time.

- **Current state (verified, `main` @ 18ff375b):** `World.cs:217` seeds `SharedRandom = new MersenneTwister(RandomSeed)`; `World.cs:225-228` seeds `LocalRandom = new MersenneTwister(DeriveLocalSeed(localSeed))` when `localSeed != 0`, else falls back to the wall-clock ctor. `DeriveLocalSeed` (`World.cs:283-286`) is the canonical PCG64 LCG `x*6364136223846793005 + 1442695040888963407` truncated to int32. Landed at `2d3c8fe0` (2026-07-20, verdict_version 5) and refined at `d71dd48b` (2026-07-24). Pinned by `engine/OpenRA.Test/OpenRA.Game/SeedDerivationTest.cs` (constants + truncation + golden vector `DeriveLocalSeed(1017)==894286612` + no-fixed-point + injectivity + stream-divergence). Already promoted to `architecture.md` "Bot decisions ARE seed-reproducible".
- **Sync-semantics argument (why (a) is safe and (b) is not):** (1) `LocalRandom` is excluded from the sync hash — `World.SyncHash()` (`World.cs:540-554`) folds only Actors and `ISync` fields, and the RNG contribution elsewhere is `SharedRandom.Last` only — so its seed value never participates in desync detection and reseeding it cannot change multiplayer sync. (2) Bots tick client-side (`ModularBot.cs`) and emit choices as **orders** through the order pipeline; orders — not the RNG that produced them — are what synchronize peers and replays, so a differently-seeded `LocalRandom` changes which orders a bot issues but never breaks lockstep. (3) The two MersenneTwisters are independent, so seeding `LocalRandom` does not touch the `SharedRandom` stream (combat rolls, projectile inaccuracy, damage) at all — @stable/Normal/legacy/human sim behavior over the synced stream is byte-identical. (4) Option (b) (route bot decisions through `SharedRandom`) is the WRONG fix precisely because inserting bot draws into the shared stream would shift every subsequent combat roll and break the frozen @stable A/B baseline's RNG-stream identity — the exact invariant a prior influence-stack defect already tripped. (5) The decorrelating transform (vs. reusing the raw seed) keeps decision rolls statistically independent of combat rolls while staying a pure function of the lobby seed, and the `!= 0` guard preserves wall-clock nondeterminism for normal launches so real games are not accidentally same-seeded.
- **@stable byte-identity is intact, not at risk:** the frozen A/B benchmark baseline was recorded ON the seeded-`LocalRandom` build (`2d3c8fe0` is described as "the first paired-comparison" baseline), so the seeding is already baked into the baseline — there is nothing to shift. The bot modules that still draw from `LocalRandom` are the stock/legacy squad + base-building modules (`SquadManagerBotModule`, `UnitBuilderBotModule`, `BaseBuilderQueueManager`, `McvManagerBotModule`, `CaptureManager`/`CaptureCoordinator`, `PoiOffensive`/`PoiGarrison` reeval countdowns, `HelicopterSquadBotModule`, `MinelayerBotModule`, `SupportPowerBotModule`, `HarvesterBotModule`, `Squad.cs:62` aliases `World.LocalRandom`), and they DO run for @stable — but seeded, so per-seed reproduction holds and the baseline is unperturbed.
- **@experimental influence stack stays zero-RNG:** the belief/danger/territory/frontline traits (`BeliefStore`, `DangerFieldLayer`, `ControlField`, `SightingThreatLayer`, `EngineerRouteOpenBotModule`, `LaneAmbushBotModule`, `StancePositioningExecutor`) draw **zero** `SharedRandom`/`LocalRandom` by design (SharedRandom only staggers a first tick in a couple); this fix does not add any RNG to that path and does not change it.
- **VERDICT: HOLD — no code.** The unambiguous-safe option is already in `main`; a worktree/build/test cycle would produce an empty diff. Delivered as written verdict only, per the "if implementing changes nothing you cannot rule out, deliver the verdict" clause. (Did not build, did not run tests, did not run any game/autotest.)

## 2026-08-03 — The heli transport drop-site picker is the ONE transport drop that uses the omniscient `ThreatMap.FindWeakestEnemyCell` (not the fog-legal MountedTransport path) — root cause of "drops land behind the enemy SR" (air-polish, `auto/air-polish`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers beyond D/E/F — new "Heli transport drop-site reshaping" consumer (`RiskWeightedDropSite`): the heli drop was the one transport drop still committing to the raw omniscient `ThreatMap.FindWeakestEnemyCell`, now reshaped fog-legally by `TransportDropSiteMath.ScoreDrop` over believed control depth + danger + own-SR distance (weight not filter, candidate-source omniscience precedented); plus the `FlightPathHysteresis` order-cadence damper. Verified `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:263/:246/:944/:1022`, `ThreatMapManager.cs:232`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Polishing the bot air/transport subsystem surfaced a drop-site asymmetry worth pinning:

- **`HelicopterSquadBotModule.TryLaunchTransportMission` picked its drop zone as `threatMap.FindWeakestEnemyCell(player)`** (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:~886`, pre-change), gated only by `GetThreat(weakCell) < 50`. `FindWeakestEnemyCell` (`ThreatMapManager.cs:232-273`) scans EVERY grid cell for enemy presence and returns the one with the **lowest total enemy Cost** — so a lone cheap enemy unit sitting deep behind the enemy Supply Route reads as the globally "weakest" cell and is selected, landing the infantry drop somewhere unreachable and lethal. It is a fully OMNISCIENT scan (`world.FindActorsInCircle` with a relationship filter, no fog check).
- **This is the opposite discipline from `MountedTransportBotModule`**, whose drop candidate comes from the omniscient `GetFrontline`/`GetFriendlyInfluence` but is then **post-adjusted fog-legally** by `ApplyStandoff` off the believed danger field (see influence-stack.md §consumers beyond D/E/F). The heli path had no such fog-legal reshaping — it committed to the raw omniscient pick.
- **Fix (experimental, default-off `RiskWeightedDropSite`):** rank a candidate set (the weak cell + top `PoiMap.GetOffensiveTargets` POIs) by `TransportDropSiteMath.ScoreDrop`, a fog-legal penalty over believed-enemy control DEPTH (`ControlField.ScoreAt` — negative = enemy; the enemy-SR anchor floors a radius-4 disc to ≈ −800, a strong "behind the enemy SR" signal), believed danger (`DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger`/`AirDanger`) and Chebyshev distance from our own SR (reachability). A WEIGHT not a filter (nothing hard-banned). The omniscient weak cell stays a *candidate* (candidate-source omniscience is precedented); only the reshaping term is belief-side, so the fog-legality merge-block is satisfied.
- **Both attack-heli order-issue sites that churn under @experimental are in the FSM states, not the module:** the standoff `AttackMove` re-issue (`HelicopterApproachState`) and the withdraw `Move` re-issue every tick for 75 ticks (`HelicopterWithdrawState`). The withdraw loop is the loudest — it re-issued a Move each tick to a per-tick-recomputed retreat cell (esp. the `RandomBuildingLocation` fallback jitters). Flight-path hysteresis (`HeliPathHysteresis.ShouldRetarget`, default-off `FlightPathHysteresis`) damps both by holding the committed leg until the recomputed cell shifts ≥ threshold (or the leg completes). Order-cadence only — the destination is still chosen by the standoff/danger-nav/frontier logic, so the first-contact AA gate, strategic pin, and evacuation are structurally untouched (they live on other code paths).

## 2026-08-03 — Engineer bridge-repair with `EnterBehaviour.Dispose` consumes the engineer AT HUT-ENTRY, while the bridge stays `Dead` through the repair animation — a route-open mission must gate "repair in progress" (`hut.Repairing`) BEFORE its engineer-died-en-route failure check, or every success is misrecorded as a failure (frontline-influence Phase 6, `auto/frontline-p6`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging (new Phase-6 bullet: `EngineerRouteOpenBotModule` as a ledger-committed consumer — avenue.Cell == hut.Location resolution, identify-engineer-by-trait, `bridge-repair`/`bridge-screen` ledger keys) + Consumers table P6 row; the `EnterBehaviour.Dispose` engineer-consumed-at-hut-entry lifecycle gotcha (bridge stays Dead while `hut.Repairing`; gate repair-in-progress BEFORE the failure check) → conventions.md §Engine behaviors that surprise. Verified `RepairsBridges.cs:31/:66-72/:92-118`, `LegacyBridgeHut.cs:32`, `BridgeHut.cs:240` (both `Repairing` public), `CrossingMap.cs:744-745`, `ControlField.cs:829`, `EngineerRouteOpenBotModule.cs`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Wiring `EngineerRouteOpenBotModule` (dispatch an `e6` to a repairable destroyed flank bridge when the P4 profile says that sector is the weakest enemy sector) surfaced the load-bearing lifecycle facts:

- **The `RepairsBridges` engineer (`e6`) uses `EnterBehaviour.Dispose` (`RepairsBridges.cs:31`), so the engineer actor is REMOVED when it enters the hut — before the bridge finishes repairing.** During the repair the hut's `BridgeDamageState` is still `Dead` but `LegacyBridgeHut.Repairing`/`BridgeHut.Repairing` is true (`LegacyBridgeHut.cs:32`, `BridgeHut.cs:240`, both public). A mission tick that reads "bridge still Dead + engineer gone ⇒ failure" therefore fires a FALSE failure (with cooldown + a wasted retry) on a mission that is actually succeeding. The fix: check `IsBridgeRepairing(hut)` and hold (keep the screen committed, wait for the bridge to flip to not-Dead ⇒ success) BEFORE the engineer-alive and timeout gates. Ordering is the whole safety property, mirroring the P5 posture-hold-after-retreat-gate discipline.
- **A repairable avenue's `Cell` IS the bridge hut's `Location`.** `CrossingMap.RevalidateCrossings` records a repairable `GroundCrossing` with `CellX/CellY = hut.Location` (`CrossingMap.cs:744-745`), and `ControlField.AddAvenue` carries that straight into `FrontlineAvenue.Cell` (`ControlField.cs:829`). So the consumer resolves the hut actor by exact `a.Location == avenue.Cell` over `world.Actors` filtered on `LegacyBridgeHutInfo`/`BridgeHutInfo` — no separate hut→avenue map needed.
- **Identify the engineer by TRAIT, not name list.** `actor.Info.HasTraitInfo<RepairsBridgesInfo>()` is the robust "this unit can open a bridge" test (survives faction/name variants `e6.america`/`e6.russia`); the `e6` is already in the bot's build list (`ai-america.yaml`/`ai-russia.yaml`, weight 20 / limit 2) and excluded from every other bot module, so nothing competes for it and NO purchasing hook is needed — the bot already owns engineers.
- **The RepairBridge order is `new Order("RepairBridge", engineer, Target.FromActor(hut), false)`** (`RepairsBridges.cs:66-72/:92-118`, `Target.Type == Actor`); `bot.QueueOrder` resolves it on the engineer, which queues the `RepairBridge` activity (walk-to-hut + enter + repair). No custom activity needed.

## 2026-08-03 — Phase-5 posture-hold must ride the retreat FSM's fall-back path, not become a new order writer — the safety property "never blocks a genuine retreat" is STRUCTURAL only if it is placed AFTER the retreat gate that already returned (frontline-influence Phase 5, `auto/frontline-p5`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging (new Phase-5 bullet: man-the-line `AllocateAcrossAvenues` coverage-then-largest-remainder, weakest-point ×100 bias, sector posture-hold; the posture-hold-safety-is-POSITIONAL discipline — three `CommitAndOrder` fall-back gates in order retreat→damper→posture-hold, each RETURNS before the mission-commitment snapshot; `SectorOfTargetCell` shared column-bucket helper; `AvenuesForSector`-allocates-fresh-List ⇒ read `Avenues` once per scan) + Consumers table P5 row. Verified `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:1244/:1622/:1639/:1653/:1671`, `FrontlineAllocationMath.cs:56`, `ControlField.cs:977-979`, `LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:539/:585`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Wiring man-the-line + weakest-point + posture-hold (`FrontlineAllocationMath` + `LayeredDefenceBotModule`/`PoiOffensiveBotModule`) surfaced two integration constraints worth pinning:

- **Sector posture-hold is a HOLD trigger, and its "never blocks a losing retreat" guarantee is positional, not conditional.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule.CommitAndOrder` runs three gates in order: (1) genuine retreat (`CombatRetreatMath.ShouldRetreat`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:~1528`) — RETURNS for a `Retreating` axis; (2) retreat-oscillation damper (`DamperShouldHold`); (3) NEW posture-hold (`PostureShouldHold`). Because (1) already returned for any genuinely-losing axis, placing (3) *after* it means posture-hold can only ever fire on a NON-retreating axis — the safety property is guaranteed by ordering, with no re-check needed. Posture-hold reuses the SAME `OrderRetreat(stagingAnchor ?? rallyCell)` grouped fall-back the retreat/damper use, so it composes with the FSM (one fall-back order stream) instead of fighting it; it must also run BEFORE the mission-commitment snapshot + RETURN, or a held axis would be marked `Committed` and `PartitionHeldAxes` would freeze it holding forever (same discipline the retreat/damper gates already follow).
- **A target's frontier sector is a pure column-bucket of its map-cell X, reusable across both consumers.** `FrontlineProfileMath.SectorOfMapCellX(cell.X, ControlField.Info.CellSize, ControlField.GridWidth, ControlField.FrontlineSectorCount)` maps any objective cell to the same sector index the P4 profile is keyed on — so the weakest-point bias (does this target sit in the thin sector?) and posture-hold (is this target's sector overmatched?) share one helper (`SectorOfTargetCell`) and never need the ControlField's internal grid. Fog-legal (map geometry only), zero RNG.
- **`ControlField.AvenuesForSector` allocates a fresh `List` per call (P4 adversarial note); the man-the-line consumer therefore reads `ControlField.Avenues` ONCE per scan and buckets per-avenue in a single pass (`TryBuildAvenueData`), never calling `AvenuesForSector` per-unit.** `Avenues` is map-static + deterministically ordered, so the allocation (`FrontlineAllocationMath.AllocateAcrossAvenues`: coverage-first, largest-remainder surplus on the outnumbered avenues) is reproducible without touching the per-call allocation.

## 2026-08-03 — A Phase-4 sensor gated on the ControlField world trait would NOT be byte-identical: @stable now PARTICIPATES in the influence stack, so the profile flag must be a per-@experimental-player opt-in, not a world-trait/world.yaml global (frontline-influence Phase 4, `auto/frontline-p4`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging (Phases 0–4) + §Participation — the `@stable`-now-PARTICIPATES correction (fixed the stale "excluded" claim) + the per-player-opt-in gating recipe for a new read on a shared world layer; CrossingCells vs repairable Crossings avenue union. Verified `InfluenceStack.cs:47-48`, `ControlField.cs:280/:500/:579/:933`, `CrossingMap.cs:427/:453`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Building the frontline strength profile (`FrontlineProfileMath` + `ControlField` extension, `ControlField.cs`) surfaced the load-bearing gating subtlety for anything hung off the always-on world layers:

- **`InfluenceStack.Participates` returns true for @stable too** (`InfluenceStack.cs:48`, since the 2026-08-02 @stable→@experimental parity promotion). So @stable DOES get a `PlayerControl` and DOES run `RecomputePlayer`. A naïve "gate the new work by a flag on `ControlFieldInfo` set in `world.yaml`" would therefore build the profile for @stable (and for humans, who also participate for the overlay) — breaking the "byte-identical for @stable / normal / human" invariant. The `MinFrontierDistanceCells`/`FrontierDistance` precedent is *not* a counter-example: that array is built for ALL participants unconditionally, and byte-identity there only holds because @stable/human building the same deterministic array doesn't change a *non-participant* game — but a NEW per-participant array is extra state inside the @stable sim itself.
- **The fix: a per-player opt-in.** `ControlField.RequestFrontlineProfile(player)` adds the player to a `HashSet<Player>`; `RecomputePlayer` builds the profile only for members. The master flag lives on `PoiOffensiveBotModuleInfo.FrontlineProfileEnabled` (default false — the `ForwardStagingEnabled` pattern, set true only in the `@experimental` ai.yaml block, no @stable twin ⇒ C# false inherits), and the @experimental module calls `RequestFrontlineProfile` once when resolving its ControlField ref. @stable/normal/human never set the flag ⇒ never opt in ⇒ `profileEnabled` empty for them ⇒ zero profile work, byte-identical. This is the general recipe for ANY future per-@experimental-only read on a shared world layer that @stable now shares.
- **`CrossingMap` exposed crossing-cell COUNT but not the cells, and repairable (destroyed-bridge) crossings are NOT in `crossingCells`** — destroyed-bridge cells are water, so the geometric span detector never adds them; they live in `Crossings` (`GroundCrossing` with the hut's `CellX/CellY`). Avenue mapping therefore unions two sources: `CrossingMap.CrossingCells` (intact spans, newly exposed in `CrossingMap.cs`) tagged `Intact`, plus `CrossingMap.Crossings[].Cell` tagged `Repairable`. Both are map-static geometry, so the crossing→sector association is player-independent and refreshed at most once per tick.

## 2026-08-03 — Through-crossing DISTANCE (not binary reachability) is the lever that de-concentrates River-Zeta axes; barrier detection must use a NON-fording graph, and `tracked` is split by MOUNTAINS not the river (frontline-influence Phase 1.5, `auto/frontline-p01`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging (through-crossing distance provider + water-terrain barrier detection + locomotor water table). Verified `PoiReachability.cs:71`, `CrossingMap.cs:336/:459`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:768-778`, `world.yaml` TRACKED/LIGHTTRACKED/FOOT. NIT confirmed: the shipped barrier keys on water TERRAIN TYPE, not foot-impassability.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Wired `PoiReachabilityMath.ThroughCrossingDistanceCells` into `PoiMap.GetOffensiveTargets` distance (gated `Func<CPos,int?>` provider, null ⇒ exact Euclidean ⇒ byte-identical off) and validated with the one granted `test-frontline-reachability` rerun (verdict pass). The `[crossingmap]`/`[exp-reach]`/`[exp-reach-dist]` logs gave three concrete facts:

- **`tracked` is split into 3 ground components on River Zeta by MOUNTAINS, not the river.** `[crossingmap] built ground=3 infantry=1 amphibious=1`. `Locomotor@TRACKED` (`world.yaml`) has NO `Rock` terrain speed → `Rock` (mountain) is impassable for tracked, so mountain ranges disconnect the tracked graph; `foot` has `Rock: 50` and fords the river band, so the infantry graph is a single connected component. So neither the (fording) `tracked` NOR the (mountain-crossing) `foot` graph cleanly represents "the river as a barrier" — a component-count of the army locomotor is the WRONG signal for river-crossing distance.
- **Barrier detection must key on a NON-fording reference, and it works.** `CrossesGroundBarrier` walks the straight SR→POI line and flags any cell impassable for the non-fording `foot` locomotor (deep river/water; `foot` has no `River`/`Water` speed). `crossingCells=10` (foot-passable land cells spanning foot-impassable water on opposite sides, clustered) and `barrierCrossed=7`/11 POIs — the geometry detector found the real fords and the far-bank POIs. `foot` being connected overall does NOT stop per-line barrier detection (foot routes AROUND the water; a straight line still crosses it).
- **The lever materially de-concentrates the axis distribution.** Far-bank POIs' crow-flies distance is replaced by the through-crossing detour: e.g. `oilb@56,26` crow-flies ≈46 from SR@14,45 → `dist=65` (`score` 6.44M), `oilb@76,35` → `dist=64` (6.44M), while same-bank `oilb@19,76`/`@15,3`/`@25,22` keep crow-flies (`dist=30/42`, `score` 30M/24M/33M) — near-bank POIs now outrank the far-bank cluster by ~4–5×. The USA-bot's actual axes over the run were `supplyroute@80,35` (enemy-SR Pressure, top score) + near-bank west `oilb@17,44`/`@25,22` — i.e. secure the own bank + pressure the enemy SR, NOT lunge across the river at the far-bank central-crossing targets that raw crow-flies made look adjacent. (Caveat: no in-run flag-off A/B — the evidence is the inflated `dist`/reduced `score` on `crossesBarrier=True` POIs vs the unchanged same-bank ones, plus the resulting near-bank axis set.)
- **The reachability FACTOR stays inert here (`reach=Same` for all POIs)** because SR + all POIs sit in the same `tracked` component (the river is fordable, the mountains don't separate them), so the DISTANCE lever is the sole active mechanism — confirming the earlier finding that River Zeta's central-focus is a distance-metric artefact, not hard unreachability.

Kept `GroundLocomotor: tracked` default (component classification / reach factor); the barrier/distance path deliberately uses the `foot` (infantry) graph for the "is this land" side of a crossing span, and the actual water TERRAIN types (`Info.WaterTerrainTypes = {Water, River}`) for the barrier itself (adversarial NIT-A: keying the barrier on foot-IMpassability misreads cliffs/walls as water; keying on terrain type does not, and is unchanged on River Zeta whose foot-impassable line cells ARE River/Water). `lighttracked` ground A/B remains a documented follow-up.

**Metric note (adversarial optional NIT):** `CrossingMap.ThroughCrossingDistanceOverride` measures cell-space (`CVec.Length` = `ISqrt(dx²+dy²)` in cells) while `PoiMap`'s default distance is world-space (`WPos.Length/1024`). These are numerically the same quantity to within sub-cell rounding (a cell delta of (dx,dy) gives `WPos.Length = 1024·ISqrt(dx²+dy²)`), and the override only fires when the through-crossing detour is *tens* of cells longer than crow-flies, so the ≤1-cell metric discrepancy is immaterial to the DistanceFactor bucket. Left un-unified deliberately: the provider only has the POI's `CPos` (not its `CenterPosition`), so matching `PoiMap`'s WPos math exactly is not a two-line change and would not move any ranking.

## 2026-08-03 — River Zeta's ground graph is CONNECTED for the `tracked` locomotor (it fords River): the far-bank "unreachability" premise is weaker than assumed — the real central-focus lever is through-crossing DISTANCE, not binary reachability (frontline-influence Phase 0/1, `auto/frontline-p01`)

> **[promoted (durable subset) → influence-stack.md §Frontline reachability & staging — the locomotor water table (tracked fords River, lighttracked river-blocked, foot no-water/crosses-Rock) + `CrossingMapInfo.GroundLocomotor` tunable + reachability-inert-when-one-component. The River-Zeta-specific run observations are ephemeral, not banked. Verified `world.yaml`, `CrossingMap.cs:324`.]** (curation 2026-08-03).

Building CrossingMap (Phase 0) + reachability-gated POI scoring (Phase 1) and running the ONE budgeted observation autotest on real River Zeta (`test-frontline-reachability`, verdict pass) surfaced a correction to the research doc's Symptom-1 root cause:

- **The `tracked` locomotor can FORD rivers.** `world.yaml` `Locomotor@TRACKED` lists `River: 10` (nonzero ⇒ passable), and `RiverShallow: 75`; only deep `Water` (absent from `tracked`) is impassable. So with `GroundLocomotor: tracked` (the CrossingMap default), the `[exp-reach] reeval` log shows **all 12 River-Zeta oil-derrick POIs classify `Same`** (one connected ground component) — the river is not a hard barrier for tracked units, and the binary reachability penalty is correctly **INERT** on River Zeta (byte-identical there). The gate bites only on maps with genuine deep-`Water` barriers (or a `River`-terrain barrier under a River-blocked representative locomotor).
- **Representative-locomotor choice is load-bearing and army-dependent.** `lighttracked` (bradley / m109 / m270 — the bulk of the offense free pool) is River-blocked (`RiverShallow: 75` only, no `River`), `tracked`/`heavytracked` ford (`River: 10`/`5`), amphibious locomotors add `Water`. `tracked` (default) over-estimates reachability for the actual army; `GroundLocomotor: lighttracked` is the more army-faithful representative to A/B on a future budgeted run. `CrossingMapInfo.GroundLocomotor` is a tunable seam for exactly this.
