- **FIX (auto/heli-missions, @experimental-only, default-off ⇒ @stable/normal byte-identical):** new `HelicopterSquadBotModule.EvacuateWhenIdle` (+ `EvacuateIdleTicks` 500, `EvacuateHomeRadiusCells` 12, `MissionTargetRangeCells` 60). `EvaluateIdleHelicopters()` runs each `BotTick`, iterates managed attack helis in **ActorID order** (HashSet iteration is non-deterministic — must sort for lockstep), and for a genuinely `IsIdle` heli (never one mid-mission/mid-withdraw/mid-evac) evacuates it via `heli.QueueActivity(false, new RotateToEdge(heli, true, heli.GetSellValue()))` when the pure `HeliEmploymentMath.Decide` says so: (1) spent with no rearm host ⇒ evac at once; (2) armed but no BELIEVED worthwhile target (`BeliefStore.Contacts`, fog-legal) AND loitering within the home radius past the patience window ⇒ evac; else HOLD for the squad mission loop. The active mission arc (belief-routed ingress via `HeliDangerNav`, standoff strike, `HitAndRunCooldown`, RTB-on-ammo/damage, withdraw-on-AA-spike) ALREADY existed and is enabled on @experimental — the only missing piece was the exit-to-reserves.
- **The evac MUST be made uncancellable (adversarial-review FIX-1).** `Evacuate` drops the heli from the idle pool / squads / managed set / blackboard — but removing it from `managedHelicopters` is exactly what makes `FindNewHelicopters` (which filters `!managedHelicopters.Contains`) RE-ADOPT it on the next `ScanInterval` (100 ticks), and `IsReadyForMission` rejected only the literal `"Resupply"` activity — so an evacuating heli (`RotateToEdge`) was re-recruited within ~100 ticks and a squad order cancelled its evac (upkeep never stopped; it then fought without ammo). Fixed with a module-tracked `evacuating` `HashSet<Actor>`: the heli is added the moment evac is queued, and BOTH `FindNewHelicopters` and `IsReadyForMission` exclude the set; it is dropped once the heli leaves the world (`RotateToEdge` disposes it at the edge). Engine-blessed alternative signals exist (`RotateToEdge` grants an `"evacuating"` external condition `:143-145`, or `CurrentActivity is RotateToEdge`) but the module set is authoritative from the exact instant of queueing (no `OnFirstRun` grant gap) and is trivially deterministic. Empty set when the flag is off ⇒ both exclusions are no-ops ⇒ byte-identical.
- **Pre-contact evac is gated on first contact (adversarial-review FIX-2).** `EvacuateHomeRadiusCells` (12) > `ForwardStagingMaxDistanceCells` (8), so a forward-staged, target-less heli is inside the evac-eligible home radius — without a gate it would be evac'd and re-bought during the opening before any enemy is seen. A module `enemyEverObserved` latch (true once `BeliefStore.Contacts` is ever non-empty) is threaded into `HeliEmploymentMath.Decide` and gates ONLY the target-less branch; the spent-with-no-rearm branch stays unconditional.
- **Determinism:** zero RNG (existing module RNG at `:397/:468` left untouched), integer geometry (`AnyTargetWithin` squared-distance compare), ActorID-sorted iteration, `HashSet.Contains`/`RemoveWhere` (membership/predicate — order-independent), boolean `world.Actors.Any` for the rearm-host check.
- **Gating / @stable byte-identity:** the EvacuateWhenIdle fields are set ONLY on the `HelicopterSquadBotModule@experimental` block (its own per-profile trait instance — first gating pattern, influence-stack.md §Invariants). The `@stable` twin sets none of them, so they default off regardless of which block it uses. (Correction to an earlier draft of this entry: a `HelicopterSquadBotModule@stable` block DOES exist on current `main` — `ai.yaml:775`, `SkipRearmReadyCheck: true`, from the stable-0802 parity rewrite; this branch is based off `493f76a4` where `@stable` instead inherits the base block. Either way `@stable` never sets EvacuateWhenIdle, and the two shared-code exclusions above are inert with an empty `evacuating` set ⇒ `@stable` is byte-identical.)
- **Verification:** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **633/633** (622 baseline + 11 new `HeliEmploymentMathTest` pins: spent±rearm, no-target-at-home after/before first contact, in/out of window, target-holds, forward-holds, range boundary inclusive, determinism). `make.ps1 test` YAML lint: the 422 errors are ALL pre-existing (map cordons + unrelated rules-field items e.g. `EjectionSurvivalRate`/`being-captured`); the field-existence lint did NOT flag the new trait fields ⇒ they are recognized. No in-game/autotest run (hard rule + explicitly forbidden this window); the evac cadence/K:D effect is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game and needs a benchmark before trusting the tuning (500-tick window / 60-cell range / 12-cell home radius are first-guess values).

## 2026-07-31 — AdaptiveProduction combat-buy routing hazard is LIVE on @experimental NATO (not BRICS): `unitProducers[0]` is the DISABLED `@russia.fixedwing` twin, so every counter-buy vanished — fixed with the canonical `FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault` seam, gated so @stable stays byte-identical (auto/adaptive-routing)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI production (disabled-but-constructed producer twins — `TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestUnitProduction>` returns condition-DISABLED twins in construct/merge order; routing to `[0]` lets a disabled twin swallow requests so `pending` climbs while `alive` stays 0; fix routes to the first ENABLED producer via `Exts.FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault`, `AdaptiveProduction` gated default-off `RouteToEnabledProducer`). Verified `AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:93/:128/:269`, `AdaptiveRoutingMath.cs:35`, `Exts.cs:596-606`, `McvManagerBotModule.cs:117`, `HarvesterBotModule.cs:158`. Call-site line refs drifted +12 (:242/:332 → :254/:356).]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Closed the "NOTE (out of scope)" the `auto/tecn-priority-fix` entry below left open (line ~15): `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` carried the IDENTICAL `[0]`-routing hazard on the COMBAT path that a3bceb5a fixed on the capture path. Base `main` @ `a3bceb5a`. Verified LIVE before fixing, then fixed only where live.

- **VERDICT: LIVE on @experimental NATO; a no-op on @experimental BRICS; must stay frozen on @stable.** `unitProducers = PlayerActor.TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestUnitProduction>()` returns ALL `UnitBuilderBotModule` twins incl. condition-disabled ones, in trait CONSTRUCT order. Construct order preserves YAML/merge order among same-type traits (`ActorInfo.TraitsInConstructOrder` seeds `resolved` from source order; all UnitBuilder twins share one dependency profile so they never reorder relative to each other). Merge order = mod.yaml Rules load order: `ai.yaml` (`:622` russia.fixedwing, `:661` russia.heli, `:681` america.fixedwing, `:715` america.heli) THEN `ai-america.yaml` (`:7` america.normal, `:54` america.experimental) THEN `ai-russia.yaml`. So `unitProducers[0]` = **`UnitBuilderBotModule@russia.fixedwing`** on EVERY player.
  - On @experimental **NATO**: `[0]`=russia.fixedwing has `RequiresCondition: enable-ai-any && player.brics` → `player.brics` is a never-granted condition on a NATO player (`player.yaml:249-257`, granted by prerequisite/faction), so the twin is CONSTRUCTED-but-`IsTraitDisabled`. Its `BotTick` never runs (`ModularBot.cs:96` ticks only enabled modules), so the legacy `foreach(unitProducers){ RequestUnitProduction; break; }` (`AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:242`, pre-fix) dropped every counter-buy into a dead queue; `RequestedProductionCount` sums that stuck queue so `alreadyRequested>=2` (`:239`) then wedged re-issue. **Hazard confirmed live.**
  - On @experimental **BRICS**: `[0]`=russia.fixedwing is ENABLED (`enable-ai-any && player.brics` both true), so the legacy path already landed on an enabled twin (the single-name `BuildUnit(bot,name)` overload services a ground unit from the aircraft builder by resolving the actor's OWN queue). `FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault` returns that same `[0]` → **byte-identical for BRICS experimental.**
- **`AdaptiveProductionBotModule` is NOT experimental-only — it has `@stable.america`/`@stable.russia` twins (`ai.yaml:873/:882`), so the shared `BotTick` code runs for @stable too.** On @stable NATO the legacy path ALSO routes to disabled `[0]` (currently loses the buy) — but @stable is the frozen benchmark reference and MUST stay bit-identical, so the fix could NOT change routing unconditionally.
- **FIX (mirrors a3bceb5a's "route to the first accepting/enabled producer", reusing the canonical in-repo seam).** McvManager (`McvManagerBotModule.cs:117`) and Harvester (`HarvesterBotModule.cs:158`) ALREADY route via `Exts.FirstEnabledTraitOrDefault()` — AdaptiveProduction was the sole module still on the broken `[0]` shape. New default-off `AdaptiveProductionBotModuleInfo.RouteToEnabledProducer`; `SelectUnitProducer()` delegates the index choice to new pure `AdaptiveRoutingMath.SelectProducerIndex(enabled[], routeToEnabled)` — off ⇒ index 0 verbatim (frozen), on ⇒ first `IsTraitEnabled()` producer. Both call sites (`:242` counter-buy, `:332` SR-defense) rewired through it. Set `RouteToEnabledProducer: true` ONLY on the two `@experimental` AdaptiveProduction twins (`ai.yaml:463/:494`); the `@stable` twins omit it.
- **Byte-identity reasoning.** `@stable.america`/`@stable.russia` omit `RouteToEnabledProducer` (→ `false` → `SelectProducerIndex` returns 0 = the exact legacy `unitProducers[0]`, even when `[0]` is disabled — the frozen lost-buy behaviour is preserved). The SR-defense call site is additionally already `SupplyRouteDefenseEnabled`-gated (experimental-only, off on @stable), so @stable never enters it. No RNG added; `SelectProducerIndex` is a single ordered walk over a construct-order array, `IsTraitEnabled` reads a condition token — no dict/hash enumeration feeds the decision.
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **622/622** (617 baseline + 5 `AdaptiveRoutingMathTest` pins: frozen-always-0 incl. disabled-`[0]`, first-enabled-skips-disabled, `[0]`-enabled no-op, none-enabled → −1, empty/null → −1). No in-game/autotest run (hard rule) — the "counter-buys now actually build on @experimental NATO" behaviour is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game.

## 2026-07-31 — TECN priority deadlock RESOLVED: the request was landing on a condition-DISABLED UnitBuilder twin (its BotTick never runs), NOT dropped by a busy queue — pending=82/alive=0 was a routing bug, fixed by routing to the first ENABLED producer + peek-don't-pop delivery + an in-flight cap (auto/tecn-priority-fix)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI production (same disabled-twin routing paragraph — the accept/reject handshake half: `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction.RequestPriorityUnitProduction` returns `false` when `IsTraitDisabled`, and `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule` routes to the first twin that accepts; peek-don't-pop priority drain makes delivery non-lossy). Verified `UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:154-164`, `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:683-694`, `ModularBot.cs:96`.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Confirmed and fixed the supply-side deadlock the `auto/tecn-supply` merge (`f9da1860`) left behind. Base `main` @ `02667dfb`. The prior entry's "REPORT DISCREPANCY" bullet guessed the pending-gate deadlock could only happen "unless the target `unitProducers[0]` instance is perpetually paused/never-ticked (unconfirmed)" — this pass **confirms exactly that**, but the cause is condition-DISABLE, not pausing, and the task's stated "busy-queue drop" mechanism is NOT what the benchmark exhibited.

- **VERIFIED ROOT CAUSE — the priority request lands on a DISABLED UnitBuilder twin whose `BotTick` never runs.** A player carries many `UnitBuilderBotModule` instances (`ai-america.yaml:7/:54` `.normal`+`.experimental`, `ai-russia.yaml:6/:53`, plus `ai.yaml:622/:661/:681/:715` fixedwing/heli), all but one condition-disabled per game (`RequiresCondition: … && !enable-ai-experimental` vs `enable-ai-experimental`). `TraitsImplementing<IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction>()` returns ALL of them incl. disabled ones; `MaintainTecnFloor` called `priorityProducers[0].RequestPriorityUnitProduction(...)` (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:686`, pre-fix). `ModularBot.cs:96` ticks only enabled modules (`if (t.IsTraitEnabled()) t.BotTick(this)`), but the interface methods are called directly regardless of enabled state. So `[0]` (a disabled twin) accepted the request into its `priorityBuildRequests`, its `BotTick` never drained it, yet its `RequestedProductionCount` kept counting it → the coordinator's `pending` climbed forever while `alive` stayed 0 and no TECN was ever built.
- **PROOF from the raw batch (`tools/autotest/tournament-results/260731_streak_exp_vs_stable0730_tecn_s2combat/match_1_debug.log`):** `tecn-floor-request … alive=0 pending=78→79→80→81→82 priority=True floor=5`, exactly one issue per ~225 ticks, pending increasing by EXACTLY 1 per issue with **nothing ever draining it**. If the enabled UnitBuilder had received it, its 30-tick `BotTick` drain (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:123`) would `RemoveAt(0)` unconditionally (pre-fix) and pending would oscillate near 0, not grow monotonically — so the receiving instance's drain provably never ran ⇒ it was disabled. The 4/10 games that DID field TECN got them from the enabled experimental UnitBuilder's own blind lottery (`UnitsToBuild: tecn.america 500`), independent of the floor lever entirely.
- **The "busy-queue drop" (task's stated diagnosis) is a REAL but SECONDARY latent bug.** `BuildUnit(bot, name)` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs:265`) only issues on an empty queue (`FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())`) and the drain popped `RemoveAt(0)` even on failure — so even once routing is fixed, a busy Infantry queue would silently discard the popped request. Fixed alongside so delivery is actually reliable.
- **FIX (all @experimental-gated, @stable byte-identical):** (1) `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction.RequestPriorityUnitProduction` now returns `bool`; the UnitBuilder returns `false` when `IsTraitDisabled` (`UnitBuilderBotModule.cs`), and `MaintainTecnFloor` routes to the FIRST producer that accepts (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs` foreach) → the request lands on the enabled twin. (2) Priority drain is **peek-don't-pop**: `if (Count>0 && BuildUnit(head)) RemoveAt(0)` — a busy-queue cycle keeps the request at the head for the next free slot; the drain still runs before the FIFO/lottery so it PRE-EMPTS the next free slot; in-progress builds are never cancelled. (3) `CaptureSupplyMath.ShouldRequestTecn` gains an in-flight CAP (`if (pending >= floor) return false;` after the staleTicks<=0 frozen guard) → pending bounded to [0, floor]; the staleness re-issue is now a backstop that can't duplicate, since peek-don't-pop makes the in-flight request non-lossy.
- **Byte-identity reasoning.** `@stable.tecn` (`ai.yaml:801`) sets `TecnFloor:1` but omits `TecnRequestPriority` (→ ordinary path, never the priority routing change) and `TecnRequestStaleTicks` (→ `staleTicks=0`, the cap sits after the frozen-guard return, so `ShouldRequestTecn` is bit-identical). The priority-drain block only executes when `priorityBuildRequests` is non-empty, which requires a `TecnRequestPriority` caller (experimental-only) → the block is skipped for normal/rush/turtle/@stable. The ordinary FIFO drain (`:135-140`) is untouched (it ignores `BuildUnit`'s new bool return and still removes unconditionally). Zero RNG added.
- **NOTE (out of scope): `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` has the same `[0]` routing shape** (`AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:242/:332` `foreach up … break`) for its combat buys; if `[0]` is disabled its combat requests also vanish (the queue was kept busy by the enabled UnitBuilder's own lottery, not by these). Not touched — it is the combat path, not the TECN lever, and changing it would alter @experimental combat behaviour.
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **617/617** (615 baseline + 2 net new `CaptureSupplyMathTest` cap pins; 1 existing staleness test rewritten to the partial-floor case the cap still allows). No in-game/autotest run (hard rule; measurement dispatched separately) — the fix is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot S2 game.

## 2026-07-31 — TECN capture-supply guarantee: the S2-loss "TecnFloor deadlock" is really a queue-slot STARVATION, not a pending-gate deadlock; all four fixes gate off NEW default-off Info fields because @stable.tecn ALSO sets TecnFloor:1 (auto/tecn-supply)

> **[rejected: superseded by the `auto/tecn-priority-fix` entry above — this entry's "REPORT DISCREPANCY" bullet guessed the deadlock could only come from a paused/never-ticked producer; the later entry CONFIRMED exactly that, but the cause is condition-DISABLE, not pausing, and it fixed the routing. The durable routing fact is promoted via that entry → architecture.md §AI production; the per-feature byte-identity gating (`@stable.tecn` also sets `TecnFloor:1`, so each fix keys off a NEW default-off Info field reproducing the frozen path) is an application of the already-promoted shared-trait rule (architecture.md §Adding a behavioural field to a trait shared by both bot profiles).]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Implemented the four coupled capture-supply fixes on `auto/tecn-supply`, base `main` @ `e3ba32f9`, driven by `WORKSPACE/recon/260731-loss-analysis-exp-vs-stable0730.md` (Experimental fielded ZERO TECN in 6/10 S2 games → captured nothing → lost; each held oilb ≈33k dwarfs 2–8k combat). New pure math `CaptureSupplyMath` (floor scaling + re-request predicate) + `CaptureFanoutMath` (distinct-target selection), consumed by `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule`; new `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction` dormant seam on `UnitBuilderBotModule`.

- **The byte-identity gate is NOT `TecnFloor > 0` — `@stable.tecn` sets `TecnFloor: 1` too (`ai.yaml:813`), so it runs `MaintainTecnFloor` every scan.** The recon/task framing ("set only on @experimental, so @stable stays byte-identical") is right about the *value* but the master gate had to be per-feature: every new behaviour keys off a NEW Info field whose default reproduces the current code path (`TecnRequestStaleTicks=0`, `ScaleTecnFloorToPois=false`, `TecnRequestPriority=false`, `CaptureFanoutEnabled=false`), and `@stable.tecn` omits all four → its `MaintainTecnFloor` and PoiMap capture pass are bit-for-bit unchanged. `CaptureSupplyMath.ShouldRequestTecn(floor,alive,pending,tick,last,staleTicks<=0)` is provably the exact frozen `alive+pending<floor` gate (NUnit-pinned across the whole boundary), and `EffectiveFloor(scaleEnabled=false,…)` returns the static floor verbatim.
- **REPORT DISCREPANCY — the `:619` "pending≥1 suppresses re-requests forever" deadlock is NOT reproducible from a static read; the confirmed starvation is a lost queue-slot race.** `UnitBuilderBotModule.BotTick` pops `queuedBuildRequests.FirstOrDefault()` and `Remove`s it **unconditionally** every `FeedbackTime=30` ticks (`:118-123`, whether or not the build succeeded), so `RequestedProductionCount` (= the coordinator's `pending`) returns to 0 within ~30 ticks — it cannot sit at 1 for 18k ticks unless the target `unitProducers[0]` instance is perpetually paused/never-ticked (unconfirmed). The mechanism that IS mechanically confirmed (and matches the older `260720_tecn_availability_cycle2.md` recon): the single-name `BuildUnit(bot,name)` overload only builds on an **empty** Infantry queue (`FirstOrDefault(q => !q.AllQueued().Any())`), which combat production keeps busy, so the popped TECN request is **silently dropped** and the TECN never enters production. So the true failure is *combat out-competing for the queue slot*, targeted by change (c); change (a)'s tick-based re-issue additionally covers the framed pending-deadlock if it does occur via a paused producer.
- **(c) A dormant priority-request seam keeps the SHARED UnitBuilder byte-identical.** The experimental bot has no ground UnitBuilder of its own — ground units (incl. TECN) come through the shared `IBotRequestUnitProduction` FIFO on the `enable-ai-any` aircraft UnitBuilders, used by @stable too. Adding an experimental-only YAML field to those instances would leak into @stable. Instead the priority lives on the REQUESTER side: new `IBotRequestPriorityUnitProduction` + a `priorityBuildRequests` list drained before the FIFO and the blind lottery, populated ONLY when the coordinator opts in (`TecnRequestPriority`). @stable.tecn calls the ordinary path → the priority list stays empty → `UnitBuilderBotModule.BotTick` skips the new block → byte-identical. (Not persisted in the save data — transient, re-issued each scan — so the save format is also unchanged.)
- **(b) The floor scales off the SAME PoiMap capture list, counting neutral IncomeStructure/Capture POIs only.** `CountReachableNeutralMoneyPois` filters `OrderedCaptureTargets()` to `Kind==IncomeStructure && Action==Capture && owner==Neutral` (enemy income is defended, SRs aren't money), clamped `[TecnFloor, TecnFloorMax]` → ~1 capturer per free oilb, cap 5. Deterministic integer count over the sorted list.
- **(d) The 2-TECN→1-oilb clustering is CROSS-scan, not within-scan.** `QueueCaptureOrdersFromPoiMap` already fans out within one scan (each iteration removes the assigned capturer, and PoiMap targets are distinct actors). The waste is a newly-idle capturer in a LATER scan being sent to an oilb another capturer is already walking to (still Neutral, so still top of the list; the goal-guard ledger tracks the CAPTURER commitment, not the target). Fix: build the in-flight target-id set from committed capturers' `capture:<id>` objectives and exclude them (`CaptureFanoutMath.SelectDistinctTargets`, ordered walk, membership-only set reads — no hash-order leak).
- **Verification.** `make.ps1 all` clean (0 errors); NUnit **615/615** (600 baseline + 9 `CaptureSupplyMathTest` + 6 `CaptureFanoutMathTest`). No in-game run / autotest (hard rule + the fix is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot S2 game) — declared as the later benchmark check, same posture the escort/echelon/standoff levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-31 — Frontier-distance BFS needs no new timer or gate — it rides RecomputePlayer, which IS the Participates gate; standoff push-back reduces to a coordinate-agnostic step count (auto/frontier-standoff)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers (frontier-distance standoff — `DistanceToEnemyFrontier` BFS computed at the tail of `ControlField.RecomputePlayer` so it rides the Participates gate with no new timer; both consumers reduce to `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardSteps`, one Chebyshev-scaled coarse-cell hop with an explicit off-grid hard-stop; default-off `MinFrontierDistanceCells`). Verified `FrontierStandoffMath.cs:59`, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:280/:407/:1028`, `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:95`. Item B (escort reduction-only `EscortSizingMath.ResolveEscortCount`) is already covered by the promoted escort-sizing entry above.]** (curation 2026-08-02).

Implemented `DistanceToEnemyFrontier` + standoff consumers on `auto/frontier-standoff`, base `main` @ `3975b012`. The follow-on seam after the frontline contour shipped: the front is well-defined data-side (`ControlFieldMath.IsFrontlineEdge`, the enemy-region half-plane), so give standoff units a distance-to-that-region field to hold behind.

- **The BFS is auto-gated to participants by WHERE it runs, not a flag.** `ControlField.RecomputePlayer` is reached ONLY via the `InfluenceStack` participant cursor (`GatherParticipants` → `Participates`), so calling `ComputeFrontierDistance` at the tail of `RecomputePlayer` (after `ApplyAnchors`, when the score grid is final for this cycle) inherits the exact per-player gating + `UpdateInterval` cadence the score field already has — no second timer, no explicit `Participates` check, and @stable/normal never build the array because they never get a `PlayerControl` at all. Mirrors how `LastVerified` piggybacks the same loop (`ControlField.cs`).
- **Distance-to-region is order-independent, so the determinism belt-and-braces is nearly free.** Multi-source BFS seeded row-major from every `Classify==Enemy` cell (score < −GrayBand), 4-connectivity, FIFO `Queue<int>` (cell packed `(gx<<16)|gy`). Because a stored value is an edge-count shortest path, neighbour visit order can never change it — the fixed neighbour order is insurance, not correctness. Bounded work: the flood stops expanding a ring once `nd >= MaxFrontierDistanceCells` (default 64), so unreached cells (and EVERY cell when no enemy region exists) keep the cap as the "far" sentinel. `FrontierDistanceAt` returns that same sentinel for no-field/off-grid ⇒ a consumer reads "far behind the front" ⇒ zero push ⇒ byte-identical until the field is populated.
- **Both standoff consumers reduce to ONE pure decision: how many rearward steps.** The echelon anchor (WPos, `PoiOffensiveBotModule.PushEchelonBehindFrontier`) and the heli engage cell (CPos, `HelicopterStates.PushHeliBehindFrontier`) live in different coordinate systems, but the decision — walk back along the away-from-target axis in one-coarse-cell hops until frontier distance ≥ min, bounded — is coordinate-agnostic. `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardSteps(Func<int,int> frontierAtStep, minCells, maxSteps)` takes a caller-built stepper (WPos or CPos), so one NUnit-pinned function serves both; each consumer just multiplies the returned step count by its own step vector. Step budget `min+2` is enough to lift a distance-0 anchor clear yet is a hard cap — a walk-back, never a free search (task constraint). The heli's rear bearing is `squadLeader − target` (well-defined even when the engage cell == the target, which the WPos echelon's `anchor − target` is not — echelon anchors are already offset behind the screen so that case doesn't arise there).
- **Byte-identity gating differs by consumer, both default-off.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule@experimental` is a per-profile instance ⇒ a bare `MinFrontierDistanceCells > 0` gate on the experimental block suffices (the `controlField` resolution was broadened to `StrategicRepointEnabled || MinFrontierDistanceCells > 0`; the push is additionally `&& controlField != null`). The heli module gate is `standoff ? info.MinFrontierDistanceCells : 0` then `> 0` + `control.HasField(player)`, so it can't fire without the standoff loop it rides. Both default 0 in C#; enabled `4` (≈8 map cells) only in the two `@experimental` YAML blocks.
- **Item B — the escort reduction-only guarantee was live in an inline `Math.Min`, now extracted + pinned.** `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.DispatchEscort`'s tier→count mapping (None→0, Light→`Math.Min(want, LightEscortSize)`, Full→want) is the actual "the lever never RAISES an escort" guarantee but was untested. Extracted verbatim to `EscortSizingMath.ResolveEscortCount` (byte-identical rewire) with an exhaustive NUnit pin (`ResolveEscortCount(want,tier,light) ≤ want` across all tiers × sizes) — so a future mis-tune of `LightEscortSize` above `EscortSize` provably still can't enlarge an escort.
- **A rearward step scaled to one coarse cell EUCLIDEAN under-advances a diagonal — use max-norm.** The grid is indexed per-axis (CellSize map cells per coarse cell in EACH of X and Y), so to cross into an adjacent coarse cell along a 45° bearing you must advance CellSize map cells in BOTH axes — a step of one coarse-cell *Euclidean length* only moves ~0.7 coarse cells per axis, so consecutive hops re-read the same coarse grid cell and the walk clears fewer cells than the budget implies (worse in integer CELL space, where the toward-zero division collapses a diagonal to a 1-map-cell step). Fix: scale by the MAX component (Chebyshev), so the dominant axis advances exactly one coarse cell and every hop lands on a distinct coarse cell regardless of direction. Both consumers walk in WPos (sub-cell) via the shared `FrontierStandoffMath.RearwardStep`.
- **Off-grid mid-walk reads as "clear" — the boundary must be a hard stop, not a frontier value.** `FrontierDistanceAt` returns the FAR sentinel for off-grid cells (the same value a populated-but-enemy-free field gives), so a rearward hop that steps off the playable edge looks "behind the front" and the walk would accept a position past the map edge. `RearwardSteps` therefore takes an explicit `onGrid` predicate and never counts a hop that fails it — halting at the boundary with the last on-grid hop — rather than trusting the sampler to signal off-grid.
- **Verification.** Build clean (0 errors); NUnit **600/600** (7 `FrontierStandoffMathTest` incl. the diagonal-distinctness + boundary pins, 2 `ControlFieldMathTest` BFS pins, 1 `EscortSizingMathTest` reduction-only pin). No in-game run (game running on this machine; window focus protected) — the actual "artillery/helis now hold behind the front" behaviour is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game, so it is a declared later in-game/benchmark check, same posture the echelon/escort levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-31 — Escort right-sizing composes as a REDUCTION-ONLY lever with the existing contest-aware BUMP; capture escorts are never ledger-committed so freed units need no redirection system (auto/escort-sizing)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers beyond D/E/F — reduction-only escort tiering (`EscortSizingMath` over the ControlField ring + GroundDanger + SR distance), composes with the contest bump, escorts never ledger-committed. Verified `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:188/:987-994/:1018-1042`, `EscortSizingMath.cs`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Implemented believed-threat escort tiering on `auto/escort-sizing`, base `main` @ `bb4911a5`. Motivating report (spectating): the @experimental bot walks a full escort (+ the contest-aware bump to 4) to EVERY derrick and the party idles at the POI until capture completes — even oil derricks in our own rear next to our SR where there is zero threat. New `EscortSizingMath.Resolve` (`EscortSizingMath.cs`) buckets a target None/Light/Full from the ring-averaged ControlField score, DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger, and distance-from-own-SR; consumed by `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.DispatchEscort`.

- **The two escort levers must compose, and the clean way is to make the new one REDUCTION-ONLY.** `ContestAwareSupportEnabled` (shipped, ON in `ai.yaml`) already BUMPS the escort 2→4 for a contested derrick and pre-summons defenders earlier. Naively replacing the escort-count decision with a full None/Light/Full tier would fight that shipped bump. Instead the new lever only ever LOWERS `wantEscort`: it is applied inside `if (Info.EscortTierSizingEnabled && !contested)` so a target `IsContestedNeighbourhood` already flagged keeps its (possibly bumped) escort untouched, and within the not-contested branch the tier can only set 0 (None) or `Math.Min(wantEscort, LightEscortSize)` (Light). Net: contested→bump (old lever), safe→shrink (new lever), and they never disagree because the `!contested` guard partitions the targets. Consequence: within `!contested` the helper's `Full` branch is structurally unreachable from the module (contested already caught danger>threshold and any ring-enemy), so `Full` exists only as the pure-helper contract/tests — intentional, documented in the module comment.
- **Freed combat units need NO redirection system — capture escorts were never reserved past the tick.** `DispatchEscort` issues a single grouped `AttackMove` and adds the recruits only to a tick-local `alreadyRecruited` set; it does NOT commit them in the `PoiGoalGuard` ledger (unlike the TECN capturer itself, which IS committed). So an escort that reaches the POI and goes idle is immediately re-eligible for `PoiOffensiveBotModule`'s axis recruitment and for later captures (both filter on `IsIdle` + not-ledger-committed). Therefore "combat units freed from safe-POI babysitting go to the offense" is automatic the moment we simply DON'T recruit them for a None-tier target — no new tasking path, matching the brief's "simplest correct integration wins." (The physical lingering-at-the-POI is also self-correcting: the offense claims them while idle.)
- **A neutral derrick's OWN cell is NOT anchor-floored, but its enemy-owned sibling's IS — so sample the RING for both.** ControlField's `ApplyAnchors` stamps enemy anchors only for BELIEVED-ENEMY site structures; a NEUTRAL derrick (the common early capture) is neither self nor enemy, so its own grid cell reads ambient control, while an enemy-OWNED derrick's cell is floored to ≈ −800 out to `AnchorRadiusCells`. To read a single consistent "who surrounds this target" signal across both target kinds, `ResolveEscortTier` samples the 8-neighbour ring at `AnchorRadiusCells+1` (reusing the module's existing `RingDirections`), exactly the Stage-F balance-of-power geometry — one cell past the floored disc.
- **Distance-from-own-SR is already carried fog-legally by `ScoredPoi.DistanceCells`** ("from the perspective player's own SR", `PoiMap.cs:66`) — threaded through `IssueCaptureOrder`→`DispatchEscort` from the PoiMap capture path. The legacy no-PoiMap path passes `-1` (unknown), which the pure helper treats as failing the near-SR gate, so a lone technician is never sent on a guess. `PoiMap.OwnSupplyRoute(perspective)` is the public seam if a future non-PoiMap caller needs the SR actor directly.
- **Byte-identity + verification.** New flag `EscortTierSizingEnabled` default false on the engine class; only `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule@experimental.tecn` sets it (the `@stable.tecn` twin lacks the fields → false → `DispatchEscort` short-circuits to the exact pre-change `wantEscort` and never resolves a field). Threaded `distanceFromSRCells` is consumed only inside the disabled block. Zero RNG. Build clean (0 errors); NUnit **589/589** (7 new `EscortSizingMathTest` pins: full-first on danger/deep-enemy, none-boundaries, distance gate on/off, unknown-distance→light). YAML lint unchanged at **422** (all pre-existing map-cordon errors; none reference the AI module or the new keys). No behavioral autotest — the escort sizing is only observable in a full @experimental bot-vs-bot game (the single-human AUTOTEST harness can't form the capture pool), same posture the capture-contest / echelon levers shipped under.

## 2026-07-30 — Defence-in-depth echelon: the fires standoff anchors on the TARGET, so it never guarantees the screen is between artillery and the enemy — the fix anchors on the OWN SCREEN instead (auto/echelon)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI configuration (fires standoff is TARGET-relative; default-off `EchelonPositioning` holds IndirectFire behind the MainBattle screen centroid by range surplus, falls back to target-standoff when no screen exists). Verified `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:255/:1007-1016`, `EchelonMath.cs`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Implemented role-aware echelon positioning on `auto/echelon`, base `main` @ `314631d3`. Motivating report (user spectating): artillery gets sent alone to the front and dies. `FiresStandoff` (PIPELINE item 11) is already ON in `@experimental` — so why does artillery still die at the front?

- **The item-11 fires standoff is TARGET-relative, not SCREEN-relative — that is the whole gap.** `FiresStandoffMath.StandoffAnchor(target, unit, …)` (`FiresStandoffMath.cs:80`) places the piece at `maxRange − margin` from the axis TARGET, on the bearing from the target back toward the piece's CURRENT position. Nothing in that geometry references the friendly screen: if the piece is anchored off to a flank, or advances ahead of a slower `MainBattle` group, or the target is a forward POI the screen has not yet reached, the standoff ring can sit the piece level with or ahead of the line — exposed. The standoff solved "hold at weapon range and back off if the target closes"; it did NOT solve "stay behind your own shield." Those are different reference frames (enemy vs. own line).
- **The clean seam is the SAME peel-off loop.** `PoiOffensiveBotModule.OrderFiresStandoff` already separates `IndirectFire`-role pieces from the axis and gives each its own AttackMove destination; the remainder (`groupUnits`) IS the `MainBattle` screen. So the echelon needs no new tasking path — it only swaps the per-piece anchor computation: when `EchelonPositioning` is on AND a screen exists, anchor `depth` behind the screen CENTROID offset directly away from the target (`EchelonMath.EchelonAnchor`), where `depth = max(EchelonMinDepth, (ownMaxRange − screenRange) + EchelonBuffer)` and `screenRange` = the longest live weapon reach among the screen units. Artillery outranging the screen by ~15 cells thus holds ~16 behind it — it can still range the front the screen fights on (in range iff the screen is within ~its own engagement range of the target, i.e. exactly when combined arms applies), while never being forward of the shield.
- **The override falls out of the geometry for free: NO screen ⇒ no echelon.** A pure-artillery axis (or a deliberately-solo fire tasking) has no `MainBattle` remainder, so `hasScreen` is false and the piece uses the original target-relative standoff — it goes where the mission needs it. Capture-ferry / garrison / other taskings never reach this code at all (they own the unit through the `PoiGoalGuard` ledger; `OrderFiresStandoff` only processes units committed to THIS offensive axis). So "explicit tasking wins over the echelon bias" needed no priority flag — it is structural.
- **Determinism without an ActorID sort.** The screen reference is a centroid (an order-independent integer sum) and an engagement range (an order-independent max), so the per-piece anchors are byte-identical regardless of enumeration order — no sort needed (contrast paths where enumeration picks a single winner and must sort). Zero RNG; pure integer WVec math with a `long` bearing intermediate (mirrors `FiresStandoffMath`). Fog-legal: reads only own screen positions + the axis target (already fog-legal via PoiMap) — no omniscient enemy scan.
- **"High-value vehicles behind infantry on defence" is deliberately OUT of scope for this module.** That is an intra-formation ordering concern (the screen marches as one grouped AttackMove slotted by `CohesionMoveModifier`) AND keys on a defence state — but `PoiOffensiveBotModule` handles OFFENSIVE axes (`PoiAction` Attack/Pressure/Secure); `Defend` axes are owned by `PoiGarrisonBotModule`. So the defensive high-value ordering belongs to the garrison/cohesion layer, not here. The reported bug (artillery to the front) is the offensive-axis positioning concern this seam matches. Left as a documented follow-on.
- **Byte-identity + verification.** New flag `EchelonPositioning` default false; only `PoiOffensiveBotModule@experimental` sets it (the `@stable` twin lacks the fields). With it off, `hasScreen` stays false and every piece takes the exact pre-echelon target-standoff path. Build clean (0 errors); NUnit 560/560 (6 new `EchelonMathTest` pins: depth formula, anchor-behind-screen, degenerate/diagonal bearings, hold band, determinism); YAML validation unchanged at **422** on this base (proven by stashing only the ai.yaml change and re-linting: 422 → 422; the doc-stated "410" is stale — the lint baseline drifts upward as demo/test map folders with pre-existing `being-captured`/cordon content are added). No behavioral autotest: the offense positioning is only observable in a full bot-vs-bot game (the single-human scripted AUTOTEST harness can't form an @experimental axis), which is the user-gated benchmark/case harness's job — same posture item 11 / Stages E-F shipped under (NUnit-pinned geometry + declared benchmark).

## 2026-07-30 — Capture logging is already TWO instances (@stable's twin also emits `[exp-capture]`); and `ControlField.OwnerAt` reads Contested for unheld cells, so a "gray = contested" support predicate over-fires on open-ground captures (capture-contest lever, Option A)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers (contest predicate keys on Enemy-ring OR GroundDanger, NOT `OwnerAt != Own`, because `Classify` buckets score-0 as Contested). The `[exp-capture]`-two-instances / `CaptureTelemetryEnabled` telemetry detail is impl, left here. Verified `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:944-970`, `ControlField.cs:146-154`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Implementing the capture-contest lever on `auto/capture-lever`, base `main` @ `1babc81a`. Two implementation-time facts the recon design (`WORKSPACE/recon/260729-lever1-capture-contest-design.md`) did not surface:

- **The `[exp-capture]` markers are NOT one-sided by construction — both trait instances emit them.** `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule@experimental.tecn` and `@stable.tecn` are two instances of the SAME class (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs`), whose `Log.Write("debug", "[exp-capture] …")` calls are unconditional and stamp `player={player.PlayerName}`. So the @stable instance already logs ITS OWN capturers under `player=<stable-name>`. The attribution recon's "only the experimental trait logs" (§5, one-sided) is therefore imprecise: the stable side IS logged, but only about the stable player's own capturers via its own instance — there was no single SCAN emitting a cross-player snapshot. That gap is what the new `CaptureTelemetryEnabled` ownership-snapshot fills (one line per scan listing every player's held derricks), and it is gated to the @experimental instance so @stable's code path is unchanged. Consequence for byte-identity: adding `Log.Write` to this shared `.cs` is sim-inert (no RNG/orders/state), but new logic is still flag-gated (`CaptureTelemetryEnabled` default false) so the @stable *code path* is byte-identical, honouring the letter of the constraint, not just the sim-determinism of it.
- **`ControlField.OwnerAt` buckets score 0 → Contested, so "Enemy OR gray in the ring" flags nearly every open-ground derrick contested.** `ControlFieldMath.Classify(score, grayBand)` returns `Contested` whenever `|score| ≤ GrayBand` (150) — which includes score-0 unobserved/no-man's-land cells (`ControlField.cs:146-154`). A neutral derrick early game sits in gray, so a predicate treating `OwnerAt != Own` as contested fires on essentially every capture, collapsing the contested/uncontested distinction Option A exists to draw and risking the design's combat-net-swing guardrail (escorts doubled 2→4 everywhere thins the offense's idle-unit pool). The believed anti-ground danger channel does NOT have this problem (it only reads > baseline where a believed weapon envelope actually reaches). Chosen predicate therefore fires on believed-danger-over-threshold OR control-ring reads specifically `Enemy` (deep believed-enemy neighbourhood) — deliberately dropping the design's literal "or gray" to keep the larger escort / earlier defense SURGICAL to genuinely disputed derricks. The ring (radius `AnchorRadiusCells+1`) not the own cell is sampled because an owned/enemy site anchor floors its own grid cell (`ControlField.cs` `StampAnchor`; same reason Stage-F offense reads the ring).

## 2026-07-30 — The dropped SUPPLYCACHE already rearms (via SupplyProvider PUSH, no `RearmActors` needed); the real truck-parity gaps were `NoAutoTarget` + an over-eager self-expiry threshold

> **[promoted: → economy.md §What rearms what (SupplyProvider rearm is a PUSH gated on the recipient's `RearmCondition`=replenish-soldiers, NOT `RearmActors`; `RearmActors` gates only the recipient-initiated pull) + §SUPPLYCACHE (NoAutoTarget dropped ⇒ enemy-targetable; `RemoveBelowSupply:1` serves to empty; table TotalSupply 500→750). Verified `SupplyProvider.cs:151/:225/:308/:403-440/:546`, `AmmoPool.cs:340`, `misc.yaml:387/:411/:418`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Chasing a "dropped supply crate is inert — does no rearming" report on `auto/crate-rearm`, base `main` @ `5ff997e5`. The headline symptom did NOT reproduce; the actual defects were elsewhere.

- **Rearm-in-radius (a) already works — it is a PUSH, not a pull.** `SupplyProvider.Tick` (`engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/Traits/SupplyProvider.cs:151`) scans `FindActorsInCircle`, picks the greatest-need friendly `Rearmable` in range, and calls `GiveAmmo` directly. It **never consults `Rearmable.RearmActors`** — so a `supplycache` missing from a unit's `RearmActors` list (infantry list only `truk, logisticscenter`, `infantry.yaml:1243`) does NOT stop the crate rearming that unit. `RearmActors` gates only the unit-initiated PULL (`AmmoPool.ChooseResupplier`, `AmmoPool.cs:336-341`), the drive-to-a-host `Resupply` path. Autotest `test-crate-rearm` (pre-placed full crate 2 cells from a drained `e3`, same owner; the map's `supplyroute` has NO rearm trait, `structures.yaml:202-276`, so the crate is the sole rearmer) PASSES: baseline 0 → ammo climbs. The truck-drop path just `CreateActor`s that same `supplycache` seeded with the truck's supply (`DropsSupplyCache.cs:108-124`), so a normally-supplied drop rearms too. Both truck & crate are gated `RearmCondition: replenish-soldiers`, which only infantry carry (`ExternalCondition@AmmoReplenish`, `infantry.yaml:214`) — so **neither rearms vehicles by design**; that parity is already correct.
- **`NoAutoTarget` was the (c) gap.** `AutoTargetPriorityInfo` defaults `ValidTargets = {Ground, Water, Air}` and `InvalidTargets = {NoAutoTarget}` (`AutoTargetPriority.cs:21,27`); matching is `ValidTargets.Overlaps(targetTypes)` (`AutoTarget.cs:984,1090`). The crate's `TargetTypes: Ground, Structure` (`misc.yaml:386`) would match the base `AutoTargetPriority@FireAtWill` (Priority 1, `defaults.yaml:320`) via `Ground` — exactly like the truck's `Ground` — **but** the crate also carried `NoAutoTarget` (added `30f966b3`), which every priority's default `InvalidTargets` excludes. Dropping `NoAutoTarget` makes enemies auto-engage it like the truck. Autotest `test-crate-targetable` (enemy MBT 4 cells away, default `FireAtWill` stance — engine default `InitialStance`, `AutoTarget.cs:71-74`) PASSES: crate takes damage unaided.
- **`RemoveBelowSupply: 50` could vanish a fresh drop.** `SupplyProvider.Tick:166` disposes the actor the instant `currentSupply < RemoveBelowSupply`. A crate is created holding the truck's supply-on-hand, which can be <50 → it self-removes on its first tick → looks "inert." The 50 was cargo-culted from TRUK's `RestockThreshold` ("reserve supply for the drive home"), but a stationary cache has no trip to reserve for. Set `RemoveBelowSupply: 1` on SUPPLYCACHE so it serves down to empty and only despawns at 0. (Trade-off: an RPG-only residue too small for any nearby unit now lingers instead of decluttering — acceptable; it can still serve rifles.)
- **Harness gotcha:** a scenario map with playable non-spectating players crashes on load (`MapStartingLocations.AssignSpawnPoint`, "Collection must not be empty") unless it has `mpspawn` actors. One `mpspawn` per playable player, `Owner: Neutral`, co-located with each side's start.

## 2026-07-30 — SR-defense purchasing must CLASSIFY believed contacts, not read a scalar danger value: `DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger` does NOT separate armor from infantry, so classification via `BeliefStore.Contacts` actor-identity is the fog-legal seam (threat-reactive call-ins, `auto/threat-purchase`)

> **[promoted: → influence-stack.md §@experimental consumers (SR-defense purchasing classifies `BeliefStore.Contacts` by actor identity because the scalar GroundDanger discards attacker class + keys on target domain; matched-pool buy bypasses MinEnemySightings) + architecture.md §AI production pointer. Verified `AdaptiveProductionBotModule.cs:56/:135/:260-323`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Wired threat-reactive counter call-ins into `AdaptiveProductionBotModule` on `auto/threat-purchase`, base `main` @ `1babc81a`. Motivating bug: a bot rushed by 2 tanks at its Supply Route bought a Tunguska (AA!) + random infantry — purchasing never read the belief/danger fields and `MinEnemySightings=3` swallowed a 2-tank sighting. A first cut used a flat `DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger` threshold near the SR; adversarial review **refuted** it and the corrected design is the real lesson:

- **A flat `GroundDanger` threshold is NOT an armor detector — the ground channel does not distinguish attacker class, and a single rifleman saturates any "armor" threshold.** Reviewer's math from `DangerFieldLayer.cs:520-533`/`:169-170`: an E1 rifleman's core ground intensity ≈ **806** at confidence 100 (throughput 200×2×100/60 ≈ 666 × durabilityWeight 1.21). `GroundDanger` at a cell is a **max** (peak), so one rifleman near the SR trips a threshold of 100 eightfold — the "armor-weighted" reading is a fiction. Worse, `WeaponThreatensGround` (`DangerFieldLayer.cs:142-149`) keys on the TARGET domain, not the attacker: an enemy attack helicopter stamps the **ground** channel, so a scalar ground read would buy AT against an air threat AT cannot engage. **Takeaway: any "what should I build to counter this" decision needs attacker IDENTITY, which the scalar danger fields deliberately discard.**
- **The belief store (`BeliefStore.Contacts(player)`) is the correct fog-legal seam — it is the SAME source the danger field stamps from (equally fog-legal) but retains `TypeName`/`Cell`/`Confidence`.** So classify each believed contact by its own actor type — `AircraftInfo` → Air, `MobileInfo` + `Render.WithInfantryBodyInfo` → Infantry, `MobileInfo` otherwise → Armor (mirrors the module's existing `ScanEnemyComposition`) — and accumulate believed **value** per class = Σ `ValuedInfo.Cost × Confidence/100` (the `$`-value convention of `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.DefenseEnemyValueThreshold`, but derived from the type since a contact has no live actor). Buy the MATCHED pool: armor→`AntiVehicleUnits`, air→`AntiAirUnits` (Tunguska now the *correct* buy vs aircraft — nice symmetry with the original complaint), infantry→`AntiInfantryUnits`. Per-class value thresholds restore, per class, the anti-overreaction the `MinEnemySightings` gate provided (a lone scout's cost never clears the threshold).
- **No new ordered-unit config needed: sort the matched pool by ascending `ValuedInfo.Cost` to get the tactical priority for free.** Cheapest-first yields AT-infantry-before-IFV-before-MBT and AA-infantry-before-SHORAD deterministically (name-ordinal tie-break), reusing the already-curated `Anti*Units` pools. Cheapest ≈ fastest/most-affordable counter, which arrives at the SR soonest. Zero `SharedRandom`/`LocalRandom` draws on any path (additive value sums are order-independent; the class pick is a fixed evaluation order with a strict-greater tie-break) — preserves the influence-stack invariant.
- **Budget starvation is real: a lingering believed contact re-fires every cycle.** The SR read runs before the `MinEnemySightings` early-return and pre-seeds `requestsMade`; if it consumed both `MaxRequestsPerCycle` slots each cycle it would starve the scouted-composition path indefinitely (a decayed ghost persists many cycles). Fix: SR defense reserves **at most ONE** request/cycle (returns 1), so normal purchasing always keeps ≥1 slot. Also scan **all** owned SRs (capture can grant >1), taking the classified threat across them.
- **@stable byte-identity is by per-profile gating.** `AdaptiveProductionBotModule@experimental` and `@stable` are SEPARATE trait instances (distinct `RequiresCondition`), so a default-off master `SupplyRouteDefenseEnabled=false` set only on the experimental blocks suffices (the influence-stack "per-profile instance" pattern) — no `InfluenceStack.Participates` double-gate. On @stable the gate is false → `Initialize()` never looks up `BeliefStore`, `BotTick` never enters the block, `requestsMade` stays 0, RNG stream unchanged. Verified: build clean, 554/554 unit tests pass, YAML validation error count unchanged at 410 (all pre-existing map-cordon/`being-captured` content errors; zero reference AI config).

## 2026-07-30 — "Both sides Lost, no winner" regressed because the explicit win-award lived on ONE defeat path; the fix must fire on ANY player-lost (auto/defeat-attrib)

> **[promoted: → supply-route.md §Contestation-to-zero (win-award is now path-independent `AwardDecidedSurvivors` fired from `ConquestVictoryConditions.OnPlayerLost`, not only the SR-contested path; MarkFailed asymmetry; TestMode suppresses the verdict so tests read `Player.WinState`). Verified `SupplyRouteContestation.cs:466`, `ConquestVictoryConditions.cs:119`, `MissionObjectives.cs:129/:146-162/:171`, `PlayerProperties.cs:72`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Reported: spectating a 1v1 (Experimental vs Stable), one bot was defeated and BOTH sides showed `Lost` — no winner. Same class the 2v2 fix (37974788) and the earlier "everyone Lost / mission failed" fix targeted, but on a sibling path they never covered.

- **The 37974788 fix's explicit win-award lives only inside `SupplyRouteContestation.ResolveTeamElimination`, reached only from `OnDefeatBarFull` (SR contested to 0).** That path is provably correct for a clean 1v1/mutual overrun: `SupplyRouteContestation.cs:218`'s tick guard means whichever SR fills its defeat bar first fully resolves (Phase 1 marks the loser, Phase 2 awards the survivor) BEFORE the second SR ticks, so the second is guarded and one winner always emerges. **But any OTHER defeat path has no explicit award:** loss of all required units (`ConquestVictoryConditions.cs:69` → `MarkFailed`), surrender (`MissionObjectives.ForceDefeat`), or a near-simultaneous mutual defeat. Those relied on `ConquestVictoryConditions.Tick`'s next-tick inference (`ConquestVictoryConditions.cs:78-82`) — which **no-ops once the survivor is itself marked `Lost`** (`Tick` early-returns at `:56` for any decided player). Two defeats in the same tick therefore mark BOTH `Lost` before either inference runs → everyone `Lost`, and the end screen (`GameInfoStatsLogic.cs:114`) faithfully mirrors it.
- **`MarkFailed` is asymmetric and unforgiving here:** it overwrites a *Completed* objective back to `Failed` and re-fires `OnPlayerLost` (`MissionObjectives.cs:146-162`), whereas `MarkCompleted` no-ops on a non-`Incomplete` objective (`:129`). So once a survivor's inference is missed, nothing re-awards it.
- **The whole interactive attribution is disabled under `TestMode`** (`ResolveTeamElimination` `:427`, `ConquestVictoryConditions.Tick` `:63`, `MissionObjectives.CheckIfGameIsOver` `:171`). That is *why* the 37974788 fix was never verified by an autotest and how the regression slipped through — no test could observe a Won/Lost verdict.
- **Fix:** extract the survivor-award into a single path-independent `SupplyRouteContestation.AwardDecidedSurvivors(World)` (`SupplyRouteContestation.cs:466`) and call it from `ConquestVictoryConditions.OnPlayerLost` (`ConquestVictoryConditions.cs:119`) so it fires the instant ANY player is defeated, synchronously, regardless of path or `TestMode`. Idempotent (`ShouldAwardVictory` + `MarkCompleted` only touch Undefined/Incomplete state); `CheckIfGameIsOver` stays `TestMode`-guarded so autotests don't auto-end. Added `Player.WinState` Lua getter (`PlayerProperties.cs`) + regression autotest `test-decisive-win-attribution` (RED: survivor left `Undefined`, seed -1727235104 → GREEN: survivor `Won`, seed -1192943917). NUnit 554/554.

## 2026-07-30 — Pruning the bot roster to {Experimental, Stable} is NOT just an ai.yaml edit: the `@*.normal`-named baseline builders ARE Stable's production (not legacy), and the skirmish default-opponent seed string-matches the bot type in C#

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §AI configuration (roster is {experimental, stable}; `enable-ai-player` is the shared baseline so the `@normal`-named blocks are live production; `SkirmishLogic` seeds `"stable"`; `enable-ai-legacy-only` dead). Verified `ai.yaml:566`, `SkirmishLogic.cs:182`. NOTE: `UnitBuilderBotModule@*.normal` keys no longer exist as YAML (comment-only now) — deliberately NOT cited in the doc.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Removing the Normal/Rush/Turtle skirmish bots (leaving only Experimental + Stable) on `auto/bots-prune`, base `main` @ `9e4db100`. Three traps that make this bigger than deleting `ModularBot@normal`:

- **`enable-ai-player` is the SHARED baseline tier, not "Normal's config".** After the prune it is granted only to Experimental + Stable, so the modules gated on it — `BaseBuilderBotModule@normal` (in `ai.yaml`) and `UnitBuilderBotModule@america.normal` / `@russia.normal` (in the faction files `ai-america.yaml` / `ai-russia.yaml`) — are the LIVE ground production for both surviving bots. The `@normal` instance-name suffix is now a misnomer; the blocks must be KEPT. The two UnitBuilders additionally carry `&& !enable-ai-experimental`, so they resolve to **Stable-only** (Experimental uses its `@*.experimental` twin). Deleting them would strip Stable's economy.
- **The dead condition after the prune is `enable-ai-legacy-only`, granted to nobody.** Every block gated ONLY on it (`CaptureManagerBotModule@tecn` + `@captureenemystructures`, the legacy `AdaptiveProductionBotModule@america/@russia`, the legacy `SquadManagerBotModule@*.fixedwing` in `ai.yaml`, and `SquadManagerBotModule@america.normal/@russia.normal` in the faction files) is now unreachable → deleted. Byte-identity for Stable/Experimental holds because neither bot ever held `legacy-only`/`rush`/`turtle`, so removing those blocks removes nothing from their resolved trait set. `make test` reports ZERO errors in the AI/condition domain (no "enable-ai-* consumed but not granted"), which is the signature that the grant/consumer removal stayed balanced.
- **`SkirmishLogic.cs` seeds the default AI opponent by string-matching the bot TYPE, not by roster order.** `IClientJoined.ClientJoined` (`engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/ServerTraits/SkirmishLogic.cs:180`) does `availableBots.FirstOrDefault(t => t == "normal") ?? availableBots.FirstOrDefault()` — a hard-coded runtime dependency on the string `"normal"`. Once Normal is gone this silently falls through to whatever bot happens to be first. Repointed to `"stable"` (the frozen, benchmark-validated default) so a fresh skirmish still seeds a sensible opponent. `TournamentConfig.MatchupConfig` P1/P2 defaults (`"normal"`) are informational only (real assignment is each scenario's `map.yaml` `Bot:` field, read by `BotVsBotMatchWatcher`) but were repointed too for coherence. `LobbyDummyOptions.cs` `"normal"` values are Suppression/SupplyCapacity settings — NOT bot types — and must be left alone.

## 2026-07-29 — `AmmoPool.Reload()` is DEAD CODE — there is NO in-field ammo trickle from the pool itself; passive refill exists ONLY on the separate `ReloadAmmoPool` trait, and dry units must dock (tunguska AA quantify, PIPELINE may-salvage)

> **[promoted: → economy.md §What rearms what (an `AmmoPool` never self-refills in the field — not `ITick`, `Reload()` uncalled, `ReloadDelay` seeds an inert countdown; in-field trickle exists only via `ReloadAmmoPool` ITick→`GiveAmmo`; units without it must dock). Re-verified this pass against `AmmoPool.cs:111/:237/:361/:366`, zero `.Reload(` callers engine-wide, `ReloadAmmoPool.cs:46/:91`, `Rearmable.cs:52/:66`; 7 mod YAML refs.]** (curation 2026-07-29).

Found while quantifying the tunguska AA ammo-pool fix on `auto/may-salvage` (`WORKSPACE/plans/260729_tunguska_ammo_note.md`, base main @ `72594441`). Verified by grep + reading the two traits.

- **`AmmoPool` (`AmmoPool.cs:111`) is NOT `ITick`** — it implements `INotifyCreated, INotifyAttack, INotifyBecomingIdle, IResolveOrder, ISync` only. So the pool has no per-tick hook of its own and cannot self-regenerate on the battlefield.
- **`AmmoPool.Reload(Actor, int, int)` (`AmmoPool.cs:361`) has ZERO callers.** `grep '\.Reload('` → no matches; `grep 'Reload(self'` → the sole hit is `ReloadAmmoPool.cs:80` calling its *own* 4-arg `Reload(Actor, int, int, string sound)` (`ReloadAmmoPool.cs:83`), a different method on a different class. The `RemainingTicks`/`FullReloadTicks`/`FullReloadSteps` countdown machinery on `AmmoPool` is initialised in `Created` (`:225-234`) but only ever consumed by the dead `Reload()` — so it is inert.
- **Passive trickle exists only on the separate `ReloadAmmoPool` trait** (`ReloadAmmoPool.cs:46`), which IS `ITick` and calls `ammoPool.GiveAmmo(self, Count)` every `Delay` ticks (`:75-92`). A unit refills in place ONLY if it carries this trait. It appears in 7 mod YAML files, but **most units — including tunguska — do not carry it** (the only `ReloadAmmoPool` in `vehicles-russia.yaml` is a commented-out tesla block at `:1127`).
- **Without `ReloadAmmoPool`, refill is dock-only** — `GiveAmmo` is otherwise reached only through the resupply/rearm paths (`Rearmable` + `Resupply`/`AutoRearm`) at a rearm actor (tunguska: dock at `logisticscenter`). A dry unit must retreat and rearm; it does not top up in the field.
- **Complements the rejected 2026-03-23 entry (below).** That entry was rejected because it mis-located `FullReloadTicks`/`FullReloadSteps` on `ReloadAmmoPoolInfo` — they live on `AmmoPoolInfo` and are referenced. The subtler truth: they ARE referenced, but their only live-looking consumer (`AmmoPool.Reload`) is uncalled, so the fields drive nothing at runtime. "Dead code" is the *method*, not the fields.

## 2026-07-28 — A concealed forest ambush wins by DETECTION ASYMMETRY, not by cover/first-strike — and concealment-by-interposition blocks the defenders' own fire (case-01 authoring, PIPELINE 22)

> **[rejected: case-01 measurement-scenario authoring (detection-asymmetry win, concealment-by-interposition blocks own fire, defender-loss-0 bar design). The underlying engine mechanics — `DensityModifiesDamage` damage cut, `FiringLOS`/`ClearSightThreshold` — are real but already documented at their own trait files and hold independent of any scenario; the entry's load-bearing specifics (≤20% cut, DMR=4) are per-weapon tuning. AUTOTEST / case-authoring material, not curated reference.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Authoring the case-01 forest-ambush measurement scenario (`tools/autotest/scenarios/test-case01-forest-ambush/`) against `main` @ `57d88a74`. Three geometries measured before a robust defender win emerged; the failures are the insight.

- **The defender's real edge is that the attacker can't SEE it, not that cover/first-strike wins the fight.** When the concealment holds (defenders seated deep enough that the attacker's vision strength at their cell attenuates to ≤1, below `Detectable.Vision 3`), the attackers literally cannot acquire the defenders as targets → the defenders take ~0 return fire and the batch is defender-loss-**zero** across all 6 seeds (attacker loses 200–500cr, mean 350, for 0 defender cr). But a COMPACT variant that let the attackers detect the defenders at ~5c flipped it: defenders **lost** on 2 of 3 seeds (ratio 0.33 / 0.50). `DensityModifiesDamage` (≤20% damage cut) + Ambush first-strike do NOT win a symmetric close-range brawl — the whole margin is the non-detection. Corollary for the bar: a cost-weighted *ratio* is ill-posed here (defender loss = 0 → ÷0); a well-posed decisive-win bar is "def casualties ≤ X AND att casualties ≥ Y over N seeds."
- **Concealment and outgoing fire use the SAME interposed density, so a unit buried in cover can't shoot out.** A solid 6-row tree wall with the defenders *inside* it made them drain ammo for ~0 damage and lose — `FiringLOS` (weapon `ClearSightThreshold`, DMR = 4) is blocked by the very trees concealing them. The working design is a "kill-clearing": concealment WALL between attacker and defender, an open CLEARING the attacker crosses exposed, and a COVER PATCH *behind* the defenders (south) that hides them and grants the damage cut while leaving a clear northward firing lane. You cannot be both concealed-by-interposition and able to fire on the same enemy.
- **item-21's cover seating is viewer-independent (maximises density), which can bury units where their fire is blocked.** `Test.GroupMove` routes through the real `IModifyGroupOrder` pipeline so the item-21 ambush branch fires (gate isHuman+Ambush+non-Tight; every run `refined=5/5`), but it seats for max concealment regardless of threat bearing — good for hiding, not tuned for firing lanes. The scenario has to supply the geometry (cover to the rear, clearing to the front) rather than rely on item-21 to face the threat.
- **Scripted attack-move attackers stall as a crippled remnant and rarely fully resolve.** Once the lead attackers die in the clearing, the survivors halt at the wall's south face out of the defenders' effective engagement and the defenders (Ambush, sprung) stop firing — only 1/6 seeds annihilated the attackers within 90s. The defender-loss-0 signal is stable regardless, so the measurement caps at a fixed deadline. Not a bug; a scripted-attacker characteristic (a fighting bot-attacker variant is a separate case).

## 2026-07-28 — The garrison omniscient read is TWO dependencies (ordering AND sizing), and `PoiMap.SampleThreat`'s fallback is itself omniscient — so suppression must skip the sampler, not just null the layer (omniscient migration, PIPELINE 24)

Closing the declared Stage-F gap (`CaptureCoordinatorBotModule` + `PoiGarrisonBotModule` off `InfluenceMap.GetEnemyInfluence`) against `main` @ `20ddbc64`. Two traps the offense migration (Stage F) did not expose:

- **Nulling the enemy-influence layer is NOT enough to suppress the omniscient read — you must skip `SampleThreat` entirely.** `PoiMap.SampleThreat` (`PoiMap.cs:496`) takes the grid when present but FALLS BACK to `world.FindActorsInCircle(...).Count(enemy)` when the layer is null — and that circle scan is *also* omniscient (no fog check). So a naive `enemyLayer = null` suppression would silently re-enter the omniscient path through the fallback. The correct seam mirrors Stage-F offense exactly: `var enemyInfluence = suppressOmniscientThreat ? 0 : SampleThreat(...)` — force the safe/calm bucket at the call site, never call the sampler. Flagged with PITFALL comments at both new sites (`GetScoredPois`/`TryScore` and `GetDefendTargets`).
- **Garrison consumes the omniscient threat TWICE: for target ORDERING and for garrison SIZING — and they read on different scales.** `PoiGarrisonBotModule` sorts by the `DefendThreatFactor` score (ordering) AND sizes each garrison via `PoiGarrisonMath.GarrisonSize`, whose "+bonus under assault" bump keys on `enemyInfluence > ThreatMildThreshold` (InfluenceMap scale = 20). Migrating only the ordering would leave the size bump reading the (now-zero) suppressed influence and never firing. The fix threads the believed ground danger through BOTH: the reshaped `ScoredPoi.EnemyInfluence` field is repurposed to carry `DangerFieldLayer.GroundDanger` (danger-field throughput scale), and the size-bump threshold is swapped from `poiMap.Info.ThreatMildThreshold` (20) to the danger-scale `BelievedDangerHostileThreshold` (120) when the gate is on — so "assaulted" means a dense believed weapon envelope, not the small-positive Stage-C territory baseline. Offense never hit this because it does not size by threat.
- **Sign convention is carried by the multiplier values, not the bucket function.** Both new fog-legal factors (`PoiScoring.BelievedThreatFactor` for capture, `BelievedDefendFactor` for defend) are the SAME two-threshold three-bucket selector; capture lowers (safe ≥ mild ≥ hostile), defend raises (calm ≤ probed ≤ assaulted) purely by how the caller orders its multipliers. Pinned as explicit sign-convention NUnit tests (`PoiMapTest.cs`) so a reviewer can attack the direction directly. Two thresholds (not `ThreatFactor`'s single `≤0` safe test) because the danger field carries a small-positive baseline — "safe" must be baseline-tolerant, not literally zero.

**[promoted: capture + garrison omniscient→believed threat migration (item 24, merge `646515bd`) — two-read garrison dependency (ordering + size) and suppress-must-skip-`SampleThreat` → `influence-stack.md` §Known gaps (rewritten this pass). Re-verified against `CaptureCoordinatorBotModule.cs:127/:566-597`, `PoiGarrisonBotModule.cs:106/:236-393`, `PoiMap.cs:427/:495/:531`.]** (curation 2026-07-29).

## 2026-07-28 — PngSheet works: indexed AND 32-bit RGBA loose PNGs render in-game once `PngSheet` is in SpriteFormats — but indexed PNGs carry their OWN palette (no player-remap) and auto-sliced frames anchor CENTERED (original SHP offset is lost) (sprite tooling, PIPELINE 29 ph3)

Validated for PIPELINE item 29 against `main` @ `fac291e9` by enabling `PngSheet` (`mods/ww3mod/mod.yaml:327`) and rendering both pixel formats via an isolated throwaway scenario (three neutral props in a row: a stock SHP tree control, a loose indexed PNG prop, a loose RGBA PNG prop; one screenshot). Both PNG props rendered. Answers the recon's two open questions (`recon/260728-sprite-tooling.md`, Q2 RGBA fidelity + frame-offset anchoring).

- **Enabling is one additive line, safe for a shared checkout.** `SpriteFormats: …, PngSheet`. `FrameLoader` tries loaders in order and `PngSheetLoader.TryParseSprite` gates on `Png.Verify(s)` (magic bytes, ignores the filename extension — `engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/SpriteLoaders/PngSheetLoader.cs:49`), so existing SHP/TMP sprites still load via their own loaders; nothing else changes. The mod loaded and passed with it on.
- **A PNG can shadow a `.shp`/`.tem` name** — because the loader sniffs content, not extension — so a PNG-format file named `3tnk.shp` (or `t01.tem`) dropped loose renders as a PNG. No sequence edit needed for a drop-in replacement. To wire a genuinely new `.png` asset, point a sequence at it with `AddExtension: false`.
- **Indexed PNG ≠ index-only SHP for colour.** `PngSheetLoader` attaches an `EmbeddedSpritePalette` from the PNG's PLTE (`PngSheetLoader.cs:87-88`), so an indexed PNG renders with **its own baked colours** and does **not** take the actor's assigned palette or the player-colour remap (indices 80–95). The control tree and the indexed-PNG tree looked identical because the PNG's PLTE *is* temperat.pal — but a unit that needs team-colour remap will NOT recolour if delivered as an indexed PNG. For player-remap fidelity, use the SHP round-trip (`import.sh` → drop-in `.shp`), which stays pure indices and is coloured by the runtime palette. RGBA PNGs render as pure truecolour (palette-independent, no remap) — good for decoration art, wrong for team-coloured units.
- **Anchoring: auto-sliced PngSheet frames are CENTERED on the cell; the source SHP's frame offset is NOT inherited.** With no embedded metadata, `RegionsFromSlices` sets frame `Offset = float2.Zero` (`PngSheetLoader.cs:136-142`) = sprite centre on actor centre. Whole image = 1 frame unless a `FrameSize`/`FrameAmount` chunk is present (`:115-134`). So the SHP→PNG→PngSheet path **loses** the original trunk/turret anchor (a symmetric 48×48 tree happens to look right centred; an asymmetric sprite will sit wrong). To fix anchoring for a PngSheet sprite: set the sequence `Offset:` field in YAML, or embed `Offset`/`FrameSize`/`FrameAmount` chunks with `./utility.sh --png-sheet-import <png>` (reads a sibling `.yaml`). The **SHP round-trip preserves offset** through `--png`/`--shp` and is the safer path when replacing an existing sprite whose anchoring matters.

**[promoted: loose-PNG rendering behaviours — enable via `SpriteFormats` (content-sniff shadowing), indexed-PLTE no player-remap, auto-sliced frames anchor centered → `architecture.md` §Asset pipeline: loose files & sprite loading. Re-verified against `PngSheetLoader.cs:49/:87-88/:112-160`, `mod.yaml:327`.]** (curation 2026-07-29).

## 2026-07-28 — Loose-file-over-mix precedence CONFIRMED: last-mounted package wins, and `ww3mod|bits` mounts after the RA mixes, so a loose sprite shadows the mix copy (sprite tooling, PIPELINE 29 ph2)

Verified for PIPELINE item 29 (sprite pipeline foundation) against `main` @ `2cca6275`. The recon (`recon/260728-sprite-tooling.md`, Seam #6 / Open questions) flagged loose-vs-mix override as assumed-but-unproven. **Answer: the loose file wins — drop-in replacement works.**

- **The rule is "last-mounted package wins."** `FileSystem.GetFromCache` resolves a filename via `fileIndex[filename].LastOrDefault(x => x.Contains(filename))` (`engine/OpenRA.Game/FileSystem/FileSystem.cs:195`); the `fileIndex` list is built in `Packages:` mount order, and the fallback path also uses `mountedPackages.Keys.LastOrDefault` (`:246`). So a filename present in multiple packages resolves to the one mounted **latest**.
- **Mount order puts loose dirs after the mixes.** In `mods/ww3mod/mod.yaml` the RA `.mix` archives are mounted at lines 21–37 and the loose `ww3mod|bits*` dirs at lines 49–59 — later ⇒ they win.
- **Empirically proven via the shipped Utility** (same `ModData.DefaultFileSystem` the game mounts): extracted `3tnk.shp` from the mix (sha `2c512f0f…`), rebuilt a byte-different-but-valid copy through `tools/sprite/import.sh` (sha `bb052330…`), dropped it at `mods/ww3mod/bits/3tnk.shp`, and re-ran `--extract 3tnk.shp` → resolved bytes = the **loose** copy (`bb052330…`), not the mix. Removing the loose file restored the mix result. Throwaway cleaned up.
- **CAVEAT — filename must match the sprite's *resolved* name, including tileset extension.** Trees/terrain use `UseTilesetExtension: true` (`DefaultSpriteSequence.InferExtension`, `engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/Graphics/DefaultSpriteSequence.cs:363-377`), so the `t01` tree sequence resolves to `t01.tem` on TEMPERAT, `t01.sno` on SNOW, etc. A loose `t01.shp` will **not** shadow it — the drop-in for a tree must be named `t01.tem` (and one file per tileset you care about). Units referenced by a plain `.shp` sequence shadow with a loose `.shp`. `import.sh` always emits `.shp`; rename to the tileset extension for tree/terrain drop-ins.

**[promoted: last-mounted-package-wins precedence + tileset-extension resolved-name caveat → `architecture.md` §Asset pipeline: loose files & sprite loading. Re-verified against `FileSystem.cs:195/:246`, `mod.yaml:21-59`, `DefaultSpriteSequence.cs:363-380`.]** (curation 2026-07-29).

## 2026-07-28 — The control field's full-map wash is only visible if HomeLocation differs per side — stripping MapStartingLocations collapses the Voronoi seed to CPos.Zero for everyone (PIPELINE 23, territory-overlay-v2)

> **[promoted (partial): → influence-stack.md §Stage C seed (stripping `MapStartingLocations` collapses `HomeLocation` to `CPos.Zero` for both sides ⇒ whole-map Contested, Voronoi wash vanishes; set `HomeLocation` per PlayerReference in demo map.yaml). Verified `Player.cs:182`, `ControlField.cs:371`. The overlay legibility gotchas (alpha ramps, light staleness stripe, int.MaxValue clamp) are SCREENSHOT-recipe material, left here.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Building the player-facing territory overlay (`TerritoryOverlay.cs`, hold-key `T`; render-only view of Stage-C `ControlField`), the first screenshots showed only presence/anchor cells washed — the whole-map green/red Voronoi field was missing. Root cause is a demo-scenario trap, not an overlay bug, but it's a sharp edge for anyone staging the control field:

- **`ControlField.SeedVoronoi` reads `player.HomeLocation`. When a map/rules block strips `MapStartingLocations` (as the intel/territory demos do), the spawn-point assigner is null, so `Player.HomeLocation` falls back to `PlayerReference.HomeLocation` — default `CPos.Zero` — for BOTH sides** (`Player.cs:182` `assignSpawnPoints?.AssignHomeLocation(...) ?? pr.HomeLocation`). Equidistant-from-(0,0) ⇒ `SeedScore` returns 0 everywhere ⇒ the entire map seeds Contested (gray), and only presence painting + site anchors (which key off SR *structures*, not HomeLocation) produce any owned cells. Fix in the demo: set `HomeLocation: x,y` explicitly on each `PlayerReference@…` in map.yaml. In real play spawns are always assigned, so the seed works — this only bites synthetic scenarios.
- **Overlay legibility gotchas that cost two iterations (for the next visual-overlay author):** a translucent green wash over WW3MOD's dark-green temperate terrain is nearly invisible below ~alpha 90 — the whole-map ownership read needs bold alpha (owned ramp 95→180 here). And a *dark* staleness stripe (near-black) vanishes on dark terrain; a **light** stripe (near-white, ~235) reads as a hatch over BOTH the green/red wash and bare ground. Diagonal white hatching = the universal "stale/uncertain" motif and it layers over the `FilledQuadAnnotationRenderable` wash with no z-fighting (annotations composite in emission order).
- **`TicksSinceVerified` returns `int.MaxValue` for never-observed cells** (a seeded/persisted belief the viewer has literally never had eyes on) — so those cells stripe at max intensity immediately. That's the intended "you've never verified this" read, and the stripe-intensity clamp must happen *before* the multiply or int.MaxValue overflows (`TerritoryStripeMath.StripeAlpha`).

## 2026-07-28 — Path string-pulling lives in the TURN-CHAIN, not the pathfinder: the 45° zig-zag is a sequence of `IsTurn` arcs through cell BOUNDARIES; smoothing = projecting each boundary onto the sightline to the farthest visible waypoint, with per-cell reservations untouched (auto/path-string-pulling, PIPELINE 28)

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §Heavily modified systems (Move.cs row: smoothing retargets the turn-chain arc's rendered WPos onto the sightline, CPos reservation cadence untouched; gated `!LocomotorInfo.SharesCell` not the unset `AlwaysTurnInPlace`; half-cell divergence clamp; Amanatides-Woo LineOfWalkClear). Verified `PathStringPulling.cs`, `Move.cs:370-398/:653`, `world.yaml:32/:47/:64/:80`, `infantry.yaml:48`.]** (curation 2026-07-31).

Built PIPELINE item 28 (rung b of recon-260728 §Q7) against `main` @ `2cca6275`. Non-obvious points:

- **The zig-zag is a continuous `MoveFirstHalf` turn-chain, and that is the ONLY site that needs smoothing.** A straight run (all-East) has `IsTurn` false, so each cell routes center→center via `MoveSecondHalf` (`Move.cs:678`, which then `SetPosition(ToCell)` snaps to the cell centre) — already a straight line, nothing to fix. A diagonal-ish route is a chain of ≤135° turns (`IsTurn`: `delta != 0 && (delta < 384 || delta > 640)`, so 45° = delta 128 qualifies), rendered as boundary→boundary elliptical arcs (`Move.cs:654-668`) that never pass through a centre. Those boundary midpoints ARE the zig-zag. So retargeting the arc's `To` (and taking its `From` from the actor's real `CenterPosition` for continuity) straightens the whole run; the center-snap `MoveSecondHalf` path is left alone and only its `From` is fixed so a prior corner-cut doesn't snap.
- **Cell reservations / crush / adjacency are WPos-independent, so string-pulling can't touch them.** Occupancy is ActorMap influence keyed on `FromCell`/`ToCell` (`Mobile.SetLocation` add/remove, `:582-587`); crush fires from `PassAction` over `GetActorsAt(ToCell, ToSubCell)` (`Mobile.cs:610-620`) on `EnteringCell`/`FinishedMoving`, driven by `SetLocation` cadence — never by CenterPosition overlap. `PopPath`'s `AreAdjacentCells` invariant (`Move.cs:253`) checks reserved cells, not the rendered line. Because the pop/reserve cadence is one adjacent cell per segment completion and we never widened a segment to span cells for RESERVATION (only the visual `To` moves), all four hazards reduce to "keep `SetLocation`/pop cadence, only change WPos" — which the injection points do.
- **Divergence does NOT accumulate, but the sim re-anchors on the previous shadow, so a hard clamp is the honest bound.** Each segment's rendered target is the projection of the CURRENT geometric boundary onto the sightline, so |shadow − reservedBoundary| references the real boundary every step (no drift). But the next segment's sightline starts from that shadow (off-axis), so worst-case per-step perpendicular error is geometry-dependent. Rather than argue a soft bound, `PathStringPulling.ClampDivergence` caps |shadow − geomTo| at half a cell (`DefaultMaxDivergence = 512`), pinned — a corridor-shape-independent ceiling.
