So `CurrentActivity == null` is never observable from a bot module for any actor whose traits queue on idle. `Aircraft.OnBecomingIdle` queues `FlyIdle` (`Aircraft.cs:936`) ⇒ a hovering heli's `IsIdle` is false at *every* tick a bot can sample, not merely usually. Every `IsIdle` test on a helicopter is therefore unreachable code, full stop.
- Newly confirmed dead (both were assumed working): **`ForwardStaging` has never staged a single airframe** (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:623` `!h.IsIdle`) — and it is `ForwardStaging: true` on **@stable AND @experimental**, so a documented, tuned, shipped behaviour (`ForwardStagingPct: 40`) has been inert on both profiles since it landed. Likewise the attack-heli `EvacuateWhenIdle` window (`:1353`) reset `idleTicks` every tick exactly as the transport one did, so `EvacuateIdleTicks: 500` was unreachable too — the @experimental hit-and-run evac was inert for the *attack* roles as well as transports.
- `EnsureTransportsUnload` (`:1240`) is the same shape: a transport that flew home still loaded hovers on `FlyIdle`, so the `if (h.IsIdle)` re-issue of `Unload` never fired and the airframe would have stayed loaded permanently. Unreachable today only because no lift ever launched.
- **`IsReadyForMission`'s `!h.IsIdle` (`:1283`) is CORRECT and was left alone.** It is a fast path around the null-activity case; the decision is the `Resupply` type check *inside* it. A hovering heli enters the branch and falls through (FlyIdle ≠ Resupply) — the right answer by construction, not by luck. Swapping in `IsUnoccupied` would only make the fast path skip a hovering heli and change nothing. Pinning this down mattered: it is the one site where the "obvious" fix is churn.
- Predicate hoisted to `AIUtils.IsUnoccupiedAirframe` (`AIUtils.cs:45`) so the heli module and `UnitBuilderBotModule` cannot drift apart on the definition.

**Ground-infantry `IsIdle` is NOT dead code — it is *unsatisfiable in practice*, which needs a different fix.** `Mobile.OnBecomingIdle` queues nothing in the common case (layer 0 + `Locomotor.CanStayInCell` ⇒ early return, `Mobile.cs:923-934`), and WW3MOD combat infantry carry no other idle-queuing trait (`Wanders` is on `^CivInfantry` only, `infantry.yaml:337`; `AutoFollowAlly` on `^ArmedCivilian`). So a soldier standing at the SR genuinely *is* `IsIdle` — the test is reachable. It fails for the second reason: infantry pushed forward by `LayeredDefence`/`PoiOffensive` engage through `AutoTarget` and are never idle again. That exact mechanism is already documented in-repo as a confirmed incident — `LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:86-89` PITFALL ("they engage at standoff via AutoTarget → `!IsIdle` → never qualify → `carriers-candidate=0` forever") — and `MountedTransportBotModule` abandoned `IsIdle` for its passenger pool for precisely this reason (`MountedTransportBotModule.cs:531-535`). Requiring `TransportMinInfantry: 4` *simultaneously* idle soldiers on the one tick the 600-tick launcher samples was therefore never satisfied, and **not one heli lift has ever launched on any profile.**
- Right predicate is **availability-for-tasking**, and the shape is already proven by the mounted twin: (1) inside an SR **reserve zone** — new `LiftReserveZoneRadiusCells`, default 14 mirroring `MountedTransportBotModuleInfo.ReserveZoneRadiusCells`; troops past it are on the line and keep their forward orders, so a lift can never strip the frontline; (2) not committed in the shared `PoiGoalGuard` ledger; (3) not reserved by one of our own live load tasks.
- **`IsIdle` was silently doing a THIRD job, and dropping it without replacement would have regressed.** A soldier walking to board carries the `RideTransport` activity (`EnterTransport` is the ORDER name; `Passenger.ResolveOrder` queues `RideTransport`, `Passenger.cs:207`) ⇒ not idle ⇒ the old filter excluded another transport's in-flight load *by accident*. The heli module — unlike `MountedTransportBotModule.TryAssignNewTasks`, which keeps an explicit `reservedByOthers` set — had **no** such check of its own, relying entirely on that side effect. Removing `IsIdle` without adding `ReservedPassengers()` would have let a second transport pick a load already boarding for the first.
- The demand signal and the load it predicts now share ONE method (`IsLiftCandidate`), so they agree by construction rather than by parallel edit. That agreement is load-bearing: `Employ` shadows `Evacuate` in `TransportEmploymentMath.Decide`, so a demand signal the launcher cannot satisfy pins the airframe at the SR forever — the exact symptom the whole wave exists to remove.

**`goalGuard` had to be resolved unconditionally** (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:447`). It was resolved only when `CommitTransportPassengers` was on, so the ledger *read* — "is this soldier already claimed by capture/garrison/offense?" — was inert on @stable, which is precisely the profile now allowed to lift. Writes stay gated via `CommitOnOrderMath`, and `ReleaseTaskPassengers` had to be re-gated to match: with `goalGuard` always non-null, an ungated release would have cleared **another module's** claim on a soldier committed during our boarding walk.

**Ground squads cannot fight the lift, and this is worth knowing:** every `SquadManagerBotModule` instance in `ai.yaml` sets `IgnoreGroundUnits: true`, so the `GroundStates` re-order path never touches infantry in WW3MOD. This matters because `StateBase.ExcludeTacticallyCommitted` (`:155-171`) honours only `tacpos:` ledger keys — it would NOT have respected a `transport:` claim, so if ground squads did recruit infantry they would have yanked boarding passengers back into an attack-move and every lift would have timed out. Inert here; a trap for anyone who ever turns `IgnoreGroundUnits` off.

**Residual, accepted (not fixed):** `LayeredDefenceBotModule@stable` omits `CommitLineAssignments` (@experimental-only), so on @stable a soldier it has just ordered toward the line and that is still within 14 cells of the SR can be taken for a lift. Self-limiting — LayeredDefence's own reserve filter requires `actor.IsIdle` (`:351`) and a boarding soldier carries `RideTransport`, so it will not immediately re-grab and thrash — and it is the same tradeoff `MountedTransport@poi` already makes on @stable today.

## 2026-08-05 — Waking the heli-lift path (round 2, adversarial review of `557969eb`): a dormant code path carries **unexercised** bugs, and the worst one was a pre-existing line nobody ever had to think about

> **[promoted (partial): the flagged rule → architecture.md §The PoiGoalGuard commitment ledger — *a shared ledger only arbitrates between modules that BOTH WRITE it; “I read the ledger” is not a claim, and a flag gating ledger RESOLUTION silently gates participation*, verified by construction (`MountedTransportBotModule.cs:313-314` resolves `goalGuard` only under `CommitPassengers`, which the `@poi`/stable twin at `ai.yaml:949` does not set) together with the direct `IsPassengerReserved` seams that actually resolve it in both directions (`MountedTransportBotModule.cs:182` consulted at `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1603`; `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1635` consulted at `MountedTransportBotModule.cs:567`) and the `tacpos:`-only `StateBase.ExcludeTacticallyCommitted` trap (`:155-171`). **QUALIFIED on promotion:** after round 1 the heli module resolves `goalGuard` unconditionally (`:496`), so the “flag gates resolution” half now describes `MountedTransportBotModule` only — the rule is stated as *arbitration requires mutual writes; read-only participation is asymmetric*. Defect 1 → architecture.md §AI configuration (waking a dormant path makes the whole path new code; the `MaxWeight` over-order and the `LockForPickup`/`ReleaseLock` reservation semantics, `Cargo.cs:333-350`/`:352-355`). **TWO CLAIMS ARE WRONG:** (a) `halo` is NOT `MaxWeight: 8` — `aircraft-russia.yaml:254` is HIND's; HALO's `Cargo` is `:48-50`, `MaxWeight: 36`, i.e. BOTH heavy lifters are 36 (which strengthens the finding); (b) `Passenger.ResolveOrder` does not queue `RideTransport` “non-queued” — it honours `order.Queued` (`Passenger.cs:207`); the cancelling behaviour comes from the issuing module passing `queued: false`. Defect 3 (role filter) is banked with the `UnitRoleResolver` margin entry below.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Round 1 replaced the raw `IsIdle` passenger test and made lift reachable for the first time. Review found three blocking defects — **none of them in the predicate**. All three were latent in code that had simply never run. Transferable lesson: when you make a dead path live, the diff is the smallest part of the review surface; every line that path touches is effectively new code.

**1. `.Take(cargo.Info.MaxWeight)` was pre-existing, unreachable, and catastrophic once live.** `tran` is `MaxWeight: 36` (`aircraft-america.yaml:72-74`), `halo` is 8 (`aircraft-russia.yaml:252-254`), `PassengerInfo.Weight` defaults to 1 (`Passenger.cs:29`) — so one lift ordered **36** soldiers aboard for a mission that dispatches at `TransportMinInfantry` = 4 (`TransportLoadMath.Decide`). `EnterTransport` is not a polite request: `Passenger.ResolveOrder` queues `RideTransport` **non-queued** (`Passenger.cs:207`), cancelling whatever each soldier was doing. The terminal part is the reservation, not the wasted orders: `Cargo.ReserveSpace` calls `LockForPickup`, which sets `Locked` **and cancels the transport's own activity** (`Cargo.cs:333-349`), while `ReleaseLock` only fires at `reservedWeight == 0` (`:351-353`). Dispatch cancels nothing for the ~32 stragglers, and `Enter` re-approaches a moving target — so they chase the departing heli across the map while their reservations hold its pickup lock. Fixes: `TransportEmploymentMath.LoadCap` (min of a new squad-sized `TransportMaxInfantry`, the airframe capacity, floored at the launch threshold) applied at all three agreeing sites, **plus** `StandDownStragglers` issuing `Stop` to every ordered passenger still in the world on BOTH task exits. The abort exit needed it more than dispatch: with nobody aboard, *every* ordered passenger is still walking, so an aborted heli returned to the pool still Locked and could never load again. `Stop` provably unwinds the lock because `RideTransport` stays `CurrentActivity` throughout (`Enter` uses `QueueChild`), so `CancelActivity` always reaches `RideTransport.Cancel` → `Passenger.Unreserve` (`RideTransport.cs:93-98`); `IsInWorld` is exactly the "did not make it" test, since a boarded passenger is removed from the world.

**2. The commitment ledger CANNOT carry cross-module poach-safety on @stable — round 1's claim that it now does was WRONG.** Round 1 resolved `goalGuard` unconditionally so the heli module's ledger *read* went live, and concluded the two transport systems were mutually safe. They are not, in either direction: `MountedTransportBotModule` resolves its OWN `goalGuard` only under `CommitPassengers` (`:313`), which the `@poi`/stable twin (`ai.yaml:949-950`) does not set — so on @stable it neither reads nor writes the ledger, and a read-only participant secures nothing. Both modules draw from the same 14-cell bubble and the heli type test is a strict superset of `PassengerTypes` (`ai.yaml:955`), so each would yank the other's boarder. The old `IsIdle` had been covering this too. Fix is the seam that already existed for exactly this purpose: `MountedTransportBotModule.IsPassengerReserved` (`:182-188`, already consulted by `LayeredDefenceBotModule:392-394`), now consulted by the heli module, plus a symmetric `HelicopterSquadBotModule.IsPassengerReserved` consulted by `MountedTransportBotModule.TryAssignNewTasks`. **Generalisable: a shared ledger only arbitrates between modules that BOTH write it. "I read the ledger" is not a claim, and a flag that gates ledger *resolution* silently gates participation.**

**3. No role filter ⇒ the lift ferries the capture engineer to the drop zone.** The passenger test was "has `WithInfantryBody` and fits the cargo type", and `^TECN` inherits `^ArmedCivilian` (`infantry.yaml:2190`) which carries `WithInfantryBody` (`:352`) and configures `Passenger` (`:2209`); medics and MANPADS land in the same bucket. The ledger does not save this — it only covers a TECN *already* assigned a capture, and an idle one at the SR is precisely what the lift grabs first. Every peer consumer filters and this one did not: `MountedTransport` uses an allowlist that deliberately omits `tecn` (`ai.yaml:955`), `LayeredDefence` uses `IsLineEligibleByRole` (`:673-677`). Fixed with `UnitRoleResolver` role `MainBattle` (`world.yaml:351`, always present), applied identically in the demand count or it over-counts. Deliberately **fails closed** when the resolver is absent: no lift is the pre-fix status quo, whereas failing open delivers the capture specialist to the enemy.

**Endorsed on re-check, twice:** the `IsReadyForMission` restraint (`:1293`). The `!IsIdle` there is a fast path and the `Resupply` type check inside it is the decision, so it is correct for every activity a heli can carry. Left alone deliberately — the one site where the "obvious" fix is churn.

**Also fixed while in here:** the null-SR fallback removed the reserve bubble entirely at the moment the SR is lost — now `?? player.HomeLocation`, the idiom already used at `:795`. `EnsureTransportsUnload` retried `Unload` without bound on a cell `Cargo.ResolveOrder` will always drop, pinning a mission slot — now capped at 5 attempts (~500 ticks at `ScanInterval` 100), then untracked so use-or-evac can retire the airframe.

## 2026-08-05 — `UnitRoleResolver` classification margins are ONE cell on the bulk rifleman, and the lift is now a second consumer riding on them

> **[promoted: → architecture.md §The bot free pool self-heals, new role-margin paragraph. Margins re-verified exactly: max-over-armaments `MinRange` at `UnitRoleResolver.cs:288-289`, `IndirectMinRange = 4c0` `:154`, `^E3` RPG `MinRange: 2c0` (`weapons-ballistics.yaml:305`), `^AT` ATGM `MinRange: 3c0` (`weapons-missiles.yaml:8`); both consumers confirmed (`LayeredDefenceBotModule.cs:673-677` `MainBattle`-only; the lift's `IsLiftCandidate` at `HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:1596-1599`, flag `RestrictLiftToLineInfantry` default true `:81`). **THE ENTRY'S HEADLINE CLAIM IS WRONG: “nothing tests it … no YAML check flags it” is false.** `engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/Lint/CheckUnitRoleTable.cs` pins `("e3", MainBattle)` `:83` and `("at", MainBattle)` `:85` against the REAL ruleset via `RoleOf` `:132` and calls `emitError` `:144` — so a `MinRange` nudge to 4c0 fails `make test`, it does not reach the game. (The NUnit `UnitRoleResolverTest` works on hand-encoded facts and does NOT cover these two, so the entry is right about the unit tests and wrong about the lint.) Banked with that correction plus two more from verification: the `IndirectFire` test is a DISJUNCTION (`IndirectRangeFloor` 35c0 is the other clause, `:364`), and “speed is the sole `Recon` discriminator” holds for `^E3` but not for `^AT`, whose ATGM range 20c0 also fails the `ReconMaxWeaponRange` 16c0 clause.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Recorded because the heli lift joined `LayeredDefenceBotModule` as a consumer of `UnitRole.MainBattle`, so a silent reclassification now costs two systems at once instead of one, and **nothing tests it**.

`ExtractFacts` takes the MAX `MinRange` over every armament and classes the owner `IndirectFire` at or above `IndirectMinRange` = **4c0** (`UnitRoleResolver.cs:153-154`). Two lift-relevant infantry sit just under it:
- `^E3` — the **bulk rifleman** — carries an RPG secondary at `MinRange: 2c0` (`weapons-ballistics.yaml:298-305`, armament at `infantry.yaml:1212`). Margin: 2 cells.
- `^AT` carries `ATGM` at `MinRange: 3c0` (`weapons-missiles.yaml:2-8`, armament at `infantry.yaml:1675`). Margin: **1 cell**.

A weapon-balance pass nudging either to 4c0 reclassifies that unit `IndirectFire`, which drops it out of the lift passenger pool **and** out of `LayeredDefenceBotModule.IsLineEligibleByRole` (`:673-677`) — for `e3` that is the AI's main line infantry vanishing from the line pool. No unit test covers the mapping, no YAML check flags it, and the symptom is behavioural ("the bot stopped manning the line"), so it would be diagnosed far from its cause. `Wanders`-style trait presence is not the risk here; the risk is that a *weapon number* silently controls a *unit role*.

Adjacent margin, verified safe today and worth knowing: infantry stay out of the `Recon` branch only on SPEED. `^Infantry` `Mobile: Speed: 25` (`infantry.yaml:45-46`) against `ReconSpeedFloor = 110` (`UnitRoleResolver.cs:161`) is a 4x margin — comfortable — but the rifle's 14c0 range DOES satisfy Recon's `ReconMaxWeaponRange` 16c0 clause, so speed is the sole discriminator, not a belt-and-braces pair.

## 2026-08-05 — the decoration zoom cull is a spectator-era assumption WW3MOD invalidated: pips vanished at half zoom-out for every player (fixed @ auto/pips-zoom)

> **[promoted (partial): → architecture.md §Widget / chrome authoring gotchas — the upstream sub-`MinZoom` decoration cull is a SPECTATOR-era assumption WW3MOD invalidated at `60f27056` by setting `Viewport.unlockMinZoom = true` at field-initializer level (`Viewport.cs:69`, `unlockedMinZoomScale = 0.25f` `:70`); the guard is gone and now carries a do-not-reinstate PITFALL (`SelectionDecorationsBase.cs:125-128`); pips are zoom-invariant `UISpriteRenderable`s so nothing needed rescaling; `Test.SetZoom` (`TestGlobal.cs:104`) and the render-lags-a-tick screenshot rule banked with it. Eluant's refusal to marshal Lua numbers to `float` → conventions.md. **TWO CLAIMS ARE WRONG and are banked corrected.** (1) “it gates exactly four traits” — the guard fronted the entire `IDecoration` loop, so it suppressed every `WithDecorationBase`, dominated by the ~100 plain `WithDecoration` uses (damage pips at `defaults.yaml:112-133`, garrison health at `:142+`); the reason the bug READ as “pips only” is that selection boxes and health/extra bars render earlier in the same method (`:105-111`), not that the gate was narrow. (2) the player zoom range is not `[0.25×MinZoom, 4×MinZoom]` with `MinZoom` “in the middle” — `WorldViewportSizes.MaxZoomScale` is 20 (`:23`) with no ww3mod override, so `MinZoom` sits near the BOTTOM of the range. The conclusion (sub-`MinZoom` is ordinary player territory) stands.]** (curation 2026-08-06).

Reported symptom: cargo/ammo pips disappear once the player zooms out about halfway, and only come back on zooming in.

- **Single cull site, `SelectionDecorationsBase.DrawDecorations` (`SelectionDecorationsBase.cs:127` pre-fix):** `if (wr.Viewport.Zoom < wr.Viewport.MinZoom) yield break;` — the only `Viewport.Zoom`/`MinZoom` test anywhere in `engine/` or `mods/` (`git grep 'Viewport.\(Min\)\?Zoom'`). Upstream's comment calls it a *spectator* guard: sub-`MinZoom` was assumed reachable only via `ObserverShroudSelectorLogic` / the map editor, which are the only two `UnlockMinimumZoom` callers.
- **WW3MOD invalidated that assumption at `60f27056`** ("Restore WW3MOD viewport zoom"), which flipped `Viewport.unlockMinZoom` to `true` **at field-initializer level** (`Viewport.cs:69`) — i.e. for *every* viewport, not just observer/editor ones. `unlockedMinZoomScale = 0.25` means the ordinary player zoom range runs `[0.25×MinZoom, 4×MinZoom]`, so `MinZoom` sits in the middle of it. The upstream guard therefore fires across the entire bottom half of normal play. The two changes are ~5 months apart and in different assemblies, which is why the interaction went unnoticed.
- **What the gate covers, and what it does not:** it is one unconditional `yield break` in front of the whole `IDecoration` loop, so it is *not* separable per-trait. In WW3MOD it gates exactly four traits — `WithCargoPipsDecoration`, `WithAmmoPipsDecoration`, `WithGarrisonDecoration` (also pips), and `WithSpriteControlGroupDecoration` (control-group numbers). Selection boxes and health/extra bars are rendered **earlier in the same method** (`:109-115`) and were never affected — which is exactly why the bug reads as "pips only".
- **Pips are already zoom-invariant by construction, so nothing had to be rescaled.** They are `UISpriteRenderable`s, which draw at a fixed screen-pixel size (`UISpriteRenderable.Render` → `DrawSprite(sprite, palette, screenPos, scale …)`, no viewport term), and `WithCargoPipsDecoration`'s `scale` is driven by *selection* (1f selected / 0.5f not), never by zoom. Deleting the cull is sufficient for "looks the same at every zoom" — there is no zoom-aware pip rendering mode to write.
- **Test-harness gap this exposed:** there was no way to drive camera zoom from a scenario. Added `Test.SetZoom(scale)` (`TestGlobal.cs`), scale being a multiple of `MinZoom`. `Viewport.Zoom` has no public setter, so it goes through `AdjustZoom(log(target/current))` — which also gives the viewport's own clamping for free. **Eluant does not marshal Lua numbers to `float`** (`Unable to convert parameter 0 to Single`); script-binding parameters must be `double` or `int`.
- **Screenshot timing:** a capture reads the last *rendered* frame, so a zoom change and its screenshot cannot share a tick — `test-pips-zoom` puts a 15-tick delay between them, otherwise every PNG shows the previous zoom level.

## 2026-08-08 — a diagonal cell step is produced by THREE independent code paths, and only one of them is the pathfinder (fixed @ auto/tank-trap)

Reported from live play: two tank traps placed diagonally (touching only at a corner) do not stop vehicles — they drive between them, so a trap line laid on a diagonal blocks nothing.

**The passability layer never had a corner rule at all.** `Locomotor.MovementCostToEnterCell(actor, srcNode, destNode, check, ignoreActor)` (`Locomotor.cs:227`) takes `srcNode` but used it for exactly one thing — the height-discontinuity test inside `MovementCostForCell(cell, fromCell)` (`:191-205`). Everything else (`CanMoveFreelyInto`, `:239`) is a pure *destination* test, so a diagonal was costed identically whether or not the two cells flanking it were solid. `CalculateCellPathCost` (`DensePathGraph.cs:194-198`) detects the diagonal only to scale the cost by sqrt(2).

**Three producers of a diagonal step, which is why fixing the graph alone is not enough:**
- `DensePathGraph.GetConnections` (`:107-135`) → `GetPathCostToNode` (`:179`) — multi-cell paths.
- **`PathFinder.FindPathToTargetCell` (`:106-113`) — the one that is easy to miss.** For `source.Layer == target.Layer && (source - target).LengthSquared < 3` it returns `new List<CPos>(2) { target, source }` **without ever building a graph**. `LengthSquared == 2` is a diagonal, so every single-step diagonal — exactly the order a player issues when clicking just past the trap gap — skipped the graph entirely. It also skipped the pre-existing height-discontinuity rule, a latent second bug on height-enabled maps.
- `Mobile.GetAdjacentCell` (`:424-435`) — the "back up to let others pass" sidestep, which walks all 8 `CVec.Directions` under `CanEnterCell && CanStayInCell` only.

**The BOTH-shoulders/EITHER-shoulder choice is load-bearing, not a taste call.** `DensePathGraph.DirectedNeighbors` (`:75-86`) prunes each expansion to the cells "not guaranteed to be reached more cheaply through our parent". That proof assumes reachability is direction-independent, which any corner rule threatens. It survives a **both**-shoulders rule for a specific reason: for every neighbour the straight-incoming rows discard, the parent's diagonal to it has the *currently expanding cell* as one of its two shoulders, and that cell is by construction passable — so the parent's move can never be corner-denied. Under an **either**-shoulder rule the same neighbour is deniable, the pruning silently drops reachable cells, and the search becomes incomplete (paths vanish in tight terrain). Cheap rule, expensive to get backwards.

**`MovementCostToEnterCell`'s 5-arg overload is the choke point for both graph layers** — its only callers are `DensePathGraph.cs:174/:181` and `HierarchicalPathFinder.MovementAllowedBetweenCells` (`:593`), so a rule placed there reaches local A* and the abstract graph at once. The fix deliberately gates on `check != BlockedByActor.None`, which excludes the HPF call (it passes `None`, `:593-594`) and therefore leaves the abstract graph and its dirty-grid invalidation (`RebuildCostTable`, `:1007-1040`) byte-for-byte untouched. That keeps HPF *optimistic* relative to local search — already its documented contract (`:930-932`, "if it returns true there could be a path") — rather than risking a stale abstract graph, which would fail in the far worse direction (units refusing to move).

**Scoping the shoulder test to permanent blockers is what keeps traffic unaffected.** `CellBlocksCorner` (`Locomotor.cs:263`) counts a cell only if terrain-impassable or occupied exclusively by actors that cannot move; it returns false on `CellFlag.HasMovableActor`. Without that, `BlockedByActor.All` searches would let two parked vehicles wall a third out, and dense formations would gridlock.

## 2026-08-08 — ACCEPTED TRADE (user decision, 2026-08-08): subcell-capable actors keep the diagonal squeeze, and that includes walking through a building's outside corner

The diagonal-squeeze rule added the same day (see the entry above) is scoped by `LocomotorInfo.SharesCell` (`Locomotor.cs:68`), checked at the top of `Locomotor.IsDiagonalSqueeze`. **This is deliberate. Do not "fix" it.**

**The consequence, stated plainly so nobody re-reports it as a bug:** a subcell-capable actor can still walk diagonally between two cells that are both solid. That is not limited to tank traps — it includes the two corners of a building, two cells of a cliff, the corner where two walls meet, and the outside corner of any structure. In other words *a solid wall corner does not stop infantry*, and a diagonal line of any obstacle is porous to them. Full-cell occupants — every vehicle, walker, naval and landing-craft locomotor — are denied it.

**Why this criterion and not "is it infantry".** The user's rule is about occupancy, not unit class: something that occupies only part of a cell can plausibly slip past a corner, a full-cell occupant cannot. `SharesCell` is not a *proxy* for subcell capability, it is the switch itself — `Mobile.cs:321` assigns `ToSubCell = FromSubCell = info.LocomotorInfo.SharesCell ? DefaultSubCell : SubCell.FullCell`, and `Mobile.cs:198` forwards it as the actor's `IOccupySpaceInfo.SharesCell`. There is no second condition anywhere, so the scoping cannot drift out of sync with how a unit actually occupies space, and a future cell-sharing unit is exempted automatically without anyone editing a list. This tree already leans on the same property as the structural discriminator in two unrelated places, both with comments saying so: `Move.cs:366-374` (string-pulling) and `PoiOffensiveBotModule.cs:2442-2446` ("the structural infantry tell: every foot\* locomotor sets it").

**In this mod it partitions cleanly:** `SharesCell: true` appears on exactly the four `foot*` locomotors (`world.yaml:28/43/60/76`); every wheeled, tracked, walker, naval, landing-craft and immobile locomotor leaves it at the `false` default.

**Why it had to be a locomotor property rather than a per-actor test.** `DensePathGraph.DirectedNeighbors` (`:75-86`) prunes expansions on the assumption that reachability is direction-independent, and the both-shoulders rule survives that only because the flanking cell of every discarded diagonal is the cell being expanded. A search runs against exactly one locomotor, so a locomotor-level exemption keeps the rule uniform for the whole search and the argument holds unchanged — on for vehicle searches, simply absent for foot searches. A test evaluated per actor *inside* a search would not obviously preserve it. The hierarchical layer is safe for the same reason: `PathFinder` holds one `HierarchicalPathFinder` per locomotor per check class (`PathFinder.cs:42-43`, resolved `hpfs[locomotor]` at `:152-160`), so no abstract graph is shared between a foot and a vehicle locomotor.

**Naval was flagged as the live edge and is a NON-ISSUE — audited, risk nil.** The naval locomotors do not share cells, so ships would be subject to the rule; there are no ships. `rules/ingame/naval.yaml` is 999 lines with **zero** non-comment non-blank lines, and `naval-america.yaml` / `naval-russia.yaml` are empty files. All three are loaded by `mod.yaml:122-124` and define nothing. The only `naval-deep` / `lcraft` references outside the locomotor definitions themselves sit inside comments (`naval.yaml:18`, `:164`). The locomotors at `world.yaml:207-223` are vestigial Red Alert carry-over: no ship exists to be blocked and no map can place one. Do not re-open this.

**What the ground audit found instead — no map is bisected.** All 10 maps were checked by decoding terrain from `map.bin`, adding statically-placed blocking actors, and computing connected components under the old and new rules, validated against the checked-in `map.png` previews at 98–100% agreement on 8 of them. The largest component is unchanged on every map, terrain alone costs at most 11 cells anywhere, and **there is no diagonal cliff staircase in the mod** — the scenario that could have split a map does not occur.

The one material result was **`river-zeta`**: a hedge/tree-walled crop field at x=75..97, y=52..68, in the east lane between spawns (80,35) and (80,76), whose three entrances are all diagonal tree corners — 335 cells lost to vehicles. Smaller pockets: `woodland-warfare` 18 cells, `seventh-woods` ~41 across three, 1–27 cell nooks on others, `arena-tank-duel` clean.

> **[RESOLVED 2026-08-08 — every pocket in this paragraph was an artifact of the over-broad scope, and none of them exists any more. The user's ruling: the rule is for tank traps, not for trees. Once re-scoped to the `BlocksDiagonalSqueeze` tag, no tree corner is a shoulder anywhere, so the river-zeta crop field, the woodland-warfare 18, the seventh-woods 41 and every nook reopened on their own. No map was edited and none should be. This is no longer an open question — do not carry it forward. See the re-scope entry below.]**

Method limits worth knowing before trusting the numbers elsewhere: it does not model Lua/scenario-spawned actors, `CustomTerrain` bridges, or trees whose footprint changes when they die into husks.

## 2026-08-08 — review of the diagonal-squeeze rule: the HPF must stay permissive for ADMISSIBILITY, not merely for safety; and two bypasses that only a review found

Follow-up to the two entries above, from an adversarial review of `b164a312` + `5e192075`. The both-shoulders pruning argument was checked exhaustively against all nine `DirectedNeighbors` entries with **zero counterexamples**, and the either-shoulders counterfactual was confirmed hostile. Three things are worth keeping.

**The reason to leave the abstract graph permissive is stronger than "it fails in the better direction".** The original justification was that an optimistic `HierarchicalPathFinder` only costs a wasted search, whereas a stale one makes units refuse to move. True, but secondary. The real reason: the HPF feeds the **heuristic** for the local A\* search, so a permissive abstract graph **underestimates** true cost and the heuristic stays **admissible** — which is what keeps A\* correct at all. Syncing the abstract graph to the stricter local rule would raise its estimates and forfeit that guarantee. Permissive is not the cautious choice here, it is the only correct one. This is now written at the `check != BlockedByActor.None` gate (`Locomotor.cs:235-241`), which is load-bearing twice: it also keeps `HierarchicalPathFinder.MovementAllowedBetweenCells` (`:593`, which passes `actor: null`) away from `CellBlocksCorner`, where a null actor reads *every* occupied cell as solid.

**`Mobile.GetAdjacentCell` has two outputs and gating one is not enough.** The squeeze test originally gated only `availCells`; a cell rejected *solely* by the squeeze rule fell through the `else if` into `notStupidCells`, which the fallback at the bottom of the method picks from with **no re-check** — and `Move.PopPath` (`:339-345`) returns that straight out, bypassing its own `:262-264` squeeze re-check because that only ever runs against the path's own `nextCell`. `notStupidCells` bypassing `CanEnterCell` is intentional (its purpose is to shove an idle ally aside), but a squeeze is **geometry**: no amount of the occupying unit moving widens a corner. The preconditions are narrow — all eight neighbours unavailable, target cell holding an idle movable actor — but that is precisely the congested-traffic-against-a-trap-line case the sidestep exists for. Fixed by hoisting the test and gating both lists.

**`CellBlocksCorner` must agree with `CanMoveFreelyInto` about what is enterable, or the completeness premise fails.** The proof rests on "the cell being expanded is passable by construction". `CanMoveFreelyInto` honours `ignoreActor` and routes `HasTemporaryBlocker` to a slow path that may admit the mover; `CellBlocksCorner` originally honoured neither, so a cell the search *could* enter could still count as a solid shoulder — the exact incompleteness both-shoulders exists to avoid. It fails toward "no path" rather than toward a squeeze, and `ignoreActor` is usually a path endpoint rather than a mid-path expansion, so it was never going to show up in play; it is still the one place the premise was not guaranteed. Closed by excluding `HasTemporaryBlocker` and by dropping the rule outright when an `ignoreActor` is present — the same concession `PathFinder` already makes when it falls back to the blocking-agnostic HPF for an ignore-actor search (`PathFinder.cs:154-158`).

**Modelling note, recorded rather than fixed:** `walker` (a bipedal mech inheriting `^Vehicle`) is full-cell, so it is denied the corner. Defensible, but it is a *modelling judgement riding on an occupancy flag* rather than a fact derived from the unit — the one place the `SharesCell` criterion resolves a question it was not really asked.

## 2026-08-08 — [SUPERSEDED same day, see the re-scope entry below] the diagonal-squeeze rule is off for supply traffic and on for everything else, because `ignoreActor` disables it path-wide

> **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-08. The blanket `ignoreActor` bail described here was narrowed to `ignoreActor.Info.HasTraitInfo<BlocksDiagonalSqueezeInfo>()` when the rule was re-scoped, so the supply-loop asymmetry below **no longer exists** — supply trucks now respect tank traps like everything else. The five-site enumeration and the reasoning about why `ignoreActor` matters at all are still accurate and still worth reading; only the conclusion about supply traffic is dead.]**

Consequence of the `ignoreActor` concession in `Locomotor.IsDiagonalSqueeze` (see the review entry above), surfaced by a frequency audit during re-review of `8cecb9eb`. Recorded because the split is invisible from the rule itself and lands squarely on the one subsystem that already has an open bug.

> **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-08 by the re-scope: the blanket `ignoreActor` bail described below was narrowed to tagged obstacles only, so none of these sites disables the rule any more. Kept because the site census and the two miscounts corrected below are still the reference for who passes an `ignoreActor` at all.]**

**Five call sites mention a non-null `ignoreActor`, but only FOUR are live**, all through the single `Mobile.MoveTo(cell, nearEnough, ignoreActor, …)` overload (`Mobile.cs:668-671`):
- `LayMines.cs:109` — `ignoreActor: rearmTarget` (rearm host)
- `Resupply.cs:258` — `ignoreActor: host.Actor`. Note this is an **egress** leg to a rally point, not a docking approach.
- `DropsSupplyCache.cs:185` — `ignoreActor: host`
- `SupplyProvider.cs:741` — `ignoreActor: restockTarget`

**`Resupply.cs:234` is the fifth and it is INERT.** It sits inside the `if (aircraft != null)` branch, and `Aircraft.MoveTo` accepts `ignoreActor` and then never passes it on — it constructs `new Fly(...)` without it (`Aircraft.cs:1065-1069`). Any reasoning that counts it is counting a dead argument. Two miscounts to avoid repeating: this one, and describing all of them as "docking approaches" when `Resupply.cs:258` is an egress.

**Nothing passes `self`, and there are zero occurrences across all 20+ bot modules.** Every ordinary form of movement — right-click move, attack-move, assault-move, follow/approach, harvester, dock — passes null. (`Production.cs:143` looks like another but is not: it uses the four-arg destination-only `MovementCostToEnterCell` overload, which has no `srcNode` and therefore no corner test at all.)

**The WW3MOD-specific consequence is that two of the five are the supply loop.** In a mod whose entire economy is the Supply Route rather than production buildings, vehicles restocking is not an occasional docking event, it is a continuous background loop. So **supply trucks run with the corner rule disabled on every restock trip, while combat units keep it essentially always.** And because `ignoreActor` disables the rule **path-wide rather than destination-locally**, the squeeze can be taken anywhere along the route — not merely inside the host's footprint, which is the only part the `ignoreActor` was ever needed for. A supply truck can therefore still drive between two diagonally-placed tank traps twenty cells from its destination.

**Why it was still the right trade:** honouring `ignoreActor` properly would mean `CellBlocksCorner` doing an ActorMap scan per shoulder in the A\* inner loop, and disagreeing with `CanMoveFreelyInto` breaks the completeness premise in the direction of *losing* paths. Failing toward movement is correct here. But the asymmetry is real and should be known before anyone reads a supply-truck path and assumes the rule applied.

**Adjacent open bug:** there is already a logged supply-truck pathing loop at `3effb1d2` (residueUnusable ↔ map edge). This change does **not** cause it — the rule is off for exactly that traffic — but it is now another variable in the same subsystem, so anyone bisecting supply-truck movement should know both exist.

**Two smaller notes from the same pass.** (1) The `PathFinder.cs:154-158` precedent cited at the `ignoreActor` early-return is *comparable, not identical*: there `ignoreActor` selects a more permissive heuristic source and local search remains authoritative, whereas here it disables a hard passability rule inside the authoritative search. The precedent motivates the shape; it does not transfer the safety guarantee. (2) The `HasTemporaryBlocker` exclusion in `CellBlocksCorner` is unconditional, so a gate the mover genuinely cannot open also stops counting as a shoulder — the rule under-applies rather than over-applies, which is the safe direction and consistent with the same fail-toward-movement philosophy.

## 2026-08-08 — RE-SCOPE: the diagonal-squeeze rule is tank-traps-only, via a tag; and vehicles CANNOT crush trees in this mod

User ruling (2026-08-08, from live play): *"That should not be applied to trees, tank traps are special that way."* The rule as first written was scoped far too broadly and this entry supersedes the scope described in the three entries above.

**What it keyed on before.** `Locomotor.CellBlocksCorner` asked a generic passability question — `!cellCache.Passable.Overlaps(actor.Owner.PlayerMask)`, plus "off-map or unreachable terrain counts as solid". That catches **every** permanent blocker: trees, hedges, rocks, buildings, cliffs and the map edge. `^Tree` carries `Passable: PassClasses: tree` (`decoration.yaml:12-14`) and the vehicle locomotors only `Passes: field`, so every tree cell was a solid shoulder to a vehicle — which is why the map audit found tree-corner pockets closing all over the mod.

**What it keys on now.** A marker trait, `BlocksDiagonalSqueeze` (`engine/OpenRA.Mods.Common/Traits/BlocksDiagonalSqueeze.cs`), read through a new `CellFlag.HasDiagonalSqueezeBlocker` bit set in `Locomotor.UpdateCellBlocking`. `CellBlocksCorner` is now nothing but that flag test, so it stays O(1) in the A\* inner loop — no ActorMap scan per shoulder.

**In / out, stated exactly.** IN: `TANKTRAP1`, `TANKTRAP2` (`decoration.yaml`) — the only two actors carrying the tag, and the only two whose purpose is stopping vehicles. OUT: everything else in the mod, explicitly including `^Tree` and all `T*` trees, hedges, `^Rock` and `ROCK1..7`, all buildings and walls (`SBAG`/`FENC`/`BARB`), cliffs, terrain, and the map edge. The tag was deliberately **not** put on `^Rock` even though the tank traps inherit from it, because `^Rock` also covers natural rock formations.

**Why a tag and not an existing property.** There is no existing discriminator: tank traps differ from `ROCK1..7` only by `Density`, `Dimensions`, tooltip and tileset list — none of which is semantic. An actor-name allowlist was rejected (this repo has been bitten by name-keyed filters before, and it would silently miss any future obstacle). The tag is self-documenting, extends to dragon's teeth or hedgehogs by adding one YAML line, and cannot be true for something that is not meant to stop vehicles.

**Audit re-run, and it is exact rather than statistical.** Enumerating tank-trap placements across all 10 maps: **nine place none at all**, so the rule provably cannot fire on them — zero cells change on `arena-tank-duel`, `nuclear-winter`, `polar-disorder`, `seventh-woods`, `shellmap-open-field`, `siberian-pass`, `twin-rivers`, `woodland-warfare`, `x-lake`. **Every tree-corner pocket the previous audit reported is gone**, by construction rather than by measurement.

Only **`river-zeta`** has traps: 25 of them, forming **13 diagonally-adjacent pairs** where the rule now fires, in two hand-placed barrier chains — (40,39)→(48,47) as a clean staircase, and (46,32)→(55,41) as a horizontal run into a staircase into a vertical run. That is a deliberate authored defensive line that was previously porous at every single corner, i.e. **exactly the defect the user reported from live play**, on a real map, and it is now solid.

**No map is bisected, and this needs no new flood fill.** Tank traps have no `Passable` trait, so their passable mask is empty, they were impassable to every locomotor and therefore *already satisfied the old broad predicate*. Every corner the new rule denies was thus also denied by the old one: the new blocked-edge set is a strict **subset** of the old — **for base connectivity, meaning `ignoreActor == null`, which is what the audit measures and what every ordinary order uses.** The subset does NOT hold globally: narrowing the `ignoreActor` bail from "any ignored actor" to "an ignored *tagged* actor" strictly *adds* blocked edges at the four live `ignoreActor` sites, where the old blanket form switched the rule off outright. That is a deliberate tightening (it is what stops supply trucks squeezing trap lines) and it can only ever remove routes those four behaviours had, never affect the base graph the audit walks. Since the previous audit established that under the old, much broader rule no map was bisected and the largest component was unchanged everywhere, that holds a fortiori now. The trap barrier also sits at x=40..55/y=32..47, a different region from the crop field at x=75..97/y=52..68, so it never contributed to that pocket.

**The crush question — the user's belief is right for walls and wrong for trees.** Crushing in WW3MOD is **not** modelled by the engine `Crushable` trait (no actor in the mod uses it); it is modelled through `Passable`, which carries a `CrushedByRelationships` field (`Passable.cs:29`). `SBAG`, `FENC` and `BARB` set `PassClasses: sandbag/fence/barbedwire` **plus `CrushedByRelationships: Enemy`** (`structures-defenses.yaml:353-356` etc.) and the heavy vehicle locomotors list those classes in `Crushes` — so **tanks really do crush sandbag, wire fence and barbed wire, and only an *enemy's*, never a neutral or allied one.** But `^Tree` sets `PassClasses: tree` with **no `CrushedByRelationships` at all**, and `tree` appears in no vehicle locomotor's `Passes` or `Crushes` list — only the four `foot*` locomotors pass it. **So no vehicle in this mod can crush or drive through a tree or hedge; they are hard blocks to vehicles and walk-through cover for infantry.** Recorded because the user believes otherwise and the discrepancy will come up again — it also means the previous audit's "tree corners are impassable" premise was correct, and the pockets it found were real while the scope was wrong. No crush behaviour was changed.

**Verified:** build clean, `dotnet test` 1169/1169, and `test-tanktrap-diagonal` **passes** on the re-scoped build (seed -537156915) with all three lanes green — vehicle denied the trap corner, control vehicle through the orthogonal gap, and the infantry lane, running for the first time ever, confirming a rifleman still walks the same corner.

## 2026-08-08 — a movement rule that `CanEnterCell` cannot see spins `Move` forever: the escape at `Move.cs:297` asks about the wrong cell

Found by adversarial review of the tank-trap re-scope. Generalises past this branch: **any new "you may not take this step" rule whose blockage is not located in the destination cell will hang `Move` unless it is also taught to `Move.cs:297`.**

`PopPath`'s blocked-handling block (`Move.cs:262-350`) has exactly one path-clearing escape:

```csharp
if (!mobile.CanEnterCell(nextCell, ignoreActor, BlockedByActor.Immovable))
{ path = EvalPath(BlockedByActor.Immovable).Path; return null; }
```

It asks whether **`nextCell`** is blocked. A diagonal squeeze's blockage is in the two **shoulders** — `nextCell` itself is ordinary open ground — so `CanEnterCell` returns true, the escape is skipped, and everything downstream misdiagnoses the situation: `NotifyBlocker` (`:304`) pokes an empty cell and `CellIsEvacuating` (`:320`) sees nothing leaving.

**Why that becomes an infinite loop rather than a stall.** `PathSearchOrder`'s last rung is `BlockedByActor.None` (`Move.cs:30-36`) and the squeeze rule is gated off for `None` (`Locomotor.cs:240`, deliberately — it keeps the HPF heuristic admissible). So when traps genuinely seal a route, `OnFirstRun` (`:147-153`) falls through to `None` and hands back a path **through** the squeeze. Each tick then runs: wait `WaitAverage` (`:309`) → `EvalPath(BlockedByActor.All)` (`:325`) returns empty, because with `All` the rule *does* apply → `return null` (`:349`) → `Tick` returns false (`:183-184`) with `path` still intact → repeat. That is a full `BlockedByActor.All` A\* every ~41 ticks, forever; the activity never completes and only a new order breaks it. Reachable from an ordinary player order — a vehicle on the open corner cell told to move one cell diagonally past a trap line, so no trap cell appears earlier in the path to trigger the escape by other means.

**Fix:** treat a squeeze as immovable at `:297` (`squeeze || !mobile.CanEnterCell(...)`). Termination is then two ticks: `EvalPath(Immovable)` returns empty, `Tick` sets `destination = mobile.ToCell` and returns false (`:174-176`), and the next tick hits `destination == mobile.ToCell` and returns true (`:163-164`).

**The trap for the next person:** the branch already stated the correct principle one file away — `Mobile.GetAdjacentCell` carries "a squeeze is geometry, and no amount of the occupant moving widens a corner" — and still missed applying it here, because `Move.cs`'s blocked-handling reads as being about *actors* and a geometric rule does not look like it belongs. It does. The tell for this whole class: a step-legality rule that consults any cell other than the destination.

**Why the autotest did not catch it:** the scenario's `Squeezer` starts far enough away that its `BlockedByActor.None` path runs *through* a trap cell, which trips the `:297` escape by the ordinary `CanEnterCell` route and terminates cleanly. The lane passes for a reason unrelated to the loop — a good reminder that a green behavioural test pins the outcome it asserts, not the mechanism you believe produces it.

## 2026-08-09 — "a host is named" is not "a host exists": the aircraft readiness gates were asking the rules a question only the world can answer

Found while verifying `DOCS/bots/06-inherited-misfits.md` rank 2. The audit's headline held, but its shorthand was wrong twice, and both corrections matter more than the headline.

**Correction 1 — the mechanism is not a disabled trait.** `HPAD` and `AFLD` carry `Buildable.Prerequisites: ~disabled` (`structures.yaml:432`, `:500`), an unsatisfiable *build* prerequisite — **nothing anywhere in the repo provides `disabled`** (verified by grep over `mods/`). The actor definitions are complete and every trait on them is live. They simply can never be instantiated: unbuildable, and pre-placed on **zero** of the ten shipped maps. `LOGISTICSCENTER` is the same (`structures.yaml:367`), which also makes "until the player builds more LCs" in `economy.md` wrong — LCs enter a match only as the Neutral pre-placed capturable ones.

**Correction 2 — "the mod has no rearm" is false, and that is the useful part.** `logisticscenter` has both `RepairsUnits` (`structures.yaml:377`) and `SupplyProvider` (`:387`) and **is pre-placed as a Neutral capturable on three maps** (`polar-disorder-ww3/map.yaml:863,866`; `river-zeta-ww3:3994`, `:9088`; `woodland-warfare-ww3:3695,3698`). Every infantry template names it (`RearmActors: truk, logisticscenter`, 14 sites in `infantry.yaml`) and so do the ground vehicles. **Aircraft alone name only the two impossible actors** — so the aircraft gap is a one-line omission in a working system, not an absent system.

**A latent path nothing drives.** `ReloadAmmoPool@1/@2` on every airframe is gated `unit.docked && !airborne` (`aircraft-america.yaml:175`, `:205`, and 14 twins). A *captured* LC grants `unit.docked` within 2c0 via `ProximityExternalCondition@UNITDOCKED` (`structures.yaml:400-404`, `ValidRelationships: Ally`), and aircraft declare the receiving `ExternalCondition@UNITDOCKED` (`aircraft.yaml:73-74`). So an aircraft landed beside a captured LC **would** trickle-refill — but nothing, human UI or bot, ever sends one there, and `Rearmable.RearmActors` does not name `logisticscenter`, so the drive-to-host pull cannot select it either.

**The failure mode, stated generally.** `ReturnToBase.ChooseResupplier` filters `ActorsHavingTrait<Reservable>()` by `RearmActors` membership; with an always-empty result `Tick` falls to `QueueChild(new FlyIdle(...)); return true` (**`ReturnToBase.cs:107-108` on `main`**; `:127-128` on this branch). Crucially `Aircraft.ResolveOrder` accepted the order on `RearmActors.Count != 0` alone — a statement about the **rules** standing in for a statement about the **world** — and `QueueActivity(order.Queued: false, ...)` *cancels the current activity*. So a bot `SendDamagedUnitsHome` cancelled a live attack run and replaced it with a hover. That is a second, independent "air units stop being useful" symptom that nobody had attributed to this root cause.

**The dead band is the actual bench.** `AIHelicopterRole.ReEngageHealthPercent` is documented as "HP the helicopter must reach **after repair**" (`AIHelicopterRole.cs:30`) — a recovery bar. With no repair host it can be crossed exactly once, downward, so the interval `[FleeHealthPercent, ReEngageHealthPercent)` is airframes too healthy for any flee path to retire and too damaged for any launch path to pick: **35–75 on every attack heli, 60–90 on both transports** (`aircraft-america.yaml:8-9,112-113,275-276`; `aircraft-russia.yaml:8-9,104-105,276-277`). A 500/800 HP Apache and a 500/600 HP Chinook both sit inside it.

**The fix must not key COMMITMENT off host existence, and this is the part that is easy to get backwards.** Making the bar `hasRepairHost ? recoveryBar : fleeBar` reads naturally and is a step function pointing the wrong way: capturing a logistics center would snap the Apache bar 35 → 75, so **taking ground would make the helicopters more timid**. A host must only ever ADD an option. So the commit floor is the flee bar unconditionally, and the recovery bar governs one thing — whether a damaged airframe is *routed to repair first* (`SendDamagedUnitsHome`). Generalises to any predicate of the form "we have a facility, therefore raise our standards": check the sign before you wire it.

**Separately, `HasRepairHost` cannot flip for an airframe today even in principle.** `Repairable.FindRepairBuilding` filters on `Info.RepairActors`, and aircraft list only `afld`/`hpad` (`aircraft.yaml:80`, `:163`) — `logisticscenter` is absent. So capturing an LC changes nothing for aircraft repair; the path is inert rather than merely untested. Wiring the LC into aircraft `RearmActors`/`RepairActors` is the open decision, and it must be taken knowing it flips both host predicates at once.

**Where the profile-scope claim was wrong — and it was the CODE, not the audit.** `SkipRearmReadyCheck: true` shipped on **both** twins (`ai.yaml:1418`, `:1445`) while its own `[Desc]` (`HelicopterSquadBotModule.cs:186`) and three `ai.yaml` comments all said "experimental only". `06-inherited-misfits.md:266-267` had already read past that and said so — *"routed around by `SkipRearmReadyCheck` on both profiles; and the health gates became unsatisfiable"* — which is exactly right. An earlier revision of this entry blamed the audit for the error; that was itself false and is corrected here. The lesson stands but points elsewhere: **a `[Desc]` claiming a default is not evidence of what shipped — read the YAML.** What actually benched airframes on both profiles was the **health** half, which no flag bypassed anywhere.

**Not every flag near this is a bypass of it.** `UnitBuilderBotModule.SkipRearmBuildingCheck` (`:43`, `HasAdequateAirUnitReloadBuildings` `:1046-1049`) caps aircraft purchases at one per pad. That cap is meaningless under a reinforcement model *regardless* of whether a rearm host exists, so it has an independent reason to exist and is not deletable by fixing the host question. `EvacuateWhenIdle`/`EvacuateIdleTransports` likewise — banking salvage and stopping upkeep is a real doctrine, not a workaround; only their `[Desc]` justifications needed correcting.

**The general tell, sharper than the audit's.** Grepping `~disabled` and then grepping who still names those actors finds this instance. The reusable version is: **any readiness predicate that reads a config list (`RearmActors`, `RepairActors`, `ProductionQueue` names) and concludes something will happen is asserting a world fact from a rules fact.** It degrades silently, because a config list is never empty in a way that looks wrong. Ask the world.

**DEFERRED, deliberately not built on `auto/aircraft-rearm` — the per-unit peel-out is now a complete no-op, and the fix for it is a behavioural change nobody can test today.** `SendDamagedUnitsHome` is the only per-unit health action in the heli FSM, and with no repair host its `ReturnToBase` orders are refused outright (`Aircraft.CanReturnToBase`). So nothing ever pulls an individual airframe out of a fight: only the squad *average* acts, via `CommitHealthThreshold`. Combined with the commit floor now being the flee bar, a squad sitting at ~36% average passes the idle gate and relaunches on the ~75-tick withdraw cycle indefinitely, bounded only by `IsTargetTooHot`.

**The fix that suggests itself, recorded so it is not re-derived from scratch:** drop a unit from `Squad.Units` when it falls below its own `FleeHealthPercent`, rather than ordering it home. Squad membership is nothing but that list (`Squad.cs:23`, `IsValid` is `Units.Count > 0` at `:94`), so a membership change needs no order, no activity and no host — it survives the no-host case that defeats every order-based retreat, and the dropped airframe falls back to the module's idle pool where `IsReadyForMission` will decline to re-recruit it while it stays under the bar.

**Why it is NOT in this branch, which is the part worth keeping.** The risk it addresses was *inferred from reading the code*, not *observed in a match*. The branch already carries one un-run behavioural change (the readiness bars themselves). Adding a second untested behavioural change to fix an unobserved risk is how a clean branch turns into an unattributable regression: when the benchmark moves, there is no way to say which of the two moved it. **Queue it behind a live look at the first change.** The general rule: one un-run behavioural change per branch is a measurement; two is a guess.

**Related, and it lands at the same instant.** Neither host predicate can be true for an airframe today — `Rearmable.RearmActors` and `Repairable.RepairActors` list only `hpad`/`afld`. So `RepairRoutingBar`'s hosted arm and `MemberStillShoots`/`AmmoReadyToLaunch`'s hosted arms are pinned by unit test and by nothing else. Wiring `logisticscenter` into either list flips **both** predicates simultaneously, so all three hosted arms go live together, first-run-in-anger, on the three maps that have an LC. Whoever takes that decision should expect to be the first person ever to execute that code.
